- **Every leaf is in the length band** (70 to 85% of its Book leaf), with every beat kept; most land just under 85% because fixed matter cannot shrink.
- **The flags are all fixed matter or names.** The checker's scan flags *upon* in Jack's cry (I.2), *, for* in the oils line *Cedar, for endurance.* (I.4), and *-eth* in *Daveth* (IV.5). II.3's one semicolon is the guild's oldest saying, *Birth united them; death could not separate them.*, and its paragraph count stays near the Book's because the MARKED form gives each speaker his own paragraph.
- **The Book of Knowings** carries v2.0's Plain table word for word, and the checker reads its cells as prose. Without the table its overlap is 12.1% (11.2% in prose), its mean sentence 11.8 and its grade 3.4 (g11's own count).
- **The Invocation** is told plainly from v3's own Invocation (*Sit if there's room, or stand. The guns are going cold, and the mortar won't set before dark.*), no longer from v1.3's wording.
- **The Plain keeps its own modern forms of the refrains** (§1.2): *Someone has to keep a light.* · *"Hold it, and it will hold you."* · *"It's only a rhyme."*

### 1.11 The tabs: one switch, three texts, the whole document

**One switch** (K1). The reader has three tabs, *The Book*, *Plain Words* and *Original*, at the head of every tale and of the foreword, the Invocation, the Epilogue and the Book of Knowings. A choice made at any one of them switches every leaf of the Book at once, on the story page and in the game. It is remembered with the settings, not the save. The first choice is *The Book*.

**Keeping the reader's place.** Plain Words now has its own paragraphs (about a fifth fewer than the Book's), so the tabs no longer line up paragraph for paragraph, and that is on purpose. They line up by anchors that both texts carry, in the same order and the same number:

| Anchor | In each text |
|---|---|
| the leaf headings (`##`, `###`) | 40 and 28 |
| the bold part heads (V.5's counts, V.7's parts, VI.1's havens, the Last Note) | the same set |
| `<!-- HALYNA -->` … `<!-- HALYNA END -->` | 19 pairs |
| `<!-- READING -->` | 8 |
| `<!-- RING -->` | 16 |
| `::: native` and `::: note` | 15 and 2 |
| `<!-- W … -->` | 8 |
| `MARKED`, `TOKEN`, `MIXED`, `PROLOGUE EXCERPT` (begin and end), `FACING LEAF` | 1, 1, 1, 2, 1 |
| `RIPENS` | 2 |
| every song, and every hearth's answer | the same set |

Recommended: on a switch, the page lands on the last anchor above the line in view, then the same fraction of the way to the next anchor. *Original* lines up by the same anchors once it is set down.

**The Original** (K2). It must carry the leaf in its own hands in every section: the stone leaves, headnotes, foreword, Invocation and Epilogue in Orrowen, in Seren's leaf-hand; the wood leaves in the grain, each round whole and then ring by ring. *Being set down anew in its own hands.* is not a state to ship. It must be set down from v3, because v1.3's *The Legends, in Their Own Hands* and anything set from v2.0 follow text that is gone. That is the Tongues team's pass and is owed. Until it lands, every native drawing the Book carries (the Title in both its cuttings, the four pair-marks, the rings facing I.1, the Wave, the IV.2 marks, the Stone, the three stones) shows under *Original* at its place, and nothing is drawn that has not been authored (§5.6, *truth or nothing*).

**The copy Jack shares** (K3) is the whole story page on the web: every leaf in order, all three tabs with the one switch, every native drawing in the course-hand, the Hal and the grain, the facing leaves, and every song. It is private to the people Jack chooses. It is never a trimmed reader, and it carries none of these Notes unless Jack chooses. Whether it goes out before the Original is set down is his call (§7.2 call 2).

### 1.12 How v3 was made, and what the editors made one

**The order of the work.**
1. **Research,** in `wf11/research/`: R1 Tolkien (the telescope; the grand style held back; weak men shown, not judged); R2 Homer, read with Auerbach (the scar; the deed lit and the heart left dark); R3 economy and the archaic register (the sagas, the King James Bible and Malory; the three dials and the HEAVY tables); R4 how a story builds; R5 the measured diagnosis of v1.3 and v2.0 (`style_metrics.py`); and R6a and R6b, the inventory of every tale's beats, lines, songs, seeds and payoffs, what Jack's notes change, and what invented lore muddies the story.
2. **The craft guide** (`craft.md`): twenty-two rules, each with its source and an example, the build of every tale, the frame, Seren's craft, the wood-leaf form, the never-list.
3. **The calibration** of three registers on I.1, IV.3 and IV.4, judged by four readers (A 3, B 1, C 0), and **the voice guide** written from the verdict (`voice_v3.md`).
4. **The models:** the three winning leaves, revised (`samples/I1.md`, `IV3.md`, `IV4.md`), and a Plain model (`samples/I1_plain.md`). Their own Plain twins were set aside, for they did not meet voice §11.
5. **Thirteen hands drafted the Book,** eleven for stone and two for wood. Each draft was read by two critics, one for fidelity to the elements and one with Jack's eye, and each hand revised once.
6. **The editor assembled** `book_v3_draft.md` and harmonised it. **Two critics read the whole Book,** one for continuity (C1–C15) and one for its arc (A1–A20, and twenty-nine smaller points), and **the final pass** made `book_v3.md` with one script of 113 exact-match replacements, so every change can be traced.
7. **Plain Words was retold** to voice §11 by thirteen hands from the beat lists and assembled in Book order (`plain_v3.md`).
8. **These notes** were made from the writers' notes and the editors' logs (`notes_inbox_v3.md`).

**The thirteen hands,** and the choices of note in their notes:

| Hand | Leaves | Choices of note |
|---|---|---|
| g01 | the title page and Contents, Of This Book, the Invocation, Book One's heading, I.1 | the foreword's spine (will the tales outlast their tellers?) kept in three places; the theme said once, bettered from *Forgetting chose our fathers*, which the Book's object-first order would misread; *I write alone*; the granite wish; the Epilogue's Argument *What the grey sent back.*; Book One's Argument back to v1.3's *How we came to stand.*; the calibrated I.1 touched only to mend the pennant (*Crimson is the pennant on this Keep, and on every keep we raise.*) |
| g02 | I.2, I.3 | the soldiers' shout restored; Halyna seated under the broken gate (*Her line had laid that gate. His had cut its stone out of the mountain.*); the silence built by three; *Up went their chisels as one.*; *I sang to the comma, and then I sang the rest.*; the masons on the breach as the stake (*two and two*); Hale carries the marks as words; *The Captain standeth still.* kept as Hale's present at the run; *thee* to Seren |
| g03 | I.4, I.5 | the True Men raise the new stone *mark down*; the law of the staff read in halves; the Captain at his own Rite (*Late came the Captain in off the wall, mortar to the wrist*); Seren's descent *turning by turning*; Jack's dust line verbatim; Jory paid twice in I.5; the Captain at the Naming *and the staff in its corner* |
| g04 | Book Two's heading, II.1, II.3 | II.1 rebuilt round Seren's one finger under the stone, against *They kneeled down in the snow and set their four hands under it.*; the True Men carry the old stone to the niche; *those words I have not dared to mend*; II.3's seers *with a lamp*; *Harm to one is harm to both.* with no signpost; *Every scribe before her was of a pair, and when a pair went, their book went into the vault behind them.* |
| g05 | Book Three's heading, III.1, III.2 | III.1 made the night of the oaths, each paid in VI.1; all eleven of I.1's tags carried; Harl's invented burn-line cut; Hesk's halt enlarged into the shortest stretch of the leaf; the banked fire made plain care, not a rite |
| g06 | Book Four's heading, IV.1, IV.3 | Halvard finds the letter and Rhyna touches the blot; the cargo litany chanted; *It was we who turned the key.*; Tobe planted with his thirty years; the father's name on the Trade Council; *Never in his life had he bled.*; *Yet I said naught.*; *The Council kept the letter.* |
| g07 | IV.5, Book Five's heading, V.1 | the coast's words gone; Jory brought into the opening and his kindness paid at his death; *Let the shore laugh, for the shore will never have to meet it.*; *Only the children asked.*; V.1's fires told as three deeds; the touching built by three |
| g08 | V.2, V.5 | V.2 opens on the laugh; the first order spoken in the scene; the soldiers' names; *raw edge to raw edge*; V.5's strips tied to the five named dead; *That is the whole of a True Man.* turned at Corlen's death; *The wood can fear.* on its own line |
| g09 | V.7, Book Six's heading, VI.1 | *like mallets in a far quarry*; *Enno had set the chisel, and Della struck.*; the gate-song with no first voice; the *ael* gloss cut; Idrenna's prayer to *God*; each VI.1 haven entered by its man's own sense; Kael counting Hesk's steps; the three calls never in *thou* |
| g10 | VI.3 | the sliver in Seren's own words; *Wood that knew the sea knoweth the way home.*; the lamp held at the cutting; *None brought fire to the shingle* cut; Harl's plank as a deed; the close paying I.5's *width of one old voice* |
| g11 | the Epilogue, the Book of Knowings and the Last Note | the fire beat dropped; the masters *came no further*; *I read it out to the children.*; the Last Note opens on a child and its litany ends *And no one read them.* |
| w01 | II.2, IV.2, IV.4, IV.6 | each reading pieced its own way; IV.4 the calibrated leaf, word for word; the first pillar's felling told close; IV.6's turn planted and rebuilt; *a torn net*; *Of the men in them naught is held in the grain.* |
| w02 | V.3, V.4, V.6, VI.2 | V.6 no longer a manual; VI.2 no longer a register; *Save once.*; *the grain that rots* and *bearer of new branches*; Hesk's lamp felt from under the ice (§7.2 call 7) |

**What the editor made one, at assembly:**
- *wist* for a fact in five places that had *knew*; I.5's *when the mortar taketh*; the past forms one each (*spake*, *sware*, *bare*, *kneeled*, *forgat*, *bade*; *broke*, *tore*, *climbed*), with *knelt* and *forgot* surviving only inside fixed songs.
- Hesk's line in the Book's grammar, *Someone must keep a light.*, in III.2 and in VI.1.
- Tarnel is *a boatwright's son of Tidesmeet* (II.3), to match VI.3's Tarnard, *the eldest of the boatwrights*. v1.3 and v2.0 both carried that clash.
- **The two-ink ruling:** every split pair in Halyna's mouth is wrapped in `<!-- HALYNA -->` / `<!-- HALYNA END -->`, wherever it stands (19 blocks). Other pairs' halves (Idrenna's, Orvenna's) stay unmarked in the leaf's own ink. The Epilogue's remembered *"Hold the stone."* / *"Hold it, and it will hold thee."* stays unwrapped on purpose: Seren writes that leaf *in one ink*.
- *Hold the stone, Seren.* bare, Book-wide.
- The foreword gained two beats other leaves needed: why the seal was broken (*No pair would break that seal till the wood showed Halyna what the mute stones beside it were. Then Idrenna gave leave.*), and the saying on rolls and wood (*Our rolls forget, and can be made to lie. The wood cannot lie, but it cannot see fear.*), which plants V.7's *The wood lieth not*, IV.4's *Fear leaveth no mark on wood* and V.5's *The wood can fear.* It paid for them by cutting *Words can be turned, and a knowing cannot.*
- II.1 tells only the niche; I.4 tells the new stone set *mark down*. III.2's staff law quotes the slate. VI.1 goes back to *sworn*, now that III.1 swears. *The Bearer of New Branches* in VI.1 and the Knowings, to meet VI.2's gloss. V.5 spends nothing of V.7. Book Three's Argument matches the Contents.
- The datelines were checked against the Reckoning (`wf9/reckoning.md` §4); the three first-year *…of the siege* forms (I.4, II.1, III.1) are kept to orient a first reader.

**What the final pass made one** (continuity C1–C15, all made; arc A1–A20, made or bettered):
- **C1** the lintel paid: *In the year of the homecomings, ere their chisels went into the grey, Halyna cut Halvard's name under his fathers' in the western stair. Beneath it the stone is smooth, and none of his line is left to cut it.* · **C2** in III.2 each teller lays his hand on the stone where it sits · **C3** *Ael I had written before, in Ael'thar.* · **C4** *The wall named them Mystarchs, and the soldiers the Thrones.* (V.5), which pays Ebba's *the last Mystarch* and brings in *Thrones* before VI.1 uses it · **C5** *Every one had spoken to us once* (VI.3) · **C6** Harl's *Burn it* seeded in V.1 as his temper · **C7** the dust echo matches Jack's line · **C8** the third road, *north and east and south* · **C11–C12** two headnotes · **C13** *mine* before *h* · **C14** *wist* for a fact · **C15** *taketh*.
- **A1** V.5 no longer spends V.7: Count IV ends on Kael finding Enno and Della, and Count VI opens on the fleet that went home · **A2** mirrors shown and not explained (V.1's close; VI.3 loses its glosses; the Epilogue's *I read it as it was cut, and no child laughed.*, which pays the Book's laughter) · **A4** V.6's law told once, and the nine tempers as grief, keeping the count of nine · **A5** the forgetting said once · **A7** Jack's three lore lines set as quoted formulas · **A9** Wendhessa's death made legible · **A10** Wick planted again in IV.3's closing gesture, and Wendhessa in the foreword · **A11** the Epilogue ends once, on a picture · **A12** one child: the fisher-girl of the east keep keeps the north lamp, answers the wood and lays the first hand on the boat that comes back · **A13** the foreword's hood · **A15** III.1's geography moved into Seren's italic reading of the rolls · **A17** learnability (*nigh* seeded twice; *thither* and *aught* plain where they would be one-offs) · **A18** the breath before the strike.

**Declined, with the reasons kept:**
- V.5's *a green hull that blanched was no green hull* stays: in Book order it is the plant that lets a first reader understand Pellow's *"They are not green."*
- Idlan and Denna are not named in the foreword: it is at its ceiling, and Idrenna need no cradle-names. Ormund and Penna are learnt by deed in V.7, VI.3 and the Epilogue.
- Both *Every scribe…* lines stay: the foreword (third summer) answers Halvard (first summer).
- *We will not lay it all on the Council.* is not restored: *That is our share of it.* holds the sense.
- *and it was no runner's fault* at Enrella's grave stays out, colder.
- V.5 laid after V.7 (the arc critic's stronger option) is declined: it would break the Contents, the V.5–V.6 spread and the dateline order.
- Jack's dust line keeps *you*; fixed matter is outside the dial.
- *The last Mystarch* stays in I.5: it is a canon name, now planted in V.5.
- *Myststone* stays where it is, as the hinge into *Stone does not translate. But this was never only stone.*
- *reduced to ornament* stays, as a keep of the inventory.
- V.7's part titles stay as v1.3 had them.
- *lest*, *scarce*, *whoso* and *whither* were not seeded where they would not fall naturally.

The order, the tellers and the facing spreads are v2.0's (§2.3). What v3 changed in each leaf, besides the register and the length:

The words are mine.*; the dead teller known by his wet finger; *I write alone* |
| The Invocation | the hearth | (spoken every night) | heavy grammar; *Many hands have held it, and many shall.*; the hearth's answer closes it |
| I.1 The Torn Cloak | Kael | *The first winter, the night after the Rite.* | the creed moved down to the roll, and the roll bare (each man's tag to III.1); the loaf simile gone; *A teller am I, and no singer*; the headnote's agreement; the prologue now opens *The True Men looked to their Captain.* |
| I.2 The Cry of the Bonded | Seren | *The first winter, after the first assault.* | opens on Seren afraid and *Hold the stone*; the glossary at the cry gone; Halyna seated under the broken gate; the cry in two inks; *I sang to the comma, and then I sang the rest.* |
| I.3 The Captain's Long Breath | Hale | *The first winter, in its second month.* | the twenty-four-hour arithmetic dropped; *Every haven had its runners' stone.*; the Captain goes to the far end at dawn; the masons on the breach; Hale carries the marks as words |
| I.4 The Rite of the Cornerstone | Seren | *The first spring of the siege.* | from 2,946 words to 1,212; the law read in halves; the Captain at his own Rite; the True Men raise the stone *mark down*; the staff leaned *for his work was the wall and the war*; Seren's descent *turning by turning* |

| II.2 Of the Breath of the Wood | the wood | *The fifth winter, the Tide that Learned Deceit.* | the reading; a child's question in the headnote; the lore ring cut to its joints; the fault shown before it is named (*We wove the breath thicker.*) |
| II.3 Of the Bonded and the Theoliths | Halyna, to the children | *The first high summer, Enrella's sealing night.* | the halves in two inks; the seers with a lamp; *Harm to one is harm to both.* unsignposted; *a boatwright's son*; Idrenna's nod withheld, then given |
| III.1 Of Their Raising | the True Men, called by Kael | *The first autumn of the siege.* | the night of the oaths; Kael the oath-keeper; Seren reads the rolls; every tag of I.1 carried; Hesk's mother's taper |

| The Book of Knowings | Seren | *Through the war, and closed four years after.* | the soldiers' names paid by V.2; *the Bearer of New Branches*; the Last Note opens on a child |

## 2 · WHAT V2 CHANGED, AND WHAT OF IT STANDS

v2.0 (2026-10-01) applied Jack's twenty-six notes of 2026-09-30. Most of what it settled still stands in v3: the order of the Book, the clock, the pair-names under the lintel, the runners, the marks, the oils, the staff, the twelve deaths, the half-cloak and the wood that knows only what the wood could know. What it got wrong is what Jack turned down on 2026-10-02: the long joined sentences, the length, the chronicle frame, Brenn's blows and the custom of burning. This section records each note of 2026-09-30 as the v3 Book now holds it.

### 2.1 The notes of 2026-09-30 that set the v2 work

- **The impact (J1).** *"The stories have lost some of their impact moments. It feels like you are just reading a description of the text, not an Epic Poem. This is supposed to keep the attention of all listeners of the tale — young and old, first time or 100th time. This should be formatted like the Odyssey but story building like JRR Tolkien. Give the stories feeling, then translate them into the other language."* **Stands**, and v3 answers it better: every tale has one halt told close and its strike sentences on their own paragraphs (§1.4).
- **The form.** Offered verse lines, Jack chose prose: *"I still like the heightened prose."* **Stands.** The paragraph is the unit, and the only verse in the Book is the songs. *"Can the heightened prose be made to be read aloud?"* is **superseded** by K4: *"I don't care is it is easier to read aloud."*
- **The register.** *"The new text should NOT be plain words. The words should be flowery when necessary. The original text should be just a bit hard to read for someone used to modern English. I like the longer sentences joined together. I like that there is a lot of difference between The Book and the Plain Words."* **Superseded for sentence length** by K4: the v2.0 run-ons failed, and the Book is hard through old grammar, not through length. **Standing:** *not plain words*, *hard to read for someone used to modern English*, and *a lot of difference between The Book and the Plain Words*, which the HEAVY register and the retold Plain now deliver (§1.2, §1.10).

### 2.2 Jack's notes of 2026-09-30, note by note, as the v3 Book holds them

**J1 · Impact moments; an epic, not a description of one; the Odyssey's form and Tolkien's building; feeling first, then the other tongue.**
- The strike lines of v3, in Book order: *He tore it down the back.* (I.1) · *There was no silence.* (I.2) · *The Captain standeth still.* (I.3) · *It was the mark of Aldwena.* and *Four hands laid it in two.* (I.4) · *In the morning she did not wake.* (I.5) · *It was their own.* and *No one heard it fall.* (II.1) · *She woke beside him.* (II.3) · *The ice held.* (III.2) · *▒▒▒▒ kicked it aside.*, *We obeyed.* and *Tarnel did not wake.* (IV.3) · *And we were the one his foot found.* (IV.4) · *His father was Daveth.* and *The tune stopped.* (IV.5) · *Then we turned.* (IV.6) · *I let go.* (V.1) · *It said Burn.* and *The broken grain met.* (V.2) · *His were.* (V.5, Count II) · *She was gone.*, *Ye cannot tell.* and *"One," he said.* (V.7, Part I) · *Pellow lay dead at the parapet.* (V.7, Part II) · *The ice took Hesk too.* (VI.1) · *It was green.* and *The wood took it.* (VI.3) · *It was the last of his half.* and *I knew the bite.* (the Epilogue).
- **From the Odyssey, the format**, unchanged: Books with their Arguments; the Invocation as the proem; the story begun in the middle, at the banner, reaching back through Books Two to Four and coming forward through Five and Six; catalogues (the roll of the Twelve, the havens twice, the cargo count, the carvings, the Thrones, the roll of the dead); epithets and formula lines; songs set in the tales; the law of the guest and its breaking (IV.3, IV.4) and its answer (the kneeling, VI.3); two homecomings, ours (VI.1) and theirs (VI.2, VI.3); recognition by token (the boat's stem, a chisel's bite, the warden's long sleeves); a bard inside the story (Seren); listeners who interrupt (the child of II.2, the child of IV.4, the children of II.3, the child of the Last Note).
- **From Tolkien, the methods and never the sentences**, unchanged: close scenes breaking the annal distance; genealogies (Aldwena, then Rhyna; the quarry-wardens, then Halvard; Elspet, then Daveth, then Merrick; Ebba, then Marl, then the fisher-girl); many names for one thing (the grey, the fog, the breath, the Mystlands); found documents (the founders' leaf, the rolls, the warden's letter, Brenn's confession, the youngest apprentice's leaf); the long defeat, in which every victory costs a strip of the cloak; and the turn of grace no one looked for, the green sliver.
- **Feeling first, then the other tongue.** The English is written first, with full feeling. The *Original* must now be set down from v3 (K2; §1.11).
- **v2.0's bent** (more impact moments than the outline's one to three, and tales past 900 words) is **resolved**: one halt to a tale, and every tale within its ceiling.

**J2 · Show that Halyna is one name for two people, simply, and never as a "they/them" reading.**
- **The lintel.** Every pair-name is set under one thin stroke the width of the name, as a capstone lies across two posts. The source marks it `{{Halyna}}` and the builder draws the stroke. Seren says why once, in the foreword: *So write I every bonded pair, under one stroke, as a lintel on two posts. It is one name, and it is the name of two.* II.3 has already seeded the picture: the pair-name is made *as a gate is made of two posts and the stone laid across them*.
- **The English.** A pair-name always takes a plural verb (*Halyna are*, *Halyna were silent*), and in its own mouth says *we two*. **Changed in v3:** v2.0 showed two people at the first mention in every tale with *he and she*, *the two of them* or *both*, and used those phrases 52 times in all. v3 uses neither phrase once: *The lintel says two, and the verb says two. Nothing more is needed* (craft rule 20). When one of them acts alone, the Book uses the cradle-name and *he* or *she*. *Husband and wife* is said in I.2 and II.3. No singular *they* is used for anyone.
- **Six pair-names:** Halyna (Halvard and Rhyna), Aldwena (the first builders), Idrenna (the eldest pair), Orvenna (Ormund and Penna, the wall-masters), Enrella (Enno and Della, the young pair) and Wendhessa (the archivists of Eldhythe). The cradle-names of Aldwena (Aldun and Bena), of Idrenna (Idlan and Denna) and of Wendhessa (Wendel and Tessa) live only in these notes.

**J3 · Make clear where each Book and tale stands in time.**
- **The hinge** is still the banner (§3.1), and the whole clock is in §3.
- **Changed in v3:** v2.0's two-clause datelines in capitals (*LAID IN THE THIRD WINTER… · OF ONE DAY AT THE OUTERMOST OUTPOST…*) were the history-book voice. A v3 dateline names only when the tale was laid, in eight words or fewer, in plain italic (*The third winter, in the Tide of Remembering.*). When its matter happened is told by the tale itself, or by these notes (§3.5).
- **An Argument under every Book**, one line that spoils nothing (§1.4).
- **Every tale's first sentence of story gives an hour.**

**J4 · The explaining that interrupts the story is needed, but must be done better.**
- v3 goes further than v2.0. Explanation travels in deed (the Rite read off the slate in halves while Seren forgets her bucket; the mark drawn once in the ash), in a line of speech, in a song (the masons' chant names the three parts of the siege day; the dawn drill carries the three calls), in the foreword said once, or out of the Book into these notes (§2.4).
- II.3 is the one tale that may teach, and it teaches through the children: the small girl's question, Enno's jest, the boy who counts twelve on his fingers and runs out of fingers, and Idrenna, who do not nod, and then do.
- Nothing is explained at or near a halt.

**J5 · The poems are wonderful: keep them.**
- Every verse stands letter for letter: *The Lay of the Torn Cloak* (I.1); *Two at the Gate* (I.2, and at Halyna's grave in the Epilogue); *The Grey Boat* with its two unsung lines (IV.5); *The Song of the Years* (V.6); *The Two Homes* (VI.3).
- The short verses v2.0 added stand too: the masons' work-chant (I.2), the True Men's dawn drill of the three calls (I.3), and Rhyna's mother's cradle-rhyme of Aldwena's line (I.4). Idrenna's prayer for the dead, in prose and in two halves, is told whole once (V.7 Part I) and told without its words at Ebba's grave (I.5). Its address is now *God*, never *Lord* (§7.2 call 13).

**J6 · People die in every epic; invest in them so the dying lands.**
- Twelve deaths, D1 to D12 (§3.8). Each is planted as a habit, a want or a promise, told whole once, cold, and remembered for its good.
- **The half-cloak.** The Captain wears the unwritten half of the torn cloak and cuts a hand's breadth from it for the wall's dead laid at this hearth, bound on a pike over the grave. Seven strips are told in the Book (Jory, Corlen, Wick, Enrella, Pellow, Ebba, Halyna), and Hesk's is a call (§7.2 call 3). The last goes to Halyna's gate, and he walks into the seventh winter in a plain grey cloak.
- **Changed in v3:** v2.0 followed every death with three things, one of them the hood. In v3 the hood goes back twice in the whole Book (§1.4), and the strip carries the rest.

**J7 · The runner needs a purpose, and a horn cannot carry that far.**
- **The horn is gone everywhere.** The Captain calls with his own voice at the battery's side, and a man can stand in only one place: *"Naught may change that mind but his own voice at my side, lad, and a man can stand but in one place."* (Kael to Hale, I.3).
- **The True Men** fight their batteries by the mind he gave each at dawn: *where to watch, what shot to lay, and when to hold.*
- **The runners are his eyes.** A runner's word is three words, like a chisel-cut: *"Hull hangeth back."* · *"Marks on the wood."* · *"They are looking."* · *"They are not green."*
- **The runners' oath and stone.** *"What I saw, I carry. I carry nothing else."* **New in v3:** *Every haven had its runners' stone. Ours we made the first week, of a fallen block by the inner gate, and on it we sware the old oath.* Brenn swore it at fifteen *with mine hand flat on the runners' stone*. The morning after IV.3 the wall's runners swear it again and add four words: *Not as Brenn carried.*

**J8 · The mark is the Stonwryt: a symbol every bonded pair owns and holds sacred.**
- The seers read it at the cradle; the guild gives it at the sealing, *a name for the mouth, and a mark for the stone*; *No hand but theirs may cut it, and no other pair will break it.* (II.3). Jack's words are set as a quoted formula: *The Stonwryt the masters call it: a vow sealed in living rock, earned by toil and skill, and no gold buyeth it.* Master-pairs struck coin under their marks: *trust, sealed in stone.* (II.1).
- No pair cuts or breaks another pair's mark, which is why Brenn's bundle waited under Wendhessa's seal until Idrenna gave leave. The unbonded have no mark (*I have no mark. Never shall I cut one.*).
- **Four marks recur** and can be matched by the player (§5.6): Aldwena's, Halyna's, Enrella's and Wendhessa's broken seal.
- **It is held sacred, but it is not a sign of the faith.** No pair's mark is drawn as a cross.

**J9 · Halyna had to make the oils for the anointing, and that is part of what they found.**
- *Three oils the Rite must have, that none living had made.* The build by three (I.4):
  - **cedar,** from fallen heartwood only, at the stand where the first staff was cut; *A living tree would they not fell.* Stannard knocks and listens: *"I know which of these will hold."* They render it at this hearth, *and on a wet night its stones breathe it out yet*;
  - **granite-bloom:** *Bloom the masons call the pale crust on worked stone untended for lives of men, for it flowereth where none come.* The children scrape it, Seren among them, *as gleaners along a stubble-field*, and *My fingers were raw with it.* (Unsaid: the forgetting that broke the Keep grew the strength of its Commander.);
  - **myrrh,** which the mountain cannot give: Idrenna carried one flask the whole road for the guild's dead, and *On the pass the ground would not take them*. Rhyna warms it into the cedar. *I wist for whom it had been carried.*
- So the Rite falls on the fourteenth day, by a thin sun.

**J10 · Why was the staff left behind? Generations of staffs, with Halyna finding the original.**
- **The law of the staff,** read off the slate in halves: *"A staff is cut for its work, and leaveth not its work,"* / *"and when the work is done, the staff is laid down in it."* The founders laid it in the black chest when they went down, *as masons lay by their tools at dusk*.
- Jack's description, as a quoted formula: *The slate nameth it so: a rod of polished wood capped with the finest polished ashlar, borne by every overseer of a great Stonewright work since the founding.* Then *It was the first staff of all.*
- In every falling haven the masters leaned its staff against the falling wall (*as a woman banketh the fire on her last night in her mother's house, the same as every night*), and all ten were lost. The Captain leans the first in the gatehouse corner, *for his work was the wall and the war*. Jack's line: *If you doubt the tale, go and look at the dust on it.* In the seventh spring it is gone, a clean stroke in the dust; *The work is done. I know the stair that goes no more.* · *I have not gone down to look.*

**J11 · Every story fits the others like a puzzle piece.** The locks are in §4.

**J12 · Orrowen has the rhythm of a line just short of song. Seren enjoys the tongue, and her craft is words. Plain Words unwinds it.**
- *I love our tongue.* Seren sets each tale *as masons set a course*, and says each word three times *to hear whether it will hold*. She lingers on *point* (*I love that word.*), on *bloom*, *shore* and *Naelear*, and a word fails her: *I tried that word under my tongue, and it gave, like mortar gone to sand.* (*fortune*, IV.1).
- Plain Words no longer unwinds the Book: it retells it (§1.10). The Orrowen measure, the dual and the consonant rhyme are the Tongues team's (§5.8, §5.14).

**J13 · Halyna never speak of themselves in the third person.**
- They say *we two*, twenty times in v3. When one speaks of the other it is *he*, *she* or the cradle-name.
- Every stone tale Halyna once told of themselves is Seren's. II.3 is the one stone tale still theirs.
- **Bent, as before:** Rhyna says *So were we two sealed, and named Halyna.* once in II.3, as a name received (§7.2 call 21).

**J14 · The seers read a bond God made before birth; they do not choose it.**
- II.3: *We hold that God made each pair one ere either drew breath. The seers go among the cradles with a lamp, and bend low, and read what was written before ever we were born. They choose not.* And: *the bond is made before the cradle, read in the cradle, and made whole only at the sealing.* Idrenna's prayer says it again in rite: *"God, thou madest these two one ere either drew breath."*

**J15 · We know of only one bonded pair, and Halyna do too much. They should be the discoverers of the stories Seren writes.**
- Halyna keep what is theirs by right: the cry, the finds, the making of the oils, the reading aloud of Brenn, the knowings, their own mark, the telling of II.3, and the kneeling at the front of the line. **v3 moves two more deeds to other hands:** the True Men raise the new capstone into the gate (I.4), and Halyna together, not Halvard alone, cut Halvard's name in the lintel (the Epilogue). Their finds and the deeds of others are in §3.9.

**J16 · *We remember* is like *Amen*.**
- After every stone tale the whole hearth answers *And the hearth said: We remember.* (33 times in v3, the Invocation's included). It never varies.
- It comes out of the first words of the Title. Old Ebba of Fenholm said it first, the first night the hearth asked a tale, *into the quiet* (the foreword). Most nights after, she began it *a breath before the rest*. After her death the hearth waits a breath for her (I.5, and at Fenholm's fire in VI.1).
- A wood leaf is answered with silence, *And no one answered.*, until VI.3, when her great-granddaughter says *"We remember."* to the wood first, and the hearth follows.
- It is never said over the warden. It is first said over a killing by our own after IV.3, and *the rest were slow to follow. But they followed.* It is never a joke, a Whisper or store art. **New in v3:** it is a vow as well as an answer (§1.9).

**J17 · The Guest's killing is brutal, and his broken body is thrown into the boat.**
- **Stands, with K7's change.** The post's men take up staves, stones of the shingle and the boat-poles; *One of them took up my pole*; *The blows I counted. I count them yet.*; *When he lay still, ▒▒▒▒ screamed on, and some went on.*; *Both his arms they broke: the one that had held the warden's neck so gently, and the one that had held the blade.*; *By the heels they drew him down the shingle, and his head went on every stone.*; and they cast him into his boat *as a fisher casteth a torn net into the bilge.* Brenn strikes no blow (§1.7). The wood holds his last hour by his blood (IV.4) and the boat holds the throwing (IV.6).

**J18 · The burning of the boat has a reason.**
- **Superseded by K8.** The reason is the warden's own: to hide the deed, *Leave naught to show.* v2.0's custom of the coast is gone from the Book (§1.8).

**J19 · Brenn is a runner who must take the report to the Council.**
- **Stands.** Brenn was the outpost's runner, sworn at fifteen. *On the morrow, ere any woke, I picked the stone out of the dust. Why I did it, I know not. At his door ▒▒▒▒ gave me the letter. So the gift and the lie went into one pack, and I ran it.* He runs a day and a night, saying the oath every mile. At Eldhythe an old clerk asks *"Is it so?"*, and Brenn answers *"It is written."* He confesses in his old age to Wendhessa and dies *as a man setteth down a load*.

**J20 · The warden's name is blotted out with ▒▒▒▒.**
- **Stands.** Always exactly four marks, never with an ending, twelve times in v3 (once in IV.1, eleven times in IV.3). *The guild buried him under his name. The warden's name it cut away.* IV.1 shows the blot without a word of why, and Rhyna lays her finger on it and takes it away; IV.3's annal says why.
- At every blot *Halyna were silent*, so the hearth hears a silence where the name was. In the wood he never had one: he is *the master of that house*, *the host*.

**J21 · The boat with the body rams the Shorelanders' ships in its fury: two boats, though the ship-masters say twenty.**
- **Stands.** *Twice on that road, where the breath is thin, hulls of the shore-men lay across our way. … We went into them burning, low, where they were heaviest.* (IV.6). In IV.5 Elspet counts the boats on her fingers and lays her hand flat on the board: *There were two boats.* / *Now the ship-masters say twenty.* IV.1 strikes two timber-boats off a cargo-list, *lost at the edge*.
- **Changed in v3:** Seren no longer remarks on it. v2.0's seam (*The wood says two. The ship-masters say twenty. I have kept both.*) is cut; the two facing leaves agree in silence, and the Invocation keeps both every night.

**J22 · The few sailors who survived the ramming started the rumours.**
- **Stands, and now carries more:** with no custom, the rumours are the whole root of the coast's fear of grey boats (§1.8). Three came home; Daveth came home on a spar; *All that autumn the three told it, and never again set foot in a boat.* The outpost men swore in their cups. *The shore laughed at the three from the spar.* The written lie swallowed the spoken truth: *And who will argue with writing?* Daveth *drank on his tale, and the harbour drowned him a stone's throw from his mother's house*; his son is Merrick.
- The fear helped end the felling: *After the year of the burning boat no crew would row within sight of the grey, and the rolls say not why.* (IV.1), against the wood's *In those same summers the iron came no more to the edge. The grove had not waited to see it.* (V.4).

**J23 · The stone and the grain read very differently. Keep that.**
- **Stands, by a new road.** Both now carry old grammar, so the difference is in who speaks: the stone in the tellers' own voices, curt or weighing or circling; the wood in the eldest grammar of the Book, object first, in the wood's *we*, with its own names for things and its ring formula; and above each wood leaf, Halyna's fragments, which are like nothing else in the Book. Seren's headnotes are stone even on wood leaves, so the seam stays visible.

**J24 · The grain holds only what the wood could know.**
- **Stands, and is tightened** (§1.5). What moved out of the wood's mouth in v2.0 stays out, and v3 moves four more things.

| Wood leaf | Taken out of the wood's mouth | Version |
|---|---|---|
| II.2 | the Shore's names for the mist and the pillars; *no one lies under a silverbark* | v2.0 (*none lie beneath a silver elder*) |
| IV.2 | *you call it Blackthorn*; the grain's tell | v2.0; and in v3 *Blackthorn* left the headnote too |
| IV.4 | the gift's road by place and name | v2.0 (the road in the wood's senses); in v3 the line naming the arms is gone as well |
| IV.6 | *turned from us, and ran*; *They did not know it was ignorance* | v2.0 (Seren's headnote keeps the second, as hers) |
| IV.6 | *as men cast a net they mean to burn* | v3 (a Shore intent) |
| V.3 | *as you two feel each other's hurt across a city* | v2.0 |
| V.6 | the grain-names, the root-men's kinds, the mechanics, *Sethvaren* and *Saenvael* | v2.0; and in v3 *Aelralen* and four Tide-names |
| VI.2 | every fear-name and grain-name; what the havens did with the black wood | v2.0; and in v3 Seren's Shore lines before each Throne |

- Where the two sides disagree, the Book keeps both without remarking on it. Glasspire's night stays unsolved for ever: some who fled saw a shadow in the fog, Pellow saw nothing, and the wood says *No throne sailed the night the spire fell. Of that night the grain hath naught.*

**J25 · The common origin of the two tongues is hinted at, never revealed. The parting stays untold. No cross-sign is a holy sign.**
- **One hint reaches the hearth:** *ael*. In V.7 Part I, *The carving named that Tide Aelthae. That night I knew its first half. Ael I had written before, in Ael'thar. I have written the name, and I will not write what I thought.* **Changed in v3:** no gloss is given. The reader joins it alone from three meetings: *Aelrhen … bond-to-stone* (IV.4), *Aelvaren … the Bearer of the Binding* (IV.6), and the Joined Tide set against *Aelthae*.
- *The first builders had known war. Against what, the slates say not.* Never tied to the parting.
- The Bar Before keeps its drawn shape, but it is never holy, and no pair's mark is drawn as a cross. The warden's half-mark is *the half of something, cut off in the middle*.

**J26 · The story is a standalone document; how it was made and how it enters the game go into a separate NOTES document.**
- **Stands.** The Book begins with its title page and holds no writer's note, no marker's explanation and no *Translation, unlocked by* line. **K3 adds:** the copy Jack shares is that whole standalone story (§1.11).

### 2.3 The v2 reordering, and the anchors

v2.0's changes to the order stand in v3:
- Book Three holds only the havens' raising and fall: III.1 *Of Their Raising* and III.2 *Of Their Fall*, which v1.3 had as one tale in two parts.
- The Homecomings and the Thrones moved to **Book Six, the Book of the Homecomings**, where they face each other: VI.1 (v1.3's homecoming captions, now each True Man's own entry) and VI.2 (v1.3's III.2). v1.3's VI.1, the green wood, is VI.3.
- **Anchors for the builder and for links from other documents.** A tale that keeps its number keeps its anchor (`t-I-1` and so on). Links that pointed at v1.3's III.2 (the Thrones) belong at `t-VI-2`, and links at v1.3's VI.1 (the green wood) at `t-VI-3`.
- **The facing spreads** are v2.0's (§4.6). In v3 the Contents names each one (*facing II.2*), since a headnote no longer holds a rule of the Book.
- **The fallback** (outline §12, call 1) is v1.3's order with only the Arguments and datelines added.

### 2.4 What moved out of the Book and into these notes

Nothing below is said in any tale. Each item has its home in this document.
- **The mechanics** (§5.10, §5.11, and the fleet design): a battery is a gun group; the three Fight verbs; Command Time and its numbers, including the twenty-four-hour day, which v3 also takes out of I.3; the twelve-gun fit; every gun hauled is a course not laid (said in the Book only as Orvenna's two halves); the twelvefold count of a pair's work (the Book keeps only *The work of a pair is the work of twelve, as the old masters counted it.*); the grains' tempers as tells (V.6 keeps them as griefs, with no names); the Standard-Bearer and the standard passing (one hearth-saying stays: *choose which heart ye leave them*); the seed, the husks, the bladder and the crown-hulls; the root-men's kinds and their knots of four; the groans and the third cry; the carving names beyond the Book of Knowings; the rules of knowing; and the runners' law as the in-world form of *a Knowing is always true*.
- **The world-rules** (§6): why a haven need not be a harbour (the Book keeps only Ulden's line); the double meaning of *Rivenkeep*; the Ael'thar linguistics beyond *ael*, now including *ael*'s meaning; the soft carved form *Aelthar*; the Orrowen dual and the lintel in the course-hand.
- **Wood names heard no more in the Book:** *Sethvaren* and *Saenvael* (v2.0); *Aelralen*, the bond of roots, and four Tide-names, *Leathae*, *Vaelthae*, *Rhenthae* and *Eirthae* (v3). V.6 keeps *Ralenthae* (*the tide before us*) and *Aelthae* (*ours*), and V.3 glosses the first once.
- **The soldiers' names for the Thrones and the wall's names for the grains** (v3): *the Leviathan* to *the Crystal Lich*, and *Heartoak* to *Silverbark*. They live in §6 and in the game's battle messages. The Book keeps only *The wall named them Mystarchs, and the soldiers the Thrones.*
- **The gift's road by name** (IV.4): the warden's step; Brenn's pack, a day and a night; the archive chest of Eldhythe under Wendhessa's seal; the Fall; the Road; the Keep. The Book gives it in the wood's senses alone, and v3 drops the line that named the arms.
- **The cradle-names** of Aldwena, Idrenna and Wendhessa.
- **The Throne table for the VI.3 token** (§5.13).
- **Every *Translation, unlocked by* line** (§5.6, §5.7), and every marker's meaning (§5.13).
- **The map from old tale numbers to new** (§2.3), and **the clock of the events** (§3.5), which the datelines no longer carry.
- **The writers' notes, word counts and critics' findings,** and the editors' logs, which §1.12 summarizes.

---

## 3 · THE RECKONING AND THE CAST

### 3.1 The dating system

- **One hinge: the banner.** The Rivenmen count from the dusk the Captain lifted the torn cloak, in deep winter, with snow on the hearth. These notes write it **B0**, and years before it **B−75** and so on. The Book never uses a numeral, and its datelines now say only when a tale was laid (§2.2, J3). The dates of the events are here.
- **Before the banner** the hearth counts in **generations of the shore**, about twenty-five years each (*Two generations the Trade Council sent out the axes*), and in named ages (§3.2).
- **Inside the siege** it counts by **winters**, W1 to W6. Each siege year runs from its winter to the next, so *the second spring* is the spring after the second winter.
- **A Tide each winter.** *Each growing after the first went out as tides go, at the leaf-fall, when its ring was laid, and came out of the breath with the winter.* (V.6). The older the wood, the later it came ready, so the Tide and the winter always agree, and either one dates a tale.
- **After the siege** the count is *the years after the homecomings*.
- **The wood counts in summers and rings and never knows the banner.** Seren's headnotes give only the season she knew it in.
- **The Mystaeri live two and a half of our lives** (*we live twice as long as ye, and half again*, II.2), so one of their lives is about three of ours (*We listened three of their lives*, V.4).

### 3.2 The ages

| The hearth's name | When | What it holds | Where told |
|---|---|---|---|
| **before all reckoning** | never dated | one people and one tongue, and their parting: **untold**; nothing in any tale touches it (J25) | — |
| **the First Days** (the age of the Keep) | more than fifteen generations before the banner | the first builders on the riven ridge; Aldwena lay the capstone of the inner gate; the founders' leaf; the youngest apprentice's leaf of the hearth; the black chest set in order | II.1 · I.4 |
| **the Days of the Havens** | from twelve generations before | the folk go down from the Keep, Eldhythe first and Glasspire last; walls for the high days and the weather; a staff cut for every work | III.1 · II.1 |
| **the Harvest** | five to three generations before (B−120 to B−70) | the axes; the regrowth cut again and again; the plea cut on the mute thing; the Stonewrights' warning shelved | IV.1 · IV.2 |
| **the Summer of the Guest** | B−75, late summer | one day at the outpost and what followed it: the Guest, the killing, the boat burned by the warden's order, the ramming, Brenn's run, the Aelvaren's homecoming, the vote | IV.3 to IV.6 · V.4 |
| **the Forgetting** | from the last axe to the first dark sail (B−70 to B−2) | Brenn's confession; the stones become ornament; the late humility; behind the grey, the first growing waits on the last of the old voices | IV.1 · IV.3 · V.4 · V.6 |
| **the Fall** | twenty months, B−2 to B0 | ten havens burned in turn; the column gathers haven by haven; after Glasspire it turns up the mountain | III.2 |
| **the Road** | the last month of the Fall | the passes; the night of the pass (Tarnel, Wendhessa) | III.2 · II.3 · IV.3 |
| **the banner** | day 0, at dusk | the column comes in; the capstone turned; the cloak, the cry and the terms; the first course | I.1 · II.1 · I.2 |
| **the Fourteen Nights** | day 0 to day 14 | the black chest (night 3); the oils; the Rite (day 14, by a thin sun); Halyna's mark and the Title cut, the stone set mark down, and the youngest apprentice's leaf found (that night) | I.4 |
| **the first month** | day 14 to about day 35 | the first tale (night 15); the Naming (day 21); the first assault (about day 24); the first glyph | I.1 · I.5 · I.3 · V.1 · I.2 |
| **the six winters** | W1 to W6, a Tide each | the Hasty Tide · the Second Tide · the Tide of Remembering · the Joined Tide · the Tide that Learned Deceit · the Last Tide | Book Five |
| **the Going-Down** | the end of the sixth winter to the sixth autumn | the Thrones come as the last fleets go home; the havens retaken from spring to autumn, *the year of the homecomings* | Book Six |
| **the Sending** | the sixth autumn | the green sliver, the boat, the kneeling | VI.3 |
| **the Years After** | from the seventh winter | Halyna's death (seventh winter); the Stone (seventh spring); Seren writes the Epilogue in the fourth year after the homecomings, and the Last Note that winter | the Epilogue · the Last Note |

### 3.3 The master timeline

*"prop." marks a date the Book never states and these notes propose, so the pieces fit. Everything else is said or implied in the Book.*

**The elder days**

| When | What |
|---|---|
| before all reckoning | one people, one tongue, and the parting: untold |
| ages before the banner | the Mystaeri are old in the world (*Ere your fathers cut their first stone, we were old*): the breath, the wood they guide, the pillars at the edge (II.2) |
| the First Days (prop. before B−375) | the first builders come up onto the riven ridge, *cleft from its crown to its root*, and make a hold of its own stone; the first quarry-warden cuts his name in the lintel of the western stair *and left the stone below it for his sons*; Aldwena lay the capstone of the inner gate and cut their mark and vow beneath it, *where no eye will see it again till the wall fall*; the youngest apprentice writes the leaf of the hearth; before they go down, the founders lay the Keep's slates and the first staff in the black chest, *as masons lay by their tools at dusk* |
| the Days of the Havens (from about B−300) | Eldhythe first, *that men call the Eldest*; then the other nine along the coast, Glasspire last, raised over seven generations; each haven's roll begins *This day was the first course laid, and a staff cut for the work.*; master-pairs strike coin under their marks; *Every wall of the ten was Stonewright work* |
| generation by generation | the Keep goes out of mind, *till only the songs held it*; no pair comes up to point its mortar; *In some winter that none told, the capstone of the inner gate came down from its two posts.* / *No one heard it fall.* |

**The Harvest (B−120 to B−70)**

| When | What |
|---|---|
| B−120 | *the high summer of the havens' pride*; the ships find the black pillars at the hem of the grey; the ship-masters name them the Mystholders; the Trade Council sends out the axes |
| B−120 to B−70 | two generations of felling, cargo by cargo: buttresses and bridges, the three spans at Tidesmeet, the pilings of Fenholm, the roof-trees of Holtward, the cranes of Highreach, the galleries of Sandreach, the hall on the ice at Rimewatch, the forges of Emberhythe; the axes beaten out at Carnhold; then a lord's dancing-hall at Eldhythe, the harbour-houses' trade-tables and dice-boards, and a merchant's bed at Tidesmeet |
| through it | the stumps put up young pillars, and the axemen cut the regrowth again and again for poles, *and the guild bought them for scaffold* |
| prop. B−110 to B−90 | the Mystaeri cut their plea on the mute thing, standing stones at the feet of the pillars yet standing, with Shoreland words among their letters by sound; the axemen carry the stones away with the timber; some hang in the harbour-houses, *reduced to ornament*, and the guild locks the rest in its archives with its own warning |
| prop. about B−95 | three such stones go up over a harbour-house fire on the quay at Eldhythe, where they hang *Three generations* |
| the middle years | the Stonewrights' warning is read once in the Council hall at Eldhythe and laid on a shelf |
| about B−85 | a clerk's line under a count of poles: *All along the edge, the stumps put up nothing.* The grey thins, and the Council names it fortune |
| B−85 to B−76 | over the edge the between-light goes hard and white; the newborns of the long-lived come frailer every spring; the elders counsel the trees' patience (V.4) |
| prop. B−76 | one of the long-lived takes the name Aelrhen with his errand, with three words gathered across a long life, and the gift |

**The Summer of the Guest (B−75, late summer)**

| When | At the outpost and on the shore | On the water and in the grey |
|---|---|---|
| day 1, morning | *out of the grey came a small grey boat*, with no wind; the stranger walks to the warden's door, and ▒▒▒▒ *stood within, and came no further*; he speaks in his own tongue, then *"Stop… Sky… Dying…"*; he kneels and sets down the gift *as ye would set down bread before a guest*; a gull walks among the stones; *▒▒▒▒ kicked it aside.*; his men laugh, and Brenn stands among them; the neck held, the foreheads; the blade touched to the warden's arm; he tears away, *Never in his life had he bled*, and the cut goes deep; *"Seize him."*; the Guest kneels again, the blade turned to his own arm; *"Kill him."*; staves, stones and boat-poles, one of them Brenn's pole in another man's hands; both arms broken; drawn by the heels down the shingle; cast into the boat *as a fisher casteth a torn net into the bilge*; ▒▒▒▒'s order, *Leave naught to show*; the wood screams; the boat shoved off with the same poles; Tobe washes the poles; *Well out, the boat turned*. The same day the clerk writes the letter | — |
| day 1, evening | — | on the road home, the burning Aelvaren rams two timber-boats of the felling, low with black wood; *Of the men in them naught is held in the grain*; three of the two crews come home on a spar, Daveth among them |
| day 2, before dawn | *On the morrow, ere any woke*, Brenn picks the gift out of the dust; at his door ▒▒▒▒ gives him the letter; the gift and the lie go into one pack | — |
| days 2 and 3 | Brenn runs a day and a night, saying the oath every mile; at Eldhythe the old clerk: *"Is it so?"* / *"It is written."* | the Aelvaren runs the hidden channels under the thinnest breath, burning down to a keel and a few ribs |
| the night it comes home | — | under the silver elders the council lays its hands on the keel and knows all but why the host drew back; *They saw only an act of war.*; the keel is laid among the roots, *And the roots drank*; the young put the question over it; the vote is carried *by the weight of anger and grief*; that night the first carving, *Grow until the shore is silent.*, is cut into the straightest young tree |
| that autumn | the three from the spar tell it in every harbour-house and never set foot in a boat again; the shore laughs at them; Brenn, in Eldhythe, laughs not and says naught | — |
| later that year | the outpost's men swear in their cups to the turning and the scream, and to three words no two of them agree on | — |
| B−75 to B−70 | *After the year of the burning boat no crew would row within sight of the grey*; the near pillars grow scarce and the northern hills send cheaper timber; *So the axes stopped. No one decided it.* | *In those same summers the iron came no more to the edge. The grove had not waited to see it.* |

**The vote and the growings (B−75 to the sixth winter)**

| When | What |
|---|---|
| after the vote | the council chooses its wood: *As many years as a wood had when it was chosen, so much could it hold, and the older it was, the later it came ready*; the eldest pillars are left standing |
| B−74 | the first growing, green wood of one season, is ready, one sign to a hull. *At the edge of your sea it lay, while your children had children.* *Your boats passed it in the breath, and saw naught.* |
| B−75 to about B−3 | the elders who pleaded go down into the moss one by one; *While one elder that had pleaded yet lived, the growing lay ready at the edge of your sea, and went not.* (V.4) |
| about B−3 (prop.) | the last of them goes down, and those who cried for war cut the growing loose |
| through the decades | older wood is sung into hulls; the carvers are fewer at every growing; those who cried for war *died ere their years, under the thin sky, and their own forgat them* |
| W1 to W6 | each later growing comes ready in its turn and goes out with its winter |
| the end of W6 | the last carvers draw the eldest pillars out of the earth and grow them into thrones; *Over the last groves the sky came open, hard and white*, and the children of the long-lived lie down in the light |

**The Forgetting (B−70 to B−2)**

| When | What |
|---|---|
| B−70 | the last axes; the felling generation grows old |
| prop. about B−62 | Halvard and Rhyna are born, read together in one cradle; Rhyna's mother sings over it the rhyme of Aldwena's line |
| prop. about B−52 to B−45 | Idrenna teach them as Theoliths |
| prop. about B−40 | Halyna are sealed: the pair-name and their own mark |
| about B−40 | Voss is born at Eldhythe, and Jory with him on the same quay |
| B−36 | ▒▒▒▒ dies old, *in his bed of black wood* |
| B−35 (prop.) | Brenn, in his old age the last of the outpost's twelve, confesses at the temple at Eldhythe to Wendhessa, the archivists; they seal his words with the gift, with their mark; the guild buries him under his name and cuts the warden's away; *Wendhessa sealed his words, and told no one.* Brenn dies *as a man setteth down a load* |
| B−33 | Voss, seven, skips to *The Grey Boat* on the quay, Jory turning the rope; Merrick takes the rope away: *"Not that one."* |
| prop. about B−25 | Glasspire's great glass *threw the sun a day's sail out into the grey*; *Where it fell, the breath burned away.* (III.1; VI.2) |
| about B−20 to B−2 | *Late, very late, the wheel of pride turned.* The temples are mended, and men climb the guild's steps for counsel; *But humility came too late.* |
| about B−12 | Seren and Tarnel, a boatwright's son of Tidesmeet, are read together and laid in one cradle |

**The Fall and the Road (B−2 to B0): twenty months**

*The havens fall in the order they were raised, which is the order III.1 and III.2 tell them.*

| # | Haven | Fell | How | Its True Man |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| — | the road | before the Fall reached him | — | **Kael**, *of no haven, for the Captain found me on a road* |
| 1 | **Eldhythe**, the Eldest Haven | *On the first morning of that spring*, twenty months before the banner; the city held three months | *From dawn to dusk the harbour burned*; on the last evening *the tide came in over the ash* | Corlen of the harbour |
| 2 | **Tidesmeet**, where the river meeteth the tide | late summer, in three days | the three spans went down, *The middle stood longest* | Tamm of the three spans |
| 3 | **Fenholm**, the town that was always sinking | the first days of autumn, in one night | *as if the fen had only been waiting*; Ebba carried her course-stone out on her own back | Marl the fen-born |
| 4 | **Holtward**, the keep under the canopy | the middle of autumn | burned in its trees | Aske of the canopy |
| 5 | **Carnhold**, the stronghold of the cairns | the end of autumn | burned in its smithies; *The last anvil rang a while alone.* | Garvel of the smithies |
| 6 | **Emberhythe**, the forge on the burning shore | through the winter, five months | the whole column wintered there; a mason carried out a charred plank | Harl of the burning shore |
| 7 | **Sandreach**, the citadel carved in the canyon wall | the spring of the year before the banner | taken gallery by gallery | Ulden of the galleries |
| 8 | **Rimewatch**, the watch on the frozen mere | the last autumn, on the first ice | the great hall burned on the mere with its lamps yet lit; Hesk carried his mother's lamp out lit, over ice *a night old* | Hesk of the mere |
| 9 | **Highreach**, the tower of the floating stones | late that same autumn | Lanner smelled the grey a day ahead; down the crag-stairs with the children | Lanner of the crags |
| 10 | **Glasspire**, the last and proudest | the first snows, in one night | *went down in one night into its own cellars, spire and glass together*; a shadow in the fog, or not | Pellow of the spire |
| — | **the Road** | the last month, into deep winter | the column turns up the mountain; on the night of the pass many children die, and some of the old, who had given the children their cloaks; Tarnel and Wendhessa among them | Stannard of the ridge, a quarryman of the Keep's own ridge |

On the Road, when Wendhessa's arms failed, the masons, who said the archive chest *held naught of worth*, gave it to the youngest, Tarnel and Seren, a handle each. After the night of the pass Seren carried it alone.

**The banner, the Fourteen Nights and the first month**

| Day | What | Tale |
|---|---|---|
| 0, dusk | the column comes in, *Two thousand and some hundreds, all told*, and Kael tells it head by head; Halyna turn the fallen capstone over in the gateway (*It was their own.*); the crying; the Captain climbs the wall with a coal from this hearth, tears his cloak down the back, writes the Title on the one half, binds it to a pike, and puts the other half back on; Kael reads it out; the Twelve answer, then the six companies, then the people | I.1 · II.1 |
| 0, the last light | the guild in the grass of the old ward; Halyna apart under the broken inner gate; the Captain comes down: *"Your walls are falling. Will you build, or will you watch and die?"*; Halyna's cry; the guild rises | I.2 |
| night 0 | the first fire on this hearth *in more years than any could tell*; Idrenna make the terms (*"Not the sword, but the stone."* / *"Not the killing, but the keeping."*); Orvenna split the siege day in three; the masons' chant; the first course laid in the gap of the inner gate by hissing torches; Enno and Della take the first voice of *Two at the Gate*; by morning the mortar has set | I.2 |
| the first week | the runners make their stone of a fallen block by the inner gate and swear on it, Wick last | I.3 |
| night 3 | *a light moved*: Rhyna knows where to look, Halvard goes down the shaft; the black chest, by Aldwena's mark | I.4 |
| day 4 | Halyna read the slates at the fire, in halves: the Rite, the law of the staff, the slates of building for battle | I.4 |
| days 4 to 13 | the cedar rendered; the children scrape the bloom; Rhyna warms Idrenna's myrrh into the oil; Stannard quarries the new capstone and Orvenna dress it; the empty flask on the hearthstone, and the Captain in off the wall | I.4 |
| day 14, by a thin sun | the Rite of the Cornerstone in the ward: Idrenna anoint, the staff, *Commander of Rivenkeep* | I.4 |
| night 14 | Halyna cut their mark and vow and the Title under the new capstone, Seren holding the lamp; the True Men raise it into the gate, mark down; the Captain leans the staff in the gatehouse corner; the True Men bear the old stone into the niche of the hearth-wall; Seren goes down the shaft alone and finds the youngest apprentice's leaf | I.4 · II.1 |
| night 15 | *The next night Kael stood, for he would not sit, and laid the first tale*; *none of us wist what to say*, and Old Ebba says the answer first | I.1 · the foreword |
| day 21 | the Naming, *the week after the Rite*: *"The Commander fought."* / *"The Stonewrights built."*; Rivenkeep named for all the land; hands on the stone under the pike until dark | I.5 |
| about day 24 | the first assault, *Nigh a month* after the sails first lay off the wall: one battery, Kael's; Hale's first holding; *"Hull hangeth back."*; *"Hold."*, then *"Loose."*; Voss hears a hull scream and understands the rhyme | I.3 · IV.5 |
| that evening | Hale walks the tide-line and carries *"Marks on the wood."*; the first glyph, *shore*; Jory laughs *"Wet planks"*; Enno and Della try it; Seren tries it alone and lets go | V.1 |
| about night 25 | Seren lays the second tale ever laid | I.2 |
| the end of the first month | Seren lays V.1 | V.1 |
| the second month of the first winter | Hale lays his tale | I.3 |
| the last weeks of the first winter | Jory dies on the east wall as a fire-ship comes in on the stones, Voss at his side; the first cairn on the east face; the first strip; that night Voss asks why we fight for ice and stone | IV.5 · V.5 Count I · I.5 |

**The year of the homecomings, and the years after**

| When | What |
|---|---|
| the end of the sixth winter | the ten Thrones come out of the grey as the last fleets go home, and sit down in the havens *like herons in the shallows*; *The wall named them Mystarchs, and the soldiers the Thrones.*; Kael lays Count VI; four nights before the going-down Old Ebba dies in her sleep at the fire |
| the eve of the going-down | Voss lays I.5: *Tomorrow we go down to the havens.* |
| the sixth spring to the sixth autumn | the havens retaken one at a time, in the order the Captain chooses (the player's order); after each fall one of Halyna goes among the timbers and learns the Throne's heart and name; each haven's True Man lays its homecoming at its own fire; Hesk dies at Rimewatch, and that night a fisher-girl lights the north lamp; *ere their chisels went into the grey*, Halyna cut Halvard's name in the lintel of the western stair |
| through those months | inside the heart of the Throne that will fall last, the green sliver grows in the dark: one season's wood, so it holds one word |
| the sixth autumn | the tenth Throne falls; the sliver is known: *"Home."*, *We remember our home too.*, and *Naelear* |
| the days after | on the morrow the boatwrights come down to the shingle; Tarnard lays the keel; Harl bears the first plank to his feet; the stem keyed by Orvenna, Penna holding the last key and Ormund striking it; Halyna's mark cut across the join by Seren's lamp; the bow lashing tied with Tarnel's knot; *A day it rode off the point in a hard swell, and took no water.*; at the evening tide the sending and the kneeling; VI.3 laid that night |
| the seventh winter | Halyna die in one night, Rhyna first in her sleep and Halvard *ere the lamp was out*; they lie under the new capstone; the last strip goes over the gate; the Captain goes into the winter in a plain grey cloak |
| the seventh spring | *on a morning of thin rain* the grey boat comes back with the Stone out of the Grey; *Ere that spring was out* the staff is gone from the gatehouse; from then the inner gate is left open every night |
| the fourth year after the homecomings | Seren writes the Epilogue (she is about twenty-one); that winter a child asks what the three grey stones say, and she reads the three words on the lowest and lays the Last Note, *last of all the leaves* |

**Optional: where the line of keeps stood.** I.5 says the Rivenmen raised keeps *in this war*, *like posts along a quay, a crimson pennant on every one*. If the game wants an order, the theaters' debut order gives one and changes no theater: the Keep's own shore above Eldhythe (Cam 1); the river-land of Tidesmeet (Cam 3); the fen of Fenholm (Cam 5); the woods of Holtward (Cam 7); the cairn-mountain of Carnhold (Cam 9); the burning shore of Emberhythe (Cam 11); the canyon of Sandreach (Cam 13); the frozen mere of Rimewatch (Cam 15, in winter); the crags of Highreach (Cam 17); the cellars of Glasspire (Cam 19). The havens themselves stay held by the grey sails until the Thrones come.

**Where the Reckoning was overtaken.** The Reckoning study (`wf9/reckoning.md`) came before the v2 outline, and the Books settled these differently: I.1 is laid on night fifteen, not fourteen; Halyna do not read Brenn in the first winter, for the bundle waits under Wendhessa's seal until the third (V.1's three words come from the quay's old tale); the rolls came in the archive chest, not in the masters' packs; the archive chest went to Seren and Tarnel on the Road, not to Seren alone; the homecomings fill one year; the Glasspire glass is not given as a cause of the sending. **And in v3:** the burning has the warden's reason, to hide the deed, as v1.3 had it, and not v2.0's custom of the coast.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| **W3** | the Tide of Remembering · *Ralenthae* | 15–22 | they look before they trust; the moved gun and the sounding shot; the hull that went home and came back to look | sentences, and the memory in them; the bundle opened; IV.2 whole; the gift held an hour; the Aelvaren's grief | IV.3 and IV.2 whole (winter); V.3 (deep winter); IV.4 (spring); IV.6 (summer); the foreword written (summer); Count III | Wick, in the Tide's second month |
| **W4** | the Joined Tide · *Aelthae* | 23–26 | the drums; three roads, one hour; a dead heart's hour kept; the orphaned hulls | the ties between hulls; the council; the growing | V.4 (winter); V.6 (spring); V.7 Part I (summer, the night Enrella died); Count IV | Enno and Della |
| **W5** | the Tide that Learned Deceit · *Rhenthae* (NOTES only) | 27–31 | loud, alone and straight, as the young wood came; straw down every road; hunters on the flashing; root-men down the silent roads | what a carving wants; the gift at its deepest; *Rhenear* | II.2 (winter); V.7 Part II (spring, the night Pellow died); Count V | Pellow; Ruan raised; Hale refuses |
| **W6** | the Last Tide · *Eirthae* (NOTES only), then the Thrones · *Naelsaen* | 32, then the Finale | morrows with turnings and a home clause; *Not scattered. Went.*; then ten great hulls that sit in the havens | a whole plan, with one young hand in it | V.7 Part III (winter); Count VI (the winter's end, as the Thrones came); I.5 (the eve of the going-down) | Old Ebba, four nights before the going-down |
| the sixth year | the Going-Down | the Finale | the ten Thrones, in the player's order | each Throne's heart and name; the sliver | VI.1 and VI.2, an entry a haven; VI.3 (the sixth autumn) | Hesk, at Rimewatch |

### 3.7 The cast

**The laws of the cast**

1. **A want, a habit, a line.** Everyone named has all three. The want is what a death cuts off or a homecoming pays. The habit is what the listener sees each time the person comes in. The line is what another mouth keeps after the death.
2. **Deaths are fixed points in the war.** None depends on how the game is played (§5.4).
3. **Halyna discover, Seren writes, and other hands do the work** (§3.9).
4. **The Captain is never named, never tells a tale, and lives.** He sits at the end of the bench with his hood up; *He will tell you it was not like that.* He puts his hood back twice in the whole Book: after IV.3, and in the Epilogue.
5. **No one sails in the grey fleet.** The ship is the commander. *No one saileth in us.*
6. **The warden's name is always ▒▒▒▒** in stone. In wood he never had one.
7. **No singular *they*** for anyone, and every pair-name takes a plural verb.

**The wall**

| Who | Want | Habit | Line | Fate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **the Captain** (on the wall, the Commander) | one whose place it was to lead; then to bring them all home, and to set the staff down | stands still while all else moves; *coal-black … on his fingers*, *mortar to the wrist*; wears the other half of the cloak and cuts it for the dead; hood up | the one question in I.2; of him, *there was no greater man* and *He will tell you it was not like that. It was like that.* | lives; a plain grey cloak after the seventh winter |
| **Kael the Counter**, oath-keeper of the True Men, of no haven | a count that comes out whole | counts, twice; stands to tell (*No, I will stand.*); swears | *A count is no count till it be named.* · *I swear to that.* · *I have no haven.* / *The Captain found me on a road.* | lives; counts the end |
| **Stannard of the ridge**, the quarryman | to cut stone for a house and not a wall | knocks a stone with his knuckle and listens; *No stone will he trust till he have knocked on it.* | *"It is not the ice I fight for."* · *"I know which of these will hold."* | lives; finds the cedar, quarries the new capstone, carries Enno the mile to Della |
| **Corlen of the harbour**, the eldest of the Twelve | to steer the first boat home into Eldhythe | wets a finger and holds it up before every holding | ***"Wait. The sea hath not finished."*** | dies late in the second winter (D6) |
| **Tamm of the three spans** | to set his three stones out on the bank where the spans stood | sets a stone from each span by his blanket every night | *One span is a plank. Three is a city.* | lives |
| **Marl the fen-born** | to lay his grandmother's stone on the old course | laughs at the sinking | *A town is but the last course on the old.* | lives; lays Ebba's stone, *It hath not sunk.* |
| **Aske of the canopy** | that no axe come into Holtward while he lives | lays his hand on every plank of wreck-wood before it goes on the fire | *These were trees once.* | lives; leaves the Root-Heart standing |
| **Garvel of the smithies** | to make one thing that is not a gun | listens to every gun *as a mother to a cough* | *Guns that ring true speak true.* | lives |
| **Harl of the burning shore** | his shore again, and the grey burned | *"Burn it."* of every plank; his temper, never a custom | *"Burn it."*, then *I did not burn it.* | lives; bears the first plank to Tarnard and walks the sending's bow into the tide |
| **Ulden of the galleries** | to sleep under a roof of stone again | sleeps on the open ground, with his ear to it | *Sandreach had no harbour. We called it a haven all the same, for it sheltered us.* | lives; *Tonight I sleep under mine own roof of stone.* |
| **Hesk of the mere** | to light the lamp on the ice again | lit his mother's lamp on the north wall every dusk; the night-watch steer by it | ***Someone must keep a light.*** | dies at his homecoming (D11) |
| **Lanner of the crags** | to climb the highest crag again and whistle for the wind | whistles for the wind; smells the grey a day off | *Before the sails cometh the wind.* | lives; his battery sinks the bearer of the three roads |
| **Pellow of the spire**, the youngest | to be the first eye on Glasspire come home | holds his glass up to everything, his supper among the rest; ground the far-glasses | ***"Look close."*** | dies in the fifth spring (D9) |
| **Voss who asked** | an answer of his own | keeps a question until it is answered, and circles back to it | *Ye may weigh it.* · *"We do not fight because we are right. We fight because our children are behind these walls."* · *But Mystwood remembers.* | lives; raised to Corlen's battery in the second winter; brings the first boat home |
| **Ruan of the spire** | — | Pellow's file-leader | *"Look close."* · *I have his glass. I looked close, and I looked for him.* | lives; raised a True Man by the Captain in the fifth spring |
| **Hale the runner** | to be fast enough, every time | says what he saw in the words he saw it | *I am not a teller. I run.* · ***I ran it.*** · *"I am not a True Man," Hale said. "I run."* | lives; refuses Pellow's glass and battery |
| **Wick**, the youngest runner | to carry a word to the Captain himself | runs everywhere, even to the hearth; sits at the front with the children, knees to his chin; swore the oath last, and again loudest | ***"They are looking."*** (his three words) | dies in the second month of the Tide of Remembering (D7) |
| **Jory of Eldhythe**, a soldier of the east wall | to drink again at Elspet's fire when the havens come home | whistles the quay-rhyme, *the tune and never the words*; turned the rope slower for the least of the children | ***"It is only a rhyme."*** | dies late in the first winter (D5) |
| **Old Ebba of Fenholm**, Marl's grandmother | to go home; to see her course-stone laid | begins the answer a breath before the rest; murmurs *"the Shorelands"* into her shawl | *"It is a name to come home to, when the last wall shall stand, and the last Mystarch lie broken."* · *"Perhaps."* | dies four nights before the going-down (D10) |
| **the fisher-girl of the east keep**, Ebba's great-granddaughter | to know where the green wood would go | keeps the north lamp after Hesk | *"Someone must keep a light."* · *"We remember."* (to the wood, first) | lives; first into the sea at the sending; the first hand on the boat that came back; *a child keepeth a light* |
| **Merrick**, eldest of the ship-masters, Daveth's son | never to see the grey boat | turns his face from the sea when a hull burns; bids Jory stop whistling; sits by the door at IV.5, *and he is not gone out* | *"Not that one."* | lives; watches the sending and does not look away; lays his hand on the grey boat |

| Who | Want | Habit | Line | Fate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Halyna** (Halvard Stone-Warden and Rhyna of the Inner Gate), the Bonded of the Keep | to find what the guild forgot, and to tell the wood's half so the tale is whole | one begins and the other ends; never a night under different roofs; Rhyna weeps for both | *"Hold the stone."* / *"Hold it, and it will hold thee."* · *"A tale told from one side of a wall,"* / *"is half a tale."* · *"So far can one people go alone: the gift, and the kneeling."* / *"The rest needeth the other."* | die in peace in the seventh winter (D12) |

| **Tarnel**, a boatwright's son of Tidesmeet | to fish the mountain tarns | knotted a net as he walked the passes | *"A net is only knots holding hands."* | died on the night of the pass (D3) |
| **Tarnard**, his father, the eldest of the boatwrights | — | lays his palm flat on a plank | *"Wood that knew the sea knoweth the way home."* | lives; lays the sending's keel |
| **Idrenna**, the eldest pair, the Theoliths | to seal one more pair | sit by the door and nod when a tale is laid true; speak slowly, in halves | *"Not the sword, but the stone."* / *"Not the killing, but the keeping."* · the prayer for the dead | live past the Book |
| **Orvenna** (Ormund and Penna), the wall-masters | a finished wall | never raise their voices; split the day as quarrymen split a block | *"Every gun ye haul is a course we lay not,"* / *"so spend a gun as a careful man spendeth silver."* | live; they sent Enrella apart, and keyed the sending's stem |
| **Enrella** (Enno and Della), the young pair | a house in the ward with one door | came up the mountain hand in hand; took the first voice of *Two at the Gate*; finish each other's jests | ***"It is like being one person"*** / ***"with four hands and no excuses."*** | die together in the fourth summer (D8) |
| **Wendhessa**, the archivists of Eldhythe | — | their silence; their hands on the chest's two handles | none: their silence is their line | died on the night of the pass (D3) |

| Who | Who they were | Fate |
|---|---|---|
| **Aelrhen, the Guest** | one of the long-lived; took the name, *bond-to-stone*, with his errand; gathered three words *as a child gathereth fallen leaves*; his last act was to kneel again, *there where the rite began* | beaten to death on the warden's step; cast into his boat (D1) |
| **the Aelvaren, the Bearer of the Binding** | the boat is a character: *proud of the name*; waited in the shallows *to bear him home with the rite made* | carried him home, burning, and rammed two boats on the way; its keel lies among the roots of the silver elders |

| **Tobe** | the old fisher of the twelve, thirty years at the edge | washed the poles; says nothing |
| **Elspet**, Voss's grandmother | twelve at the Summer of the Guest; kept the fire in her mother's harbour-house under the three grey stones; filled the cups of the three from the spar | *"The Mystlands make liars of honest men."*, which she said of her brother; died before the Fall |
| **Daveth**, her brother | an axeman of the felling; one of the three on the spar | drank on his tale and drowned within a few years; Merrick's father |
