| **the Last Carver** | fear first: his is the *one hand … young* of V.7 Part III, and his carving killed Pellow. Love after: he learned our letters, *Our cloak he never saw, for he never sailed. Our letters he learned.*, and cut them in stone so that one could read it alone; his one word too many answers the Guest's three | untold. The cutting stops mid-Title, its last stroke sharp yet |

*Every death is planted in at least two earlier places in play order, told whole in one place, answered by the hearth, and kept in someone else's mouth afterward. None is told before the listener has met what caused it.*

| **D11** | **Hesk of the mere** | at Rimewatch's homecoming | went out alone on the ice with his mother's lamp lit in full day, and stopped at two hundred and twelve steps, where the great hall had stood; at noon *the ice took the Long Winter down.* / *The ice took Hesk too.*; at dusk they found the lamp on the ice, burning | I.3 (Hale finds his way back by the lamp) · III.1 (*My mother kept those lamps.*; *On the ice I will light it again.*) · III.2 (*The ice held.*; *Someone must keep a light.*) · Count III (*Hesk's lamp burned yet*) · Count VI (his answer from the north wall) | **VI.1** (Kael, for Hesk) · VI.2 (Eirlenth: *no one roofed us*; *At the last a small fire stood over us*, a call) | the fisher-girl lights the north lamp that night: *"Someone must keep a light."*; in the Epilogue *a child keepeth a light*; *He came home.* | not said in the Book (§7.2 call 3) |
| **D12** | **Halyna** | the seventh winter | in one night: Rhyna first, in her sleep, and Halvard *ere the lamp was out, for he knew* | II.3 (the law; *we two fear it not*) · D8 (the law seen at work) · VI.3 (*"for we two have cut our last mark"*; his hand under her elbow) | **the Epilogue** | Seren says both halves of *"Hold the stone"* alone; Orvenna sing *Two at the Gate* at the grave, *Ormund to the comma and Penna the rest*; *Dry on my table stand their two inks.*; *The night I laid my first tale… Halyna laid my two hands on the stone.* | the last, over the gate: *It was the last of his half.*; the hood goes back |

**The rites of mourning,** the same every time so the listener learns them:
1. **The east cairns.** The ground on the east face *will not take a spade*, so the Keep's dead lie under cairns there.
2. **The Bonded lie under their work,** their mark beneath the stone, face down, and the wall standing on it: Enrella under the north stone, set by Orvenna's own four hands; Halyna under the new capstone of the inner gate.
3. **The strip.** Told whole once, at Jory: *That night the Captain cut a hand's breadth from his half of the cloak, and bound it on a pike over his cairn.* After that, a clause. The crimson on the cairns answers the crimson on the keeps.
4. **The hearth answers.** The hood stays up, except after IV.3 and in the Epilogue.
5. **Idrenna's prayer for the dead,** in two halves (V.7 Part I; told without its words at Ebba's grave).
6. **A kindness remembered,** at the cairn or the hearth: one line of the good of the dead, not only the death (§1.9).
7. **Kael names every name** at the last count: *The rest of the east cairns I named at dusk among the stones, every name, twice.*

**The strips**, which are the art hook's count: Jory (the east cairns), Corlen (the east cairns), Wick (the east cairns), Enrella (the north stone), Pellow (the east cairns), Ebba (the east face), Halyna (the inner gate), and Hesk (the mere), whose strip the Book no longer tells (§7.2 call 3). At Count VI, after the first five, *His half of the cloak hung to his knees the first winter.* / *Tonight it cannot reach his belt.* The five are named there, with the good of each, and *For each of them the Captain hath cut a hand's breadth, as poor men cut the last loaf, their own share thinnest.* The deaths told from before the banner (the Guest, Brenn, Wendhessa, Tarnel) and the troop dead under the east cairns take no strip.

**Others the Book mourns without a name:** the havens' dead; the children and the old who died on the night of the pass; the men under the east cairns (*Some of us looked not, and they are under the east cairns.*); the elders of the long-lived who pleaded and went down into the moss one by one; the children of the long-lived who lay down in the light when the Thrones drew up the last of the sky.

**What Halyna find** (each written into a tale by Seren):

|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | the fallen capstone, with Aldwena's mark and vow, the quarry-wardens' chisel-bite and the pale bloom | in the gateway at dusk; *They kneeled down in the snow and set their four hands under it.* | the banner | II.1 · the foreword |
| F2 | the black chest, by Aldwena's mark on its lid: the Rite on leaves of slate, the slates of building for battle, the founders' annal, the first staff, three stone flasks, dry | the foot of the shaft; *Rhyna wist where to look, and down the shaft went Halvard. She paid out the rope, and neither spake a word.* | the third night | I.4 |
| F3 | the oils, which they had to make | the cedars with Stannard; the bloom, with the children; the myrrh, from Idrenna's flask | the Fourteen Nights | I.4 |
| F4 | the rolls in the archive chest, with the letter copied among the cargo counts | read by night in the first year, to the bottom of the chest | the first year | III.1 · IV.1 |
| F5 | the first glyph, *shore* | the shingle, after Hale saw the marks; Rhyna's palms on the grain, Halvard's hands over hers; *"Shore," he said.* | the night of the first assault | V.1 |
| F6 | the two halves of the first heart, and every order whole | the vault, the two of them at its two ends | late in the second summer | V.2 |
| F7 | the Blackthorn heart: its shape, then the whole | the shingle (Rhyna), then the vault (Halvard) and among the children (Rhyna) | the second summer; the third winter | IV.2 |
| F8 | the seal broken: Brenn's words wrapped round the gift | the archive chest, once the wood showed them what the mute stones were, with Idrenna's leave | the third winter | IV.3 · the foreword |
| F9 | the gift, held an hour, and later at its deepest | Rhyna at the hearth while Halvard, at the mortar, began the telling; then many nights, *one at the hearth, the other asleep* | the third spring; the fifth winter | IV.4 · II.2 |
| F10 | the hull that went home; the Aelvaren's grief; the council; the growing | the shingle and the vault, turn about | the third and fourth years | V.3 · IV.6 · V.4 · V.6 |
| F11 | the three late carvings | the shingle, by Seren's lamp | the fourth to sixth years | V.7 |
| F12 | the Thrones' hearts and names | *one of Halyna went among the timbers, and the other stood at the edge with the soldiers* | the sixth year | VI.2 |
| F13 | the green sliver | a crack of the last Throne's heart: *All the same they put out a hand each, and touched it in the same moment.* | the sixth autumn | VI.3 |

**Not Halyna's:** Hale sees the marks on the wood (V.1). Seren finds the youngest apprentice's leaf (I.4), lays the two raw edges together (V.2), reads the three words on the lowest stone by eye (the Last Note), and knows the returned boat by its stem and the chisel by its bite (the Epilogue). Voss finds the three stones over his grandmother's cold hearth (VI.1). The children reach the grey boat first, and the fisher-girl lays the first hand on it (the Epilogue).

---

## 4 · THE PUZZLE PIECES

*None of these is explained in the Book. Each sits in two or more tales and waits for the listener to see it. "→" means paid off in; "←" means seeded in.*

### 4.1 The motif that tests every lock

**A half waiting for its other half.** The bond makes two into one. The Guest's rite was left half made. The cloak was torn down the back. The first heart split into two planks. The warden's arm bore *the half of something, cut off in the middle*. The Last Carver stopped his Title in the middle of a line. *Halyna* is one name made of two, and their lines come in halves. Every facing spread pairs a stone tale with a wood tale: *"A tale told from one side of a wall,"* / *"is half a tale."* And at the kneeling: *"So far can one people go alone: the gift, and the kneeling."* / *"The rest needeth the other."* (VI.3). A piece is locked when its other half turns up somewhere else in the Book.

### 4.2 The great locks, seed to payoff

**The tale-stone and the bloom.** The old capstone of the inner gate fell in some winter that none told, with its vow to the earth (the foreword, II.1). Halyna turn it at dusk on the day of the banner, *a stone that two must turn*, and find their own lines in it: Aldwena's mark (Rhyna's forebears) and the wardens' chisel-bite (Halvard's fathers), with the bloom grown *as pale as frost on a gatepost* on its upper face (II.1). The bloom becomes the second oil (I.4). The stone becomes the tale-stone in its niche, under every teller's hands, *Turn it I cannot*, for one alone cannot (II.1).

**The Stonwryt, each pair's sacred mark.**

| Mark | Seen | Paid |
|---|---|---|
| **Aldwena's** | the foreword (on the tale-stone, drawn, in the Hal) · II.1 (*Under it we cut our mark, and our vow beside it*) | **I.4: the same mark on the lid of the black chest**: *It was the mark of Aldwena.* The cradle-rhyme of Aldwena's line (*the first two keep what the first two know*) is why Rhyna wist where to look |
| **Halyna's** | **II.3: drawn once in the hearth-ash for the children, then swept away** (*Ash is for the wind. A mark is for stone, and stone will hold.*) | I.4 (cut under the new capstone, unseen: *Their mark is not mine to draw.*) · **VI.3: cut across the join of the stem**, *Never ere then had a mark of ours gone into wood.* / *The wood took it.* · **the Epilogue: on the boat come home, and face down above their grave** |
| **Enrella's** | II.3 (to be cut under the first stone of their house of one door, *for they could not wait for the last*) · V.7 Part I (*Their mark lay under its first stone. Enno had set the chisel, and Della struck.*) | **V.7 Part I: under the north stone, the only mark in the annals that one hand finished.** Seren looks for Della's lone stroke: *Ye cannot tell.* |
| **Wendhessa's** | the foreword · V.1 (*a bundle under the seal of Wendhessa that no pair would break*) | **IV.3: the seal on Brenn's bundle, drawn as it was broken**: a mark that marks a breaking |

**The black chest.** It holds the Rite on leaves of slate (its first line names itself; lower, the law of the staff); the slates of building for battle (*Walls sloped to turn a shot. Gates set crooked against the ram. Courses laid that a breach should fall outward.*); the founders' annal in Aldwena's *we two*; the first staff along its floor; three stone flasks, dry; and at the bottom, *one leaf more*, the youngest apprentice's, in letters *large and careful, as a child's are, and as mine are yet* (I.4).
- The founders laid it in order when they went down, *as masons lay by their tools at dusk*. So the Keep was not stripped and abandoned: it was set in order and left, then forgotten around its own chest, the second meaning of *It had never fallen. It had only been left.* (II.1).
- *The first builders had known war. Against what, the slates say not.* Never answered, and never tied to the parting (J25).
- The battle-slates pay off in Count I: *A breach on the south face fell outward, as the slates of the black chest said, and all behind it lived.*
- The apprentice's leaf pays the foreword's hearth (*At this hearth the first builders of the Keep laid their tales.*): *So this hearth is older than the havens. We made it not. We forgat it, and found it again at the bottom of a chest.*
- The same vault later holds the enemy's words, the planks (V.2: *the oldest room under this hall, where the Rite was found*).

**The oils** (J9; §2.2). Cedar answers the felling by not felling (*A living tree would they not fell.*). The bloom is the forgetting turned to strength. The myrrh of the road's dead ties the anointing to Tarnel and Wendhessa: *I wist for whom it had been carried.*

**The staff** (J10; §2.2). Seed: the law, read off the slate in halves (I.4). Middle: the ten havens' staffs left in their falling walls (III.2: *A staff is cut for its work, and leaveth not its work.*). The Captain leans the first in the gatehouse corner, and stands at the Naming *and the staff in its corner* (I.5). Payoff: *If you doubt the tale, go and look at the dust on it.* (I.4), turned in the Epilogue to the clean stroke where it stood, *If you doubt the tale, go and look at the dust. The work is done. I know the stair that goes no more.*, and *I have not gone down to look.* The cradle-rhyme's *under the stair that goes no more* is where the first two kept what they knew, and where the work's staff is laid down.

**The runners and Brenn** (J7, J19). *Every haven had its runners' stone.* Hale keeps the law; Brenn broke it. The oath is sworn in I.3, by Brenn at fifteen *with mine hand flat on the runners' stone*, and again after IV.3 with *Not as Brenn carried.* The Guest's three words were carried by no one; Wick's three words were carried, and *His word came to the Captain, as he had wanted all the war.* Brenn says the oath over every mile and *I ran it*, as Hale does, and the Book never points it out.

**The torn cloak and the half-cloak.**
- The Title is written in coal from this hearth on the torn cloak; the written half is the banner, the other half the Captain puts back on (I.1). *We remember* comes out of the Title's first words.
- **The strips** (§3.8): from the first (*It was the first strip.* / *It was not the last.*) to the last (*It was the last of his half.*), and then the plain grey cloak.
- The Title is cut in stone twice: once by Halyna, hidden under the new capstone in the Captain's own strokes (*It was the Captain's hand, large and slow.* / *Neither of them had mended one stroke.*); once by the Last Carver, sent across the sea and stopped halfway.
- The Stone's grain names it from the other side: *He tore his sail, the red one, and set it on the stone.*

**The archive chest, the sealed bundle, the rolls and the letter.**
- **One name: the archive chest**, the guild's plain chest out of Eldhythe, never the black chest. Wendhessa bore it out at the Fall, *his hand on one handle, hers on the other*; on the Road *their arms failed*, and *The masons said it held naught of worth, and gave it to the youngest, Tarnel and me.* After the night of the pass *she carried it alone*. The foreword: *Up the mountain I carried two things, our tongue and a chest; and only the chest was heavy.*
- What is in it, and when each comes out:

| In the chest | Found | Tale |
|---|---|---|
| the rolls of the havens: their raising, their staffs | the first autumn | III.1 (*Halyna had found the rolls in the chest I carried.*) |
| the Harvest rolls: the cargo count, the clerk's line, the warning, the stained lines | read through the first year, to the bottom of the chest on a night of thaw | IV.1 |
| **the letter, copied by a clerk between a count of poles and a count of oars, the name cut out ▒▒▒▒** | the same nights, read and copied and not understood (*What I held, I wist not.*) | IV.1, then IV.5 (*who will argue with writing?*), then IV.3 |
| the mute stones | seen from the first; *Some of those lie in the chest at my knee*; understood after IV.2 | IV.1 · IV.2 · the Last Note |
| **a bundle under Wendhessa's seal**, which no pair will break | seen from the first night | the foreword · V.1 · IV.3 |
| inside it, **Brenn's words wrapped round the gift** | the third winter: *No pair would break that seal till the wood showed Halyna what the mute stones beside it were. Then Idrenna gave leave.* | IV.3, then IV.4, then II.2 |

- **Who cut the name.** *The guild buried him under his name. The warden's name it cut away.* IV.1 shows the blot, and Rhyna lays her finger on it and takes it away; IV.3 says why.
- **The letter swallowed the rumour.** When the three from the spar swore to a burning boat, the Council already had it in writing that a hostile vessel had been burned and released, and the shore laughed: *I laughed not, for I had seen it turn. Yet I said naught. The Council had it in writing.* (IV.3) · *And who will argue with writing?* (IV.5). And in the annal, coldly: *The Council kept the letter.*
- **At the end** the chest holds the Book, among the mute stones, by the Guest's gift and the Stone out of the Grey (the Epilogue).

**The burning, and the fear of grey boats** (K8; §1.8). The burning is the warden's order, to hide the deed: *"Burn it," said he, "and shove it off, and give it the tide. Leave naught to show."* The children's rhyme keeps his words without knowing it. The irony is never said: the burning boat carried the whole deed home in its boards, so the cover-up is what told the council (IV.6: *Wood can hide naught.*). The coast's fear of grey boats comes only from the men who saw it: three from a spar, who told it all that autumn and never set foot in a boat again, and the outpost's men in their cups. It runs through the Book as plain fear:

| Tale | The fear |
|---|---|
| I.3 | Jory whistles a tune *that the old ship-masters hate*, and Merrick bids him stop |
| IV.1 | *After the year of the burning boat no crew would row within sight of the grey, and the rolls say not why.* |
| IV.5 | the three ship-masters' sayings; *Let the shore laugh, for the shore will never have to meet it.*; *I have seen one leave his cup and go out into the rain sooner than hear it.* |
| V.1 | *The old sailors on the wall would not go nigh it.* |
| VI.3 | *Six winters had they turned their faces from the sea when a hull burned.* / *They did not look away.* |
| the Epilogue | *The old ship-masters stood at the head of the shingle, and came no further.*; the children go down; *Merrick laid his hand on its gunwale.* |

**The ramming, the rumour, and the end of the axes** (J21, J22). The cruellest piece in the Book, which Seren never remarks on: **the Guest came to ask that the felling stop; his burning boat's fury helped stop it; and in the same summers his people voted for war.** Seeds: IV.1 (*lost at the edge*; *no crew would row within sight of the grey*), IV.5 (two boats; twenty), IV.6 (*twice*), V.4 (*In those same summers the iron came no more to the edge. The grove had not waited to see it.*). The rhyme's two unsung lines have their first dead: *and the one it comes for will not come home again*; and Merrick, who took the rope, is Daveth's son.

**The mute stones, and the three over the fire.**
- The wood asks and the shore answers. IV.2: *With the timber the shore-men took the stones away, and whither they went we wist not.* IV.1: *Some hung in the harbour-houses, reduced to ornament. Men drank under them, and did their reckoning. The rest the guild locked in its archives* (*It was we who turned the key.*). IV.5: three stones over Elspet's fire, *with letters fine as the tracks of birds on wet sand*, and *The ship-masters knocked out their pipes on the lowest one.*
- They come home: at Eldhythe Voss finds them *yet in the wall* over a cold hearth, *and the lowest scarred where the ship-masters knocked their pipes*, and carries them up (VI.1: *Long enough have they hung over a dead fire.*). In the Last Note they hang over the Long Hearth: *Here no pipe is knocked on them.*
- **The three words** (the Last Note): on the lowest stone, *the sounds close and the symbols wrong*, Seren finds three words of ours cut by their sound, *Stop… Sky… Dying…*, cut at the pillars' feet before the Guest came to say them at the door. She reads them by eye, a stone skill and hers alone; *three words of ours I had from Brenn*; and she says *I think*. It is the last thing she learns.

**The planks, and the two halves of one heart.**
- Seed (V.1): *One long edge was raw, split down the grain, as if the rest had been torn away.*
- V.2: the silent plank gave naught, for half a heart holds half a knowing; held at the two ends of the vault at once, the two halves wake every order. At full light Seren lays them *raw edge to raw edge*: *The broken grain met.* / *It met as the halves of a split log meet. I have not asked Halyna what it meaneth, and they have not told me.*
- VI.3: the planks of the vault become the sending boat, and the two halves, keyed by Orvenna, are its stem: *The broken grain met, and held.*
- The Epilogue: *By its stem I knew the boat: the first heart keyed, and Halyna's mark across the join.*
- The Emberhythe plank, *Burn*, stays out of the boat: the mason lays it on the black crust at Emberhythe's homecoming, and Harl *did not burn it* (VI.1; the Book of Knowings).

**The green sliver.** The first growth any pillar has put up since the iron came: a shoot of the eldest wood, grown in the dark of the last Throne's heart once its war was done, *as seedlings live in the crack of a wall*. It is one season's wood, so it holds one word, as the Song says. Three leaves promise it, each in its own place: *No growth had we put up since the iron. We were waiting for the sky to come again.* (VI.2) · *The iron will tire; and when it stoppeth, the wood will come again.* (IV.2) · *Wait with them, and when the iron stoppeth, they will grow the sky back.* (V.4). VI.3 no longer quotes them. Seren says it in her own words, and lets the reader join them: *Since the iron came, not a leaf had the pillars put up. They had waited for the sky to come again.* / *So it was seed.* The sending carries the seed of their sky home: the turn of grace, earned by tales already read.

**The sending boat.** Built of the vault's planks: *Every one had spoken to us once, and now they would speak for us.* Tarnard lays the keel himself, and over the first plank, which Harl bore out of the vault and set at his feet, he says *"Wood that knew the sea knoweth the way home."* (IV.6's own four words). Orvenna key the stem; Halyna cut their mark across the join by Seren's lamp, *and it burned down a finger's width*; Seren ties the bow lashing with Tarnel's knot, *a knot to a mile*, and Tarnard says his son's saying. It carries the sliver on its moss, a Stonwryt coin (*It was our dearest gift.*, answering IV.4's *the dearest is a sliver of such wood*), and Halyna's two chisels, which *in the bow … took the last of the light*, as in the courtyard (I.2). The whole hearth kneels; Harl walks the bow into the tide; the fisher-children push it off; *Well out on the ebb, the boat turned. Into the wind it went, and against the current, and into the grey.*: IV.3's sentence without *burning*.

**The Stone out of the Grey, and the boat that came back.** In the seventh spring the same boat comes in, known by its stem and Halyna's mark. In its bow, where the sliver and the chisels lay, is Myststone, *stone that had been wood, like the Guest's gift*, which *the Rivenmen … say … remembereth all*. Seren knows the chisel's bite: *Twice had I held the lamp for that chisel, under the new capstone and across this stem.* The Last Carver learned our letters (*Our cloak he never saw, for he never sailed. Our letters he learned.*) and cut *In the memory of our home, our families...*, with one word our Title never had, *the*, before *memory*. *I read it as it was cut, and no child laughed.* The cutting stops mid-line, *and its last stroke is sharp yet*. His fate stays untold. *We have not yet earned a guest.* · *What we will send back, I know not. I know only that it must be sent kneeling.*

**The answer, from first to last.** Ebba says *We remember* first, the first night the hearth asked a tale (the foreword); she begins it a breath early most nights, and after IV.3 *Old Ebba began it, and the rest were slow to follow*; she dies, and the hearth waits a breath for her (I.5; Fenholm's fire, VI.1); after VI.3 the hearth waits *the width of one old voice*, and her great-granddaughter, the fisher-girl who asked whither the green wood would go and went foremost into the sea, says it to the wood (VI.3); in the Epilogue the first child to lay her hands on the boat that came back is *the same child*, and she keeps the north light.

### 4.3 The smaller locks

*Keep them, and explain none.*

| Piece | Seed | Payoff |
|---|---|---|
| twelve and a warden | IV.3: *Twelve were we, and the warden* | I.1: *Twelve. I counted us twice, and it took not long.* The outpost's twelve obeyed; the Captain's Twelve keep their oaths |
| the laughter | IV.3: *Then laughed his men. I stood among them, and I hear that laughter yet.* · IV.3: *the shore laughed at three axemen* | Count I: *We laughed. God forgive us, we laughed.* · Count V: *we laughed as we laughed the first winter* · V.7 II: *Then laughed the whole wall* · IV.5: *The shore laughed at the three from the spar.* · the Epilogue: *I read it as it was cut, and no child laughed.* · the Last Note: *Under them the shore laughed* |
| two wardens | the quarry-wardens' names cut **into** the lintel of the western stair (I.1's oath; II.1) | the outpost warden's name cut **out** (IV.3) · Halvard's name cut in last, by Halyna (the Epilogue) |
| where no eye will see it | the founders: *where no eye will see it again till the wall fall* (II.1) | Halvard of their own mark (II.3) · the Epilogue, over the grave |
| the smooth stone under the last name | II.1: *beneath the last the stone is smooth* | the Epilogue: *Beneath it the stone is smooth, and none of his line is left to cut it.* |
| the open door | III.2: the havens' gates *were wide, and stood open at night* | IV.3: *We were a people of doors.* · the Stone's grain: *The open door.* · the Epilogue's last picture: *Every night is the inner gate left open, as a guest would find it* |
| came no further | IV.3: *▒▒▒▒ stood within, and came no further.* | the Epilogue: *The old ship-masters stood at the head of the shingle, and came no further.*, and the children walk past |
| leave naught | IV.3: *Leave naught to show.* | IV.6: *Wood can hide naught.* |
| kneeling | IV.3, IV.4: the Guest kneels again | VI.3: the whole hearth kneels · the Epilogue: *it must be sent kneeling* |
| harm to one is harm to both | II.3: *The old masters had a saying for it. Harm to one is harm to both.* | IV.4: *Never were the two arms laid together, shoulder to shoulder, that harm to the one should be harm to both.* |
| *ael* | IV.4 (*Aelrhen … bond-to-stone*) · IV.6 (*Aelvaren … the Bearer of the Binding*) · V.5's head, *The Joined Tide* | V.7 Part I: *That night I knew its first half. Ael I had written before, in Ael'thar.* Unglossed; the kinship stays a hint |
| the wrong fortress | Count I: *Every night it went round the benches with the cup, and every man drank to it.* | V.2 opens on it, and turns: *They had not picked anything.* / *They had been sent.* |
| Kael's number | Count VI: *I know their number, and I will not tell it at this hearth.* | V.6: *We are crewed by the living, and the living are the wood.* He counted the wood's living dead; he names ours instead |
| *Look close* | Pellow (III.1; V.7 II) | Kael (Count V) · Ruan (V.7 II; VI.1) |
| the Glasspire shadow | III.2: some saw it; Pellow, who was there, *I looked close. I saw nothing.* | VI.2: *No throne sailed the night the spire fell. Of that night the grain hath naught.* Unsolved for ever |
| the little net | I.2 (*a boy of the guild, that knotted a net as he went*) · II.3 (the knot; *with his knot is this Book bound*) · IV.3's annal (*I watched the knots.*) | VI.3: the bow lashing; Tarnard's *"A net is only knots holding hands."* |
| the silence ends with Seren | IV.3: *Wendhessa sealed his words, and told no one.* | the foreword: she tells everyone |
| waiting in the white | Hesk's lamps; the hall's lamps on the ice (III.1, III.2) | VI.2, Eirlenth: *We waited in the white a long while, and no one roofed us.* |
| a new course on the old | III.1 (Fenholm) · III.2 (Ebba's stone carried out) | VI.1: *A town is but the last course on the old, we used to say. Tonight we said it again, and laughed.*; *It hath not sunk.* |
| Glasspire's glass | the far-glasses; the great glass (III.1) | VI.2, Neivaere: *Where it fell, the breath burned away.* · VI.1: *every piece of it showed us our own lamps* |
| sent home | Count VI: *It cannot be called home. But it can be sent home.* | VI.3: sent home, kneeling |
| a morrow they did not carve for | V.7 Part III | VI.3: the sending is that morrow |
| *one hand … young* | V.7 Part III | the Epilogue: *young by their reckoning, I think, and seasoned by ours* |
| *Rhenear*, the stone-deaf | V.7 Part II | the Epilogue: answered in stone, *cut so that one could read it alone* |
| *Stone does not translate* | IV.1 | the Epilogue: *But this was never only stone.* |
| three words with no small words | IV.3 · IV.4 (*Ye join your words with small words, and small words make no sound across water.*) · V.1 | the Last Carver's one small word too many |
| the coal from this hearth | I.1 | *We remember* comes from the Title written with it |
| the Covenant of Completion | II.3: *Ye will understand it when it is yours to understand.* · *God taketh not the one without leaving the other a work to do.* | the Epilogue: *Now I understand the Covenant.* · *This is mine.* |
| *The Song of the Years* | V.6: *The green wood holds one word.* | V.1 *shore* · VI.3 *home* |
| the rhyme comes true | IV.5: every line of *The Grey Boat* | IV.3 · IV.4 · IV.6 |
| two at the gate | I.2: Enno and Della take the first voice; Seren *sang to the comma, and then … the rest* | V.7 I: *No one took the first voice, before the comma or after it.* · the Epilogue: sung at Halyna's grave, *Ormund to the comma and Penna the rest* |
| *Hold the stone* | I.2: *Tonight I was afraid to speak, and Halyna laid my two hands on the stone.* | the Epilogue: *There is none now to finish it, and I say both halves alone.* |
| the sea has not finished | Corlen (III.1; III.2: *and the sea had not finished*) | Voss at the guns (Count II) and at Eldhythe (VI.1: *Then I waited, as he would, till the sea had finished.*) · the Epilogue: *The sea hath not finished.* |
| the north lamp | Hesk lights it every dusk (I.3, III.1, III.2, Count III, Count VI) | the fisher-girl lights it (VI.1) · the Epilogue: *on the north wall a child keepeth a light* |
| Merrick | turns his face from the sea (I.3, IV.5) | does not look away (VI.3) · lays his hand on the grey boat (the Epilogue) |
| Tamm's three stones | by his blanket every night (III.1, III.2) | set out on the bank where the middle span stood (VI.1) |
| *Burn it* | Harl (III.1, III.2, V.1) | *I did not burn it.* (VI.1) · the first plank at Tarnard's feet (VI.3) |
| *These were trees once* | Aske (III.1) | the Root-Heart left standing: *It had come home.* (VI.1) |
| the fire that no one mended | I.2: the first fire *in more years than any could tell* · I.5: *In the night the fire burned down, and no one mended it.* | VI.3: *The fire burned down, and none rose to lay wood on it.* |
| *Hold them, for they are ours* | IV.1 (*Hear it now, and hold it, for it is ours.*) · III.1 · V.5 | VI.1: *for they are ours again* · VI.2: *Our own names we had. Hold them, for they were ours.* |
| the lamp, a finger's width | V.1: *The lamp burned down a finger's width.* | VI.3, at the mark · the Last Note: *the lamp burned down a finger's width while I looked* |
| the lamp filled twice | V.7 Part III: *Twice I filled the lamp ere they had told it all* | *The lamp went out on the shingle, and I filled it not a third time.* |
| the tune and never the words | V.1 (Jory) | IV.5: *All that first winter Jory whistled it on the east wall, the tune and never the words.* |
| into the wind, against the current | IV.3 (*burning*) · IV.5 · IV.6 (*Into the wind we went, and against the current, burning.*) | VI.3: the same sentence, without *burning* |
| the chisels and the last light | I.2: *Their two chisels took the last of the light.* | VI.3: *in the bow the chisels took the last of the light* · the Epilogue: *I knew the bite.* |
| turning by turning | I.4: the light going down the shaft; Seren going down after it | the Epilogue: *I know the stair that goes no more.* |
| the dearest gift | IV.4: *Of all the gifts the long-lived give, the dearest is a sliver of such wood.* | VI.3: the coin, *It was our dearest gift.* |
| the dead teller | the foreword: *One of its tellers, that held up a wet finger to every wind, is under the east cairns already.* | III.1 (Corlen's finger) · Count II (his death) |

### 4.4 The lock matrix, tale by tale

| Tale | It seeds | It pays |
|---|---|---|
| **Of This Book** | the tale-stone and Aldwena's mark (→ II.1, I.4) · the chest at her feet and the dark stone under Wendhessa's seal (→ IV.3, IV.4, the Epilogue) · the dead teller with the wet finger (→ III.1, Count II) · *We remember*, chosen (→ III.1, VI.3) · the bare first leaf (→ the Epilogue) · *I write alone* (→ II.3, the Epilogue) · the lintel over the name (→ every tale) · *Ye cannot tell.* (→ V.7 I) · the saying on rolls and wood (→ IV.4, V.5, V.7 II) · the Book in granite (open) | — |
| **Invocation** | the Title · *for once we forgat* · *we have kept both* (→ IV.5 and IV.6, III.2 and VI.2) | — |
| **I.1** | the coal from this hearth · the half-cloak (→ every strip, the Epilogue) · the crimson (→ I.5) · the roll of the Twelve (→ III.1, III.2, VI.1) · the lintel of the wardens (→ II.1, IV.3, the Epilogue) · *A count is no count till it be named* (→ Count VI) · *I counted us twice, and it took not long* (→ VI.1) · *I turned to the ward and read it out* (→ the Epilogue) · the facing rings (→ the Epilogue) | ← the foreword (the hearth asked a tale) · ← the Epilogue (the Stone's grain tells I.1 from the other side) |
| **I.2** | *a boy of the guild* (→ II.3, IV.3, VI.3) · Halyna's two chisels (→ VI.3, the Epilogue) · Idrenna's halves (→ I.4, II.3) · Enno and Della hand in hand (→ II.3, V.7 I) · *Two at the Gate* (→ V.7 I, the Epilogue) · *I came into that courtyard alone* (→ V.7 I) · Seren's two hands on the stone (→ the Epilogue) | ← II.1 (the capstone turned before the cry) |
| **I.3** | the runners' oath and stone (→ IV.3, Count III) · the late hull (→ V.2, *Wait in grey*) · the Captain's voice at the battery (→ Count II, IV; V.7 II) · Jory and Merrick (→ IV.5, I.5) · Wick (→ IV.3, Count III) · Hesk's lamp (→ III.1, Count III, VI.1) · Pellow's far-glass (→ III.1, V.7 II) · *Kael's guns, that are yet all we have* (→ V.5, V.7 II) | ← V.1 (*"Marks on the wood."*) |

### 4.5 The fuses that must burn in play order

All of these run seed before payoff in the play order of §5.3.
- **The letter:** IV.1 (Cam 10) → IV.5 (Cam 12) → IV.3 (Cam 15).
- **The rumour before the truth:** IV.5 (Cam 12) → IV.3 (Cam 15) → IV.4 (Cam 18) → IV.6 (Cam 20).
- **The bundle opened:** the foreword says at first launch that the seal was broken once the wood showed Halyna what the mute stones were, so the delay is foretold; IV.2's reading (from Cam 11; fallback Cam 14) shows the mute thing's plea before IV.3 (Cam 15).
- **The halves:** V.1 (Cam 1) → V.2 (Cam 13) → VI.3 (the Finale) → the Epilogue.
- **The marks:** the foreword (first launch) → I.4 (Cam 2) → II.1 (Cam 3) → II.3 (Cam 4, Halyna's) → V.7 I (Cam 25, Enrella's) → VI.3 → the Epilogue. Aldwena's mark is seen twice before it is explained, which is fine for lore; *after, never before* binds fleet behaviours, not lore.
- **The growth:** VI.2 (each Throne), IV.2 (Cam 11 and 16) and V.4 (Cam 23) → VI.3.
- **The deaths:** every death's plants (§3.8) come before the leaf that tells it. Two plants were checked against play order. Jory is alive in V.1 (Cam 1) and I.3 (from Cam 2), dies in Cam 2, and is told in Count I (Cam 6), IV.5 (Cam 12) and I.5 (Cam 32). The foreword's dead teller *that held up a wet finger to every wind* is read at first launch, but no player can know whose finger it is until III.1 (Cam 5) teaches Corlen's habit; between Cam 5 and his death at Cam 9 a player who rereads the foreword can guess (§7.2 call 17).
- **Corlen's last tale:** III.2 (Cam 8) → his death (Cam 9) → Count II (Cam 14).
- **The answer's heir:** the foreword and Ebba's habit (first launch onward) → I.5 (Cam 32) → VI.3 (all ten fallen) → the Epilogue.
- **The Mystarchs:** Count VI (Cam 32, last battle) names the Thrones *Mystarchs* before I.5 (Cam 32 complete) has Ebba say *the last Mystarch*.

### 4.6 The facing spreads, and the silent seams

Stone on the left, wood on the right, two halves of one event. Halyna wish it so: *Halyna would have the wood's leaf on the right, facing ours.* (the foreword). The Contents names each spread.

| Stone | Wood | The seam, unsaid |
|---|---|---|
| II.1 Of the Keep That Was Forgotten | II.2 Of the Breath of the Wood | both are tales of home |
| IV.1 Of the Felling | IV.2 The Axe in the Grain | the rolls count the cargo; the wood counts the strokes |
| IV.3 The Warden's Door | IV.4 The Gift Held an Hour | Brenn watched the deed; the wood held it by blood. *Fear leaveth no mark on wood* answers what Brenn could only see |
| IV.5 The Burning Boat | IV.6 The Voyage of the Aelvaren | *There were two boats.* / *Twice on that road*; the one turn of the seam spoken aloud is IV.6's headnote: *Of the long-lived I say what the wood cannot: They did not know it was ignorance.* |
| V.2 The Night of the Naming | V.3 The Wreck That Went Home | the soldiers' names for the orders, and one hull's memory under them |
| Seren's empty leaf | V.4 The Council Under the Thin Sky | the one reversal: the wood on the left, this once, and *Here there is no tale of ours. We were not there, and we did not know that anyone was deciding anything.* |
| V.5 The Tides, as the Wall Counted Them | V.6 Of the Growing | the wall's count of the Tides, and the wood's count of the growings |
| VI.1 The Homecomings | VI.2 The Thrones That Walk the Sea | each haven's homecoming, and the Throne that sat in it; *hold them, for they are ours again* / *Hold them, for they were ours.* |
| I.1 The Torn Cloak | the Epilogue, bound facing it | the first spread's empty rings, filled at the very end |

**What changed in v3.** Seren no longer speaks the seams. v2.0's lines (*The wood says two. The ship-masters say twenty. I have kept both.* and its kind) are cut, for they decoded the facing leaf (craft rule 10; voice §4). The foreword's saying, *Our rolls forget, and can be made to lie. The wood cannot lie, but it cannot see fear.*, stands once above all of them, and the Invocation says *Where the two agree not, we have kept both* every night. Every stone leaf of a spread is named *facing* its wood leaf in the Contents; no headnote names it.

## 5 · HOW THE LEGENDS ENTER THE GAME

*For the integrator. Nothing in this section touches the simulation: every surface named here is cosmetic and sits off the sim path. Where it names a design document, it says "the fleet design (admiral doc)"; there is no admiral in the fiction. The laws and the schedule are v2.0's; what v3 changed is marked.*

### 5.0 Where the old delivery map's sections went

v1.3 closed with "How the Legends Enter the Game". Its sections live here. Links from other documents into it, or into Notes v1.0.0 §4, should move as follows.

| v1.3 section | Notes v1.0.0 | Here |

| §1 The standing laws | §4.1 | §5.1 |
| §2 The prologue | §4.2 | §5.2 |

| §4.2 The script-unlock ladder | §4.7 | §5.7 |
| §8 Other surfaces: the reader | §4.8 | §5.8 |
| §5 The Whispers | §4.9 | §5.9 |
| §6 Battle messages (6.1 to 6.4) | §4.10 | §5.10 |
| §7 Command Time in the fiction | §4.11 | §5.11 |
| §8 Other surfaces: personas and art | §4.12 | §5.12 |
| — | §4.13 The builder's markers | §5.13 |

### 5.1 The standing laws

*Laws 1 to 7 are v1.3's, laws 8 to 12 v2.0's. Law 12 is restated for v3, and law 13 is new.*

1. **Cosmetic, off the sim path.** No leaf, Whisper or message changes a number, a spawn or a behaviour. Every unlock is keyed to a campaign milestone or to an event the sim has already recorded, so two players who play exactly the same way open exactly the same leaves at exactly the same moments.
2. **Low-load moments only.** Lore appears at first launch, in the debrief, in the interstitial between campaigns, and at battle load. It never appears during timed Build or a live Fight. Two fleet surfaces are not lore and follow the fleet design instead: the between-sortie dispatch (§5.12), and, from the Tide that Learned Deceit, the root sign a sunk hull of the running carving floats for the rest of its sortie (a drawn glyph with no words; admiral doc §9b). The root sign is Seren's eye-work: *Their shapes I learned by eye, at the tide-line and in the vault*, and only the rest of a carving must be known.
3. **After, never before.** A tale about a fleet behaviour unlocks only after the player has met that behaviour. The Knowing's law (it explains this battle and never hints at the next) binds the tales too.
4. **No forced playstyle.** Every unlock is reachable by any way of holding a battle. Sinking a fleet and routing it both yield wood. No leaf is earned only by one particular counter.
5. **Every EVENT unlock has a CAMPAIGN fallback**, so no player misses a leaf.
6. **One new leaf per debrief or interstitial.** Any others queue for the next. A ripening (a reading's story filling in beneath it, a line *Added in Seren's hand* appearing, a native mark translated) shows as a small mark and does not count toward the one.
7. **The Book only adds.** It never removes or changes a word the player has already read.
8. **Deaths are fixed points.** Each death is keyed to a campaign milestone (or, for Hesk, to Rimewatch being taken back), never to how a battle went (§5.4).
9. **A dead voice falls silent.** From the campaign of a death, no battle message or Whisper is spoken in that voice. The dead person's line passes to the mouth that keeps it (§3.8). The raised men's True Man lines begin at their raising.
10. **A death is told first by its leaf.** The one exception is a single hearth-word at the end of the death's own campaign (its interstitial), which follows the fact and tells no more than the fact (§5.4).
11. **The lore is true, as the Knowing is.** The runners' law, *"What I saw, I carry. I carry nothing else."*, is the in-world form of the rule that a Knowing is always true. A dispatch is *what is seen, as a runner carries it*.
12. **The Book is the canon.** *Plain Words* and *Original* carry every beat of the Book, in order, and every marker and native block. **v3:** they follow it beat for beat and anchor for anchor, not paragraph for paragraph, for Plain Words is a retelling with its own paragraphs (§1.10, §5.8). Where they differ in a fact, the Book is right.
13. **One switch** (K1, *new*). The reader's tab is one choice for the whole Book. Switching it at any leaf switches every leaf, and the reader keeps its place by the shared anchors (§5.8).

### 5.2 The prologue (first launch)

- **What plays.** The Invocation, then the climb-and-writing passage of I.1, marked `PROLOGUE EXCERPT`: from *The True Men looked to their Captain.* to *That was the last unbroken wall of the Shorelands, and that was the banner over it.* It plays on the torn-cloak art, is skippable, plays once, and can always be read again in the Book.
- **Its length, v3.** The excerpt is about 300 words in ten paragraphs with the Title (v2.0's was about 455; v1.3's 225), and the Invocation about 200. Read at an easy pace that is about ninety seconds, inside v1.3's target. **Recommended,** as before: pace it by beats, one paragraph to a beat of the art (the look at the wall; the climb; the cloak taken off; the silence and the child's cough; *He tore it down the back.*; the writing and the binding; the reading; the Title alone; the banner given to the wind), advanced by the player. v2.0's fallback, a shorter cut for the prologue only, is no longer needed (§7.1).
- **The excerpt stands alone.** v3 begins it at *The True Men looked to their Captain.* so it needs nothing before it. The Plain excerpt (about 250 words) stands alone the same way, and plays under *Plain Words*.
- **What the Book shows after it.** The first spread: I.1 on the left; on the right the wood's grain, the Stone's own rings with no mark in them, and beneath them one line in Seren's course-hand, which the player cannot yet read: *Untold. Halyna cannot yet hold this deep.* Its translation comes with *The Mortar Line* at Cam 1 complete (§5.7). The leaf stays bare of the wood until the Epilogue fills it at the very end. From the first minute the player sees the shape of something not yet understood, in both the hands that are to be learnt.
- **The foreword is in the codex from the start** (recommended), but the prologue does not show it. It is the key to the frame: the lintel over the pair-names, *laid*, the answer and its choosing, the two inks and the pieced readings, the silence after the wood, the unbonded scribe, and the tale that is the teller's in words that are Seren's. Its dateline says it was written in the third summer, and it says *The Book is half full.* **Its costs, v3:** it tells of *One of its tellers, that held up a wet finger to every wind*, already under the east cairns, which a player can read as Corlen once III.1 (Cam 5) has shown his habit, four campaigns before he dies; it shows Enrella alive, *the youngest*, in the present tense; and it says the seal was broken *till the wood showed Halyna what the mute stones beside it were*, which IV.2 and IV.3 later make plain (§7.2 call 17).

### 5.3 The tales by campaign (play order)

*Story order is not play order. The cycle opens at the wall in the middle of things and reaches back, and the cosmology arrives last, as memory. The play order is the hearth's own order, the nights on which the tales were laid.*

**How to read the When column.**
- A bare campaign number ("Cam 5") means the interstitial shown when that campaign is complete.
- A **debrief** is the screen after one battle.
- An **event** gate ("From Cam 11, the first … met") fires at the debrief of the first battle in which the sim recorded that event, from the named campaign on. If the event has not happened by its **fallback**, the leaf opens at the fallback instead.
- When two leaves fall due on the same surface, the one higher in this table shows, and the other waits for the next surface (law 6).

**The campaigns and the seasons** (the Reckoning's calendar, which the datelines follow):

| Winter | Tide | Campaigns, and their seasons |
|---|---|---|
| W1 | the Hasty Tide | 1 deep winter (the banner and the first month) · 2 late winter into the first spring · 3 the first summer · 4 high summer · 5 the first autumn · 6 late autumn |
| W2 | the Second Tide | 7 the Tide comes · 8 deep winter · 9 late winter · 10–11 spring · 12 summer · 13 late summer · 14 autumn |
| W3 | the Tide of Remembering | 15 the Tide comes · 16 deep winter · 17 late winter · 18–19 spring · 20–21 summer · 22 autumn |
| W4 | the Joined Tide | 23 winter · 24 spring · 25 summer · 26 autumn |
| W5 | the Tide that Learned Deceit | 27 winter · 28 late winter · 29 spring · 30 summer · 31 autumn |
| W6 | the Last Tide, then the Thrones | 32 winter · the going-down at winter's end · the Finale from spring to autumn |
| after | — | the next launch: Halyna die in the seventh winter; the Stone comes in the spring |

**The schedule.** v2.0's gates, unchanged. *"moved"* and *"new"* mark v2.0's changes from v1.3.

| Cam 29, the first decoy carving known (fallback: Cam 29) | **V.7 Part II, What We Taught Them** | the wood that learned to mislead; Pellow |
| Cam 31 | **V.5 Count V, The Tide that Learned Deceit** | the seam |
| Cam 32, the first carving known that plans its own going home (fallback: the debrief of Cam 32's second-to-last battle) | **V.7 Part III, The Plan That Carved Its Own Defeat** | the enemy hopes |
| Cam 32, the last battle's debrief | **V.5 Count VI, The Last Tide** | it can be sent home; the roll of the dead; *Mystarchs* |
| Cam 32 complete | **I.5 The Naming of the Rivenmen** | Ebba; we go home |
| *moved* · Each Throne fallen, in the player's own order | that Throne's entry in **VI.2** (the first also brings VI.2's headnote, its reading and its opening ring) and that haven's entry in **VI.1** (the first also brings VI.1's headnote and Kael's opening) | ten homecomings |
| *moved* · All ten Thrones fallen | VI.2's closing ring and Kael's close of VI.1; then **VI.3 The Knowing of the Green Wood** in full; II.2's untold word fills | recognition |
| The next launch after the Finale, or the credits | **The Epilogue** fills the leaf facing I.1 | the first leaf answered |
| *new* · The next launch after the Epilogue is laid | **The Last Note** of the Book of Knowings, with the rung *The Last Carver* (§5.7) | the last thing learned |

- **Why I.3 opens from Cam 2.** I.2's headnote says *this hearth had heard but one before it*, V.1 is laid *at the first month's end*, and I.3 *in its second month*. Opening I.3 from Cam 2 keeps the hearth's order, keeps Jory alive in it (he dies late in Cam 2), and still lands it after the player has used the calls. Fallback: v1.3's Cam 1 gate (§7.2 call 18).
- **The Homecomings and the Thrones in the Book.** An entry is laid in the player's order but stands in its haven's place, in the order of raising. An entry not yet earned shows as its haven's name and nothing more.
- **Breathing room.** Campaigns 7, 9, 11, 17, 19, 21, 28 and 30 still have no scheduled leaf, only their Whisper strand and any ripening. (An event leaf may land in Cam 11, since IV.2's gate opens there; Cam 9 carries Corlen's death as a hearth-word and a ripening.)
- **The debrief notice.** When a leaf unlocks, the debrief shows one line and no more: *"A new tale is laid at the hearth."* · *"Halyna have told more of the wood."* (with the lintel over the name).
- **The interstitial** (*changed in v3*). It shows the campaign's leaf from its first line to the end of the first sentence after about 80 words (never past about 120; never mid-sentence; never past a halt or a strike line), with *Read on in the Book*. v2.0 had widened this to 140 words for its long sentences; v3's sentences never run past 30 words, so v1.3's 80 to 120 holds again. The whole leaf is always in the Book, and nothing blocks play.

**The ripenings** (law 6: a small mark, never a new leaf). *v3 has fewer: the lines added in Seren's hand under IV.1, III.2 and IV.3 are gone from the Book (§1.14).*

| What ripens | Where it stands | When |
|---|---|---|
| ***Added in Seren's hand:*** *It was not the last.* | under Count I | Cam 9 (Corlen's death, the second strip) |
| IV.2's story, from *This is held in the grain.*, beneath its reading; the two drawn marks told | IV.2 | Cam 16 |
| ***Added in Seren's hand:*** *At the Keep that night no lamp burned on the north wall. Then a fisher-girl set one there, and lit it, and said, "Someone must keep a light."* | under VI.1's Rimewatch entry | with that entry |
| *Naelear* beside the sign | II.2 | all ten fallen |
| the Book of Knowings: an entry's line, its root sign, its fidelity; a Throne's name in the last column; *Burn*'s clause of the black crust | the appendix | as each is met, known, or (for *Burn*) at Emberhythe's homecoming |
| a native mark's translation beneath its hand | wherever the mark stands | on its rung (§5.7) |

### 5.4 The deaths in the game

| Death | Fixed point | First told in | Voice leaves the pools | Hearth-word at the end of the death's campaign | The strip |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1–D4 (the Guest; the axemen; Tarnel and Wendhessa; the warden and Brenn) | before the banner | IV.3, IV.4, IV.5, IV.6, II.3 | none speak in pools; Brenn is quoted only after IV.3 | — | none |
| **Jory** | Cam 2, the last weeks of the first winter, after I.3 has opened | Count I (Cam 6), then whole in IV.5 (Cam 12) | from Cam 2 | *"The first cairn is raised on the east face."* | the first |
| **Corlen** | Cam 9, late in the second winter, in a seed-fall | Count II (Cam 14); the line under Count I ripens at Cam 9 | from Cam 9 | *"Voss holds Corlen's battery."* | the second |
| **Wick** | Cam 16, the second month of the Tide of Remembering, the morning after the gun was moved | Count III (Cam 22) | from Cam 16 | *"Hale ran it."* | the third |
| **Enno and Della** (Enrella) | Cam 25, the morning of the three roads | V.7 Part I (Cam 25) | from Cam 25 | *"One," Kael said.* | the fourth, over the north stone |
| **Pellow** | Cam 29, the first deceit | V.7 Part II (Cam 29) | from Cam 29; Ruan's True Man lines begin | *"Look close."* (in Ruan's voice) | the fifth |
| **Old Ebba** | Cam 32 complete, after Count VI | I.5 (Cam 32 complete) | she speaks in no pool | — (I.5 itself tells it) | the sixth, on the east face |
| **Hesk** | when Rimewatch is taken back: its Throne's fall, in whatever order the player takes the havens, or, if that battle is lost, the retaking | VI.1, the Rimewatch entry | after the Rimewatch battle (he may speak in its own pre-battle pool) | *"Someone must keep a light."* (in the fisher-girl's voice) | the seventh, over the mere, in the art only (§7.2 call 3) |
| **Halyna** | the seventh winter, the next launch after the Finale | the Epilogue | after the Finale | — | the last, over the gate; then the plain grey cloak |

- **The raised men.** Voss speaks as a corporal until Cam 9 and as a True Man from Cam 9 (Corlen's battery). Ruan speaks as a file-leader until Cam 29 and as a True Man from Cam 29, when *the Captain raised him a True Man*. Hale refuses the battery and stays a runner. Kael's roll is *fourteen names, and twelve to answer* at Count VI and *Fourteen names, and eleven to answer* after Hesk.
- **No death waits on a battle's outcome.** A lost battle in a death's campaign changes nothing; the death happens at its milestone. The one keyed to a battle, Hesk's, is keyed to Rimewatch being taken back, which every player reaches.
- **The hood** (*changed in v3*). The Book keeps the Captain's hood up, and puts it back twice: after IV.3, the first Amen over a killing by our own, and in the Epilogue. So wherever the game shows the Captain at the hearth (the debrief art, *Open the Book*), his hood is up, and it is back only on the Cam 15 interstitial that opens IV.3 and at the Epilogue. The deaths are shown by the half-cloak, shorter by a strip at each (§5.12). v2.0 put the hood back on every death's debrief.

### 5.5 The Knowing: the wood leaves, unlocked by depth

**The rule of the wood.** Mystwood is not read. *The wood is not read but known, whole, as a memory is known.* Touch it and its tale is known whole; the hard part is the telling, *So Halyna give it up in pieces, the one taking up where the other's words fail.* Only one of Halyna need touch the wood, for what one knows the other knows. At first a shore-mind receives only the shape of a knowing, for the wood's thoughts are a forest's (*the rest was a forest's thought, and they had but the shore's*). Over the Tides Halyna learn to hold more of it and to tell it. Every wood leaf is gated by how much Halyna can hold and tell, and that rises Tide by Tide, by campaign bands, so it is deterministic.

| Knowing depth | Band | What Halyna can do | Wood leaves reachable |
|---|---|---|---|
| one shape | the Hasty Tide (1–6) | know that the wood means something, and tell one word of it | none (the Knowing line only) |
| two shapes, held at once | the Second Tide (7–14) | hold two signs together; tell the Hasty wrecks back whole (V.2) | IV.2, the reading only |
| sentences, and the memory in them | the Tide of Remembering (15–22) | tell sentences, and the memory in them | IV.2 whole · V.3 · IV.4 · IV.6 |
| ties | the Joined Tide (23–26) | hold who waited for whom, and so many hulls at once | V.4 · V.6 |
| aim | the Tide that Learned Deceit (27–31) | tell what a carving *wants* | II.2 (all but one word) |
| a whole morrow | the Last Tide (32) | hold and tell a whole plan | V.7 Part III (a stone leaf) |
| true names | the Thrones (the Finale) | tell true names | VI.2, an entry a Throne · VI.3's wood line · the last word of II.2 |

| Wood leaf | What is known | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| IV.2 The Axe in the Grain | a Blackthorn heart, grown from the last shoot of a felled pillar's stump | its reading from the first Blackthorn Standard-Bearer met from Cam 11 (fallback Cam 14); whole at Cam 16 |
| V.3 The Wreck That Went Home | a hull sunk on its second coming | the first verification probe met from Cam 16 (fallback Cam 16) |
| IV.4 The Gift Held an Hour | the gift, held an hour | Cam 18 |
| IV.6 The Voyage of the Aelvaren | a Remembering heart (every grove drank the Aelvaren's grief) | Cam 20 |
| V.4 The Council Under the Thin Sky | a Silverbark heart, *A cutting of those elders* | the first Silverbark heart met from Cam 23 (fallback Cam 23) |
| V.6 Of the Growing | a Joined Tide heart, *A tree we were, and old, in the first groves* | Cam 24 |
| II.2 Of the Breath of the Wood | the gift, at its deepest holding | Cam 27 |
| VI.2 The Thrones That Walk the Sea | each Throne's heart | that Throne fallen |
| VI.3, the wood's line | the living sliver in the last Throne's heart | all ten fallen |
| the Stone's grain (the Epilogue's fold) | the Stone out of the Grey | *The Last Carver* |

**How a wood leaf looks** (*changed in v3*, §1.5):
- **Blank.** The leaf's own round of grain, fully cut and not glossed, and beneath it one line in Seren's course-hand: *Untold. Halyna cannot yet hold this deep.* Only the leaf facing I.1 shows its rings uncut. Every wood leaf shows blank from the moment its stone leaf opens, so the player sees the empty half of the spread.
- **The reading alone.** Only IV.2 opens this way, at its shape gate: the dateline, the headnote and Halyna's reading, with its two marks drawn and not yet told (`legend_iv2_gap1`, `legend_iv2_gap2`). The reading is the shape of a knowing before it could be told. v2.0's *As first known, … the shape only* fragments are gone; the reading does their work.
- **Whole.** The dateline, the headnote, the reading, then the story from *This is held in the grain.* in three rings parted by `<!-- RING -->`, and the return, *…and the grain holds it still.* / *And no one answered.* **The builder keys a wood leaf on `<!-- READING -->` and on its first story line, *This is held in the grain.*** At Cam 16 IV.2's story ripens beneath its reading, which stays, for the Book never removes a word.
- **The two inks on a wood leaf.** The reading's lines alternate the two Halyna inks line by line, and neither is named. The story is in the wood role, in one ink. The headnote is stone, in Seren's ink. v2.0 alternated whole paragraphs of the story in two inks under `<!-- HALYNA -->`; v3 does not.
- **The name swap.** Inside wood leaves the wood names things by its own words: the black pillars are *our pillars* and *the Naelsaen*; the mist is *the breath*; the warden is *the host*, *the master of that house*. From VI.2 on, the Thrones speak by the names they gave themselves. The last swap is the wood's own name, **Naelear**, which fills the untold word of II.2 when VI.3 is laid.
- **The silence.** Every wood leaf closes *And no one answered.* The hearth never answers the wood until VI.3.
- **Halyna's two inks on stone leaves.** II.3 is theirs whole, inside one HALYNA block, and alone names its tellers (`<!-- MARKED -->`), for the children. IV.3's reading of Brenn is theirs, inside another, alternating by paragraph. And wherever Halyna speak a line in halves in another teller's leaf, the halves stand inside their own block and take the two inks: the foreword (1), I.2 (2), I.4 (3), IV.1 (1), IV.3's annal (1), V.1 (1), V.5 Count VI (1), V.7 (4) and VI.3 (3). That is nineteen blocks in all. Other pairs' halves (Idrenna's, Orvenna's, Enrella's) stay in the leaf's one ink.
- **Stone and wood on the page.** The builder sets stone text and wood text in two roles, per paragraph: the wood in a barely different old-style face, the stone ink a shade toward yellow and the wood ink a shade toward green, still one document. Seren's headnotes are stone even on wood leaves. Wood-role passages inside stone leaves are marked `<!-- W -->`: V.2's seven carvings; V.7's three carvings and Part II's italic line; VI.3's *We remember our home too.* and line 3 of *The Two Homes*. The Epilogue's inscription is `<!-- MIXED -->`.

**The Book of Knowings** is the fleet's own half of the Book.
- **Forty-six carvings** under ten roots: seven of the young wood, eight of each of the three Tides after it, seven of the Tide that Learned Deceit, and eight Judgements of the Thrones (two Thrones carried an older carving whole). The Last Tide carved no new head, only whole morrows *and at the end of each a turning for home*. *Burn*, a sign of the Fall, stands apart: *I count it not among these.*
- **In the game each entry fills** when its carving has been met and survived, by any route. Until then only its root sign shows. An entry gives the carved line, the root sign, Seren's gloss, a plain gloss of what the ships did, and, once the carving has been known by name, its counters (admiral doc §9d).
- **Fidelity follows depth.** An entry is first written at the depth of the band in which its carving was met, and ripens when the depth passes it. The seven Hasty entries hold Seren's one word each until V.2 is laid at Cam 13, and then *bear the soldiers' names, worn smooth with handling*; Second-Tide entries keep their gaps until Cam 15.
- **The Thrones' column** (*Whose it was at the last*) stays blank until that Throne falls. Leavaren's line reads *the Bearer of New Branches*.
- ***Burn*** shows below the table when V.2 is laid. Its clause of the black crust at Emberhythe ripens with Emberhythe's homecoming.
- **The roots and the growth lines** (admiral doc): the Wave is Flood, the Lone Stroke is Sounding, the Bar Before is Screen, the Spark is Hunt, the Mute Square is Siege, the Two Mouths are Feint, the Turned Stern is Lure, the Breath is Bleed, the Knot is Patience, and the Smoothed Cut is Mask.
- **The Last Note** is the last leaf of the Book: Seren reads the Guest's three words on the lowest of the three grey stones, by their sound (§4.2). It opens at the next launch after the Epilogue (§5.3), with its drawing (§5.6).

### 5.6 The native hands on unearned leaves

**The Book is a translation.** Every leaf the player reads in English was first laid in a hand of its own. The Rivenmen's is **the course-hand**: their tongue, *Orrowen*, written in a chisel alphabet of straight strokes that stands on a mortar line, each word a stone; its old form, the first builders' hand, is **the Hal**. The wood's is **the grain**: it holds meaning and not sound, as a round of growth rings read from the heart to the bark, with its marks cut across the rings. A surface the player has not yet earned stays in its own hand. Earning it adds the translation beneath the hand, and the hand stays (law 7). The tongues and the hands are specified in the Tongues doc.

**Every drawing in the Book's text.** The Book carries each as a `::: native` block (or a `::: note` for a mark set inside a line, written `⟦id⟧`) with its in-world caption and its fold. Every key and kind is v2.0's. The *Translation, unlocked by* lines live here.

| id | Where | Kind | What it shows | Translation, and when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `legend_stonwryt_hal` | the foreword | line | the Stonwryt of the first builders on the tale-stone's underside, in the Hal: the vow, signed under one lintel with Aldwena's pair-name | the vow in English is given in the fold at once, for Seren quotes it; the Hal itself is read at *The Bedrock Hand* (Cam 16) |
| `legend_title_coal` | the Invocation; I.1 | line | the Title in the course-hand, as the Captain wrote it in coal on the torn cloak | given at first launch, for Kael reads it out |
| `legend_stone_rings` | facing I.1 | round | the Stone's own rings, no mark cut in them | when the Epilogue is laid, the same rings carry the Last Carver's marks |
| `legend_seren_note` | facing I.1, and under every blank wood leaf | line | Seren's note in the course-hand | *The Mortar Line* (Cam 1 complete) |
| `legend_stonwryt_aldwena` | I.4 | chip | Aldwena's mark on the lid of the black chest | none: a mark is matched, not read. It must be the very mark of `legend_stonwryt_hal`, so the player can match them |
| `legend_title_capstone` | I.4 | line | the Title cut under the new capstone in the Captain's own strokes, *large and slow*, not one stroke mended | read at once |
| `legend_naelear_chip` | II.2, inline | note | the name the wood gives itself, as a sign | *Those of the Breath* (all ten fallen, VI.3 laid): *Naelear*, set beside the sign, which stays |
| `legend_stonwryt_halyna` | II.3 (in the ash); VI.3 (across the stem); the Epilogue (the stem come home) | chip | Halyna's mark | none: matched, not read. The same drawing all three times; if the builder needs one id per unlock, use `_ash`, `_stem` and `_return` |
| `legend_iv2_gap1`, `legend_iv2_gap2` | IV.2's reading, inline | note | two marks of the wood's shape that Halyna could see, and could not yet tell | *The Memory Ray* (Cam 15); told when IV.2 comes whole (Cam 16) |
| `legend_seal_wendhessa` | IV.3 | chip | Wendhessa's mark on the seal of Brenn's bundle, as it was broken | none |
| `legend_wave_chip` | facing V.1 | chip | the Wave, as Seren drew it; its one word, *Shore.*, is known at once | the whole order, *To shore. As root to water, go. Wait on no bough.*, at *Both Halves* (Cam 13) |
| `legend_stonwryt_enrella` | V.7 Part I | chip | Enrella's mark, whole, under the north stone | none. **Draw no stroke as Della's**: the player looks for it too, and *Ye cannot tell.* |
| `legend_stone_epilogue` | the Epilogue | stone | the Last Carver's letters, cut across a grain no one at the hearth has known | the letters are read at once; the grain, and the line *Known by no one at this hearth. Set down for whoever has learned the grain.*, at *The Last Carver* |
| `legend_three_stones` | the Last Note | chip | the lowest of the three grey stones: the curling letters of the edge, and among them three words of ours, cut by their sound | the three words are given in the text, by sound; the rest of the letters is never read, for stone does not translate |

- **In its own hand in the game, beyond the Book's text:** every blank wood leaf (its round, with Seren's line beneath); each Throne's name, kept in the bark until that Throne falls or, if the battle is lost, until its haven is retaken (admiral doc, T-rule 9); the Book of Knowings, where an unmet entry shows its root sign alone; the Knowing line in the debrief, where the drawn root sign replaces the admiral doc's placeholder `᛫`; and the root sign a sunk hull floats (law 2).
- **Read at once, in the hand and in English:** the Title, in both its cuttings; the rolls, the warning and the warden's letter, which their tales already quote; and the Last Carver's letters, *cut so that one could read it alone*.
- **Never read:** Seren's empty leaf facing V.4 stays empty of every hand, as she asked. The warden's name is cut out of its stone. The three stained lines of the roll are lost. What was seen in the grey on Glasspire's night is not in the grain. Halyna's mark under the new capstone is never drawn there (*Their mark is not mine to draw.*); the player meets it only in the ash, on the stem and on the boat.
- **Truth or nothing.** Every drawing is a true encoding by the Tongues doc's rules, so a player who decodes it reads what the translation says. Where no native text has been authored, nothing is drawn, and nothing is faked. This binds the *Original* tab too, until it is set down (§5.8).
- **The accessible titles never leak.** A locked drawing's accessible title says only what it is (*A leaf in the course-hand, not yet read*; *A carving in the grain, not yet known*; *A pair's mark*), never its English.
- **The marks a player can match:** Aldwena's (the foreword, I.4); Halyna's (II.3, VI.3, the Epilogue); Enrella's (V.7 Part I, alone); Wendhessa's broken seal (IV.3); and the three words on the hearth-stone (the Last Note), against the sounds Brenn gave in IV.3.
- **Still owed by the Tongues team:** the four pair-marks; the soft letters of the mute stones, so a decoding player can find the Guest's three words on the lowest stone; the Rite's slates in the Hal; the names in the lintel of the western stair, Halvard's last; the round of each blank wood leaf, composed by rule; the ten small rounds of VI.2; and the Orrowen and grain of every leaf, for the *Original* (§5.8).

### 5.7 The script-unlock ladder

*The native hands translate on the rungs of the Tongues doc's unlock ladder (its §12.2). Each rung is a milestone every player reaches. Each wood rung is one band of the Knowing given a name; each stone rung is a hand the player can now read. A translation added beneath a hand already on a leaf is a ripening, not a new leaf. The names are proposals.*

| When | Achievement | Hand | What it translates in the Book |
|---|---|---|---|
| First launch | given, not earned | stone | the Title, with its English. The leaf facing I.1 shows the Stone's rings and Seren's line, and the foreword the tale-stone's Stonwryt in the Hal, with its vow given in English |
| Cam 1 complete (V.1) | *The Mortar Line* | stone | Seren's line, wherever it stands; the living course-hand enters the codex, so any living line can be read by rule |
| Cam 1 complete (V.1) | *One Word* | wood | the root signs, a word each (*shore*, *wall*, *go*, *before*, *flash*, *grey*, *home*), and Seren's names for the ten roots; the Wave's one word |
| Cam 2 complete (I.4) | none | stone | the Title under the new capstone, read at once; Aldwena's mark on the chest lid shown, to be matched |
| Cam 4 (II.3) | none | — | Halyna's mark shown in the ash, to be matched later |
| From Cam 9, the first Second-Tide carving known (fallback: Cam 10) | *Bough and Stone* | wood | things and acts; what a closed mark is of, wood or stone; the condition bands |
| Cam 13 (V.2) | *Both Halves* | wood | the seven Hasty rounds whole, and the seven Hasty entries ripen; the Wave's whole order |
| Cam 15, the first debrief of the Tide of Remembering | *The Memory Ray* | wood | ties across rings (the Second-Tide gaps fill), memory rays, the look-signs, the question and doubt flags; IV.2's two marks, told when IV.2 comes whole at Cam 16. Wendhessa's broken seal is shown in IV.3 |
| Cam 16 complete | *The Bedrock Hand* | stone | the Hal itself: the tale-stone's Stonwryt; the slate leaves of the Rite; Aldwena's mark on the chest; the lintel's names, once authored |
| Cam 25, the first *Three roads, one hour* known (fallback: Cam 25) | *Hearts in One Trunk* | wood | joined piths, ties within a ring, the pair. Enrella's mark is shown in V.7 Part I |
| Cam 29, the first decoy carving known (fallback: Cam 29) | *The Hollow Cut* | wood | hollow and smoothed cuts, the aim, half size (*Rhenear*), the twin |
| Cam 32, the first carving known that plans its own going home (fallback: the debrief of Cam 32's second-to-last battle) | *The Turnings* | wood | forks, chains and the home clause |
| Cam 32 complete (I.5) | *Read Down the Lintel* | stone | the names at the head of the lintel of the western stair: the whole column, Hal to living, once authored (Halvard's name is added at the bottom in the Epilogue) |
| Each Throne fallen | *A Name in the Bark* | wood | that Throne's name in the bark (its VI.2 entry) |
| All ten fallen (VI.3) | *Those of the Breath* | wood | the sliver's sign; *Naelear*, set beside the sign in II.2, which stays. Halyna's mark across the stem is shown |
| The next launch after the Finale (the Epilogue) | none | both | the Stone's letters, read at once, for *The Mortar Line* is long held; Halyna's mark on the stem come home; the facing leaf's rings now carry the Last Carver's marks |
| The next launch after the Epilogue is laid | *The Last Carver* | wood | the Stone's own telling, beneath its round; and the Last Note opens, with the three stones |

- **The debrief notice** for a rung is one line: *"A leaf in the course-hand is read."* for a stone rung, and *"Halyna have told more of the wood."* for a wood rung.
- **Skill achievements** (*Pacifist* and the rest) may add one gloss line at most, never a translation. *Pacifist* could add Seren's soft readings of the signs to the sign-list.
- **The schedule is unchanged.** The Hasty entries still ripen at Cam 13, the Second-Tide gaps at Cam 15, and IV.2 comes whole at Cam 16.
- **Every tab.** A drawing is the same under *The Book*, *Plain Words* and *Original*; only its translation changes language (the caption and the gloss line may be put in plain words under *Plain Words*), and *Original* gives none.

### 5.8 The reader: three tabs, one switch

Open the Book shows three tabs at the head of every tale, and of the foreword, the Invocation, the Epilogue and the Book of Knowings:

| Tab | What it is | State now |
|---|---|---|
| ***The Book*** | Seren's English: this Book, v3.0.0, the canon | done (`wf11/book_v3.md`) |
| ***Plain Words*** | the same Book told again in easy modern English, from its beats: every event, name, number, teller and line kept, in its own sentences and paragraphs; about grade 3; no archaism; contractions where a speaker would use them; Jack's fixed lines and the liturgy in their Plain forms; the pair-names under their lintel, the blots, the markers and the native blocks exactly as the Book has them; no datelines | done (`wf11/plain_v3.md`, checked leaf by leaf, §1.10) |
| ***Original*** | the Book in its own hands: the stone leaves, headnotes, foreword, Invocation and Epilogue in Orrowen, in Seren's leaf-hand; the wood leaves in the grain, each round whole and then ring by ring | **owed** (K2): to be set down from v3 by the Tongues team. Until then every native drawing the Book carries shows here at its place, and the leaf's prose is not faked. *Being set down anew in its own hands.* does not ship (§1.11) |

- **One switch** (K1). The choice is one for the whole Book: switching at any leaf switches every leaf. It is remembered with the settings, not the save. The first choice is *The Book*.
- **Keeping the reader's place.** The three texts line up by the anchors they share, in the same number and order (§1.11): the headings, the bold part heads, the nineteen HALYNA blocks, the eight READING blocks, the sixteen RING rules, the native blocks and notes, the W markers, the MARKED, TOKEN, MIXED, RIPENS, PROLOGUE EXCERPT and FACING LEAF markers, every song and every hearth's answer. On a switch the page lands on the last anchor above the line in view, then the same fraction of the way to the next. Under *Original* a wood leaf gives one ring to a paragraph.
- ***Original* translates nothing.** It shows the native hands and nothing else, for the player who decodes.
- **A locked leaf, or a locked mark inside a leaf,** shows in its own hand under every tab until it is earned. When it is earned, *The Book* and *Plain Words* add its translation beneath the hand, each in its own words, and the hand stays. A leaf earned whole is set down in *Original* whole. No tab shows a leaf or a mark that the Book does not yet hold.
- **The grain's glosses follow the tab:** *shore*, *the mute thing* and *the dread* in the Book; the Plain's own plain words under *Plain Words*; Seren's own Orrowen words under *Original*. The tabs show on a locked leaf too, so the player learns where its translation will appear.
- **The lintel in the reader.** In *The Book* and *Plain Words* a pair-name is drawn under a fine overline the width of the name, as a lintel over two posts, wherever the source writes `{{Name}}`. The stroke is decoration to a screen reader, which reads the name as written. In *Original* the name stands under a lintel line in the course-hand, and its verb takes the dual.
- **The two inks in the reader** follow §5.5: the readings line by line; Halyna's blocks by paragraph or by half; II.3 by its marked tellers, with its italic lines in Seren's ink and the opening and the oldest saying in neither Halyna ink.
- **The story page** (*The Legends*) carries the three tabs inside the page, with the one switch, as a working model of the reader. **The copy Jack shares** (K3) is that whole story page on the web, with every leaf and every native drawing, private to the people he chooses; never a trimmed copy (§1.11).

### 5.9 The Whispers and the hearth-lines

**Where they live.** In the debrief and the interstitial, never in the first Build. Each band has three strands:
- a **hearth-word**: a short line drawn from the tales, spoken by Halyna, one of the Twelve, or another voice of the wall;
- the **Knowing**: what Halyna knew from the battle's own wreck-wood, in Seren's words. The lines below are samples; the live line is generated from the sim's record of the carving that actually ran, so it is always true, and its fidelity follows knowing depth (§5.5);
- a **wood-word**, from Cam 15 on: a line from a wood leaf's reading, in Halyna's pieces.

**The rules.**
- A line tagged ⟨tale⟩ enters its band's pool only once that tale (or that ripening) has opened. A line that names a fleet behaviour waits until the player has met it. After, never before.
- **A line that quotes a tale quotes it word for word** in the v3 Book's form (*hath*, *must*, *thee*, *lasteth*). Lines marked **(J)** are Jack's GDD Whispers and stay in his words; a line marked **(dep)** must stay, for a tale quotes it or answers it.
- **Recommended** (§7.2 call 19): a hearth-word that quotes a tale follows the reader's tab, in the Book's words under *The Book* and the Plain's under *Plain Words*.
- A Halyna line comes in two halves, a voice each.
- **A dead voice falls silent** (law 9). After a death, the dead person's line may come back only in the mouth that keeps it (§3.8).
- **Faith is never the butt of a joke.**
- ***We remember.*** **is never a Whisper.** Nor is *We remember our home too.*, which lives only in VI.3 and never appears in store art or screenshots.

**Cam 1–6 · the Hasty Tide · confidence**
- "They picked the wrong fortress." *(Kael, in the first winter)* **(J, dep: V.2 opens on it and turns it)**
- "Twelve generations of Stonewright walls. Let them try." *(a wall-hand)* **(J)**
- *Halyna:* "Hold the stone." / "Hold it, and it will hold thee." ⟨I.2⟩
- "Wherever ye see a Stonewright on the wall in the holding, look again. Ye will see two." ⟨I.2⟩
- *Orvenna:* "Put the stone down," / "and pick the next one up. That is the whole war." *(their own, a Whisper only)*
- "What I saw, I carry. I carry nothing else." *(Hale: the runners' oath)* ⟨I.3⟩
- "For the Captain, an hour lasteth as long as a day." ⟨I.3⟩
- "It is only a rhyme." *(Jory)* ⟨I.3⟩ (Cam 1–2 only)
- "Wait. The sea hath not finished." *(Corlen)* ⟨III.1⟩ (to Cam 8)
- "The fleet hath spent its fire." *(the wall's saying)* ⟨V.5 I⟩
- "Treelings the wall called them, as if a name could make them small." *(Kael)* ⟨V.5 I⟩
- "It was the first strip." *(Kael)* ⟨V.5 I⟩
- *Knowing:* "One shape, and we are not sure even of that. Halyna say *shore*."

**Cam 7–14 · the Second Tide · humility**

- "It had never fallen. It had only been left." ⟨II.1⟩
- *Halyna:* "We two lived it not," / "but our fathers' fathers lived it. They wrote it down, and never read what they had written." ⟨IV.1⟩

- "Stone does not speak as they believed." **(J)** ⟨IV.2 whole⟩

- *Halyna:* "The fleet cannot be called home," / "for the wood obeyeth the wood." *Kael:* "Then we will send it home." **(J, dep: Count VI pays it off)** ⟨V.6⟩ *(Jack's GDD has "recalled"; §7.2 call 22)*

**The Finale · the Thrones · recognition**
- *Knowing, one for each Throne:* "Here is the name it gave itself, the haven it sat in, and the judgement it was grown to carry." *(filled from its VI.2 entry)*
