**Dropped or recast in v3.** Every quoted line takes the v3 wording (*hath*, *must*, *thee*, *lasteth*, *ye*, *whoso kneeleth*, *called home*, *obeyeth*, *lieth not*, *kept no man alive*). Brenn's *I laughed. I have not laughed at anything since.* is gone with the Book's line, and his mirror takes its place. *We will not lay it all on the Council.* goes with the Book's. *The shore laughs at the grey boat. The ship-masters do not.* becomes a ship-master's own saying. Kael's *Never yet did the grey sails tell a lie that did not show its seam* goes with Count V's. *It's only a rhyme* moves from Voss over the cairn, which v3 no longer has, to Jory alive. The wood-words are now lines of the readings. The Plain's forms (*Someone has to keep a light.*, *Hold it, and it will hold you.*) are for the Plain tab only.

### 5.10 Battle messages

**The style rules**
1. **When, and for how long.** At battle load or briefing, or during the 3-2-1 transition, for three seconds. Never during timed Build or a live Fight.
2. **The draw.** Each battlefield has a pool of 5 to 8 messages, and a replay shows a different one. The draw is cosmetic and never touches the battle's seed. Because of rule 7, any draw gives every player the same information.
3. **Who speaks.**
   - Wall-voices: Hale, Voss, Kael and the other True Men by name (the Twelve, then Voss and Ruan, §3.7), Wick and the runners, Jory and the soldiers of the six companies, wall-hands, file-leaders, lookouts, masons.
   - Seren and Halyna speak rarely. A Halyna line is two halves, a voice each.
   - The other pairs, in halves: **Idrenna**, who take the long view; **Orvenna**, who speak of the wall and never raise their voices; **Enrella**, the young pair, who finish each other's jests (while they live).
   - **Merrick** and the old ship-masters, who fear the sea and the grey boat, and say so plainly. There is no saying of the coast.
   - **A Trade Council lord** who fled to the Keep, whose old pride is turning.
   - **The Captain never speaks** in a message. There are no admirals, generals or modern ranks, for there was never a navy and the generals were slain; and no one is aboard the fleet.
4. **The dead fall silent** (§5.4): Jory leaves every pool from Cam 2, Corlen from Cam 9, Wick from Cam 16, Enrella from Cam 25, Pellow from Cam 29, and Hesk after the Rimewatch battle. Voss's True Man lines begin at Cam 9 and Ruan's at Cam 29.
5. **Length.** At most 16 words, in one or two sentences.
6. **Register** (*changed in v3*). Soldiers' speech of their world, shorter and plainer than the Book. The Book is now in heavy old English; a message is read in three seconds, so it takes only the light end of that: old word order and the stock words (*ere*, *naught*, *yet*, *for* as a joining word) are welcome, and *-eth*, *thou* and the old pasts are not, unless the line quotes a tale. No contractions in new lines; Jack's own lines keep theirs. No *upon*. Humour comes from character, never from anachronism. No design vocabulary and no mechanic named. *Sortie* is allowed in soldiers' mouths only, and so is *treelings*. At most one oath in a pool, and faith is never the butt of a joke.
7. **Hint discipline.** If one message in a pool hints at the battle's carving, every message in that pool carries the same hint in a different voice. Otherwise none of them does. A hint may name a tell that fits the Tide. It never names the counter.
8. **Truth.** Never say what the fleet *will* do. What the fleet remembers lasts one siege only, for the wood remembers the wall, not the hand. A Throne line may count groans. The soldiers' fear-names for the Thrones (§6) are game words, in no leaf of the Book; use them only until that Throne's true name has been told in its VI.2 entry. A treeling line goes only in the pool of a battlefield that lands root-men, and a seed line only where crown-hulls sail. **No horn, ever**: the runners reach the Captain, and he calls by his own voice.
9. **Flavour.** The Tide sets the fleet's behaviour: one-word mobs, then pairs, then looking, then three roads, then seams, then morrows, then groans. The theater sets the texture: salt, river, fen, canopy, cairn, ember, sand, rime, crag-wind, crystal in the dark.

**Examples.** v1.3's thirteen stand; the v2.0 proposals stand with the v3 changes marked.

| Theater · Tide · voice | Message |
|---|---|
| Coastal · Hasty · Kael | "Eight sails, and not one keeping station with another. I count them anyway." |
| Coastal · Hasty · a wall-hand | "They come at the wall one by one, and each one stays until it sinks." |
| River · Second · Voss | "They come in pairs now. One takes the shot; the other takes the ground." |
| Swamp · Second · a mason | "The fen drinks mortar like ale. Lay it thick, and lay it twice." |
| Forest · Remembering · Hale | "We moved the south gun by night. By morning the grey sails were looking for it." |
| Mountain · Joined · a file-leader | "The drums went all night, hull to hull. No one on this wall slept." |
| Mountain · any · a wall-hand | "Treelings on the shingle. They find the weak stone the way water does." |
| Any · a mason | "Seed came over the east keep on the wind. The masons are still sweeping it up." |
| Any · Stannard of the ridge | "I quarried stone before I carried a pike. I know which of these will hold." |
| Desert · Deceit · Kael *(a hint pool: every message names the same tell)* | "Like the first winter's ships. But these sails go pale. I like it not." |
| Frozen · Deceit · Idrenna | "Every wall begins with one stone and one prayer." / "We laid the prayer first." |
| Any · a Trade Council lord | "My fathers sold their black pillars by the cartload. I never asked what they held up." |
| Finale · a True Man | "Two groans from the harbour. Every hand to the walls." |
| Coastal · Hasty · Corlen (to Cam 8) | "Wait. The sea hath not finished." *(v3: the Book's form)* |
| Coastal · Hasty · Jory (Cam 1 only) | "Wet planks and grey sails. I have seen worse on the quay." |
| Any · Hasty · Hale | "What I see, I carry. This morning it is eight sails." |
| Any · Pellow (to Cam 28) | "Look close. I ground that glass myself, and it lies not." |
| Any · Wick (to Cam 15) | "I can run the wall-walk twice while you say the Title. Watch me." |
| Any · Orvenna | "Put the stone down," / "and pick the next one up." |
| Any · Enrella (to Cam 24) | "It is like being one person," / "with four hands and no excuses." |
| Any · Merrick | "I turn my face when they burn. Ask me not why." |
| Any · Voss (from Cam 9) | "Corlen would have waited. So I wait." |
| Frozen · any · Hesk (until Rimewatch) | "The north lamp is lit. The night-watch can steer by it." |
| Finale · Rimewatch · Hesk | "Six winters I have waited to light this lamp on the ice." *(v3: no upon)* |
| Any · Ruan (from Cam 29) | "Look close, as Pellow said. Look close." |

**Jack's lines, kept** word for word unless a note says otherwise (spelling follows the Book's *harbour*):
- "The salt air stings. The walls don't care."
- "Grandfather built this harbour wall. I'll be damned if I let it fall." *(its pool's one oath)*
- "Two sails on the horizon, and not keeping station. This will be easy." *(edited in v1.3: "Standard formation" contradicts a Hasty Tide that keeps none)*
- "The Commander says hold the coast. We hold the coast." *(the wall calls him Commander; the hearth calls him the Captain)*
- "Fresh mortar, fresh stones. The wall stands renewed."
- "Ice in the mortar. Ice in my bones. The wall still holds."
- "Voss asked why we fight for ice and stone. I said it's not the ice I'm fighting for." *(The speaker is Stannard, who gave that answer in I.5.)*
- "The wind carries their drums. Let it carry our cannons' answer." *(the drums are the knocking of the wood along the bond of roots)*
- The Finale only, filled from the sim: "Three keeps held against the Leviathan. The Stonewrights will sing of the east wall." *(a fear-name, before the true name is told)*
- The Last Garrison: "The keep is falling! Every hand to the guns!"

**Cut, or recast.**
- "They sent the Leviathan against the Frozen pass. We sent it back." A Throne cannot appear outside the Finale, so it becomes *"They sent a great hull against the Frozen pass. We sent it back."*
- "You fled the Sandworm last time, Commander. It remembers." It implies the fleet remembers across sieges, so in the Finale only it becomes *"The Deep-Beneath has sat under Sandreach since it came. Count the groans."* (the wall's English name, which VI.1 uses).
- Any line with a horn, a horn-call or a signal flag is cut.
- Any line that gives the coast a custom of burning, or a saying about what the grey sends, is cut.

### 5.11 Command Time in the fiction

- **Jack's ruling (2026-09-26), unchanged.** A day has **24 hours** and an hour **60 minutes**. At the pace of the siege a day passes in **18 real seconds**, so an hour goes by in **0.75 s**. The racing hour is meant: it is the urgency of the holding.
- **Under command.** When the player starts an order in a Fight, time ramps down (it does not snap) until an hour takes **18 real seconds**: 1/24 speed, the hour as long as a day was, the minutes ticking every 0.3 s. When the order is committed or cancelled, time dwells a moment at that floor, so a second planned order can follow without racing the ramp, then ramps back up. Orders are stamped in sim ticks, so wall-clock speed never changes an outcome. The ramp and dwell numbers live in admiral doc §12, which restates the tick-to-hour mapping under the 24-hour day; the Fight doc must still restate any grid-per-second figures.
- **The command, told as fiction.** The Captain gives each True Man his mind before the assault: *where to watch, what shot to lay, and when to hold* (the Deploy order card). In the holding each of the Twelve fights his battery by his own judgement, because he knows that mind; that is why each battery fights on its own (auto-fire running the player's plan). **The Captain calls one battery at a time, with his own voice, standing at its side.** The three calls, which the troops drill every dawn (I.3), are the game's three Fight verbs:
  1. *Shift your ground*: the water a battery watches, not the stones it stands on (re-task the zone);
  2. *Change your shot*: for the sea, the shore or the sky (switch domain);
  3. *Hold, or loose* (fire control).

  > *Shift your ground, and watch new water;*
  > *change your shot for the sea, the shore, the sky;*
  > *hold, or loose, when the Captain is by.*

  The calls are orders to a battery of many men, and are never put into *thou*.
- **Command Time is the walk.** When the Captain goes along the wall to a battery and stands at its side to call it, the hour stretches around him: *It stopped not short. It slowed, like a stone-cart on a long hill, till a boy behind might count the spokes.*, and lets go *slow, like a held breath going out* (I.3). The stamp is the tick, not the clock.
- **One at a time is slow.** The mechanic's limit is told as a death: in V.7 Part II *The Captain went along the wall, and at each True Man's shoulder gave the call: "Shift your ground." He hurried not, as lamplighters hurry not, lest the taper die.* / *Each battery he had reached lived. One at a time is slow.* Pellow's was the farthest. It is what the player feels when one battery can be commanded at a time while the rest fight on their own.
- **The runners** carry what is seen, never commands, to wherever the Captain stands (§2.2, J7). Their words tell him where to walk.
- **The law, told as character.** Kael's mend states determinism: *"It is not the world that sloweth, lad. It is the Captain who will not hurry. The moment he chose was the moment that came."* Strategy, not skill.
- **How the Book names it** (*changed in v3*). Only in I.3, and only as something men say, never as a rule: *In the laying the hours go slow, for stone is heavy, and in the hauling slower. In the holding an hour is gone in a heartbeat.*; *"For the Captain, an hour lasteth as long as a day."*; and Hale counting the ripples down a sail, *Each, I think, was a minute.* v2.0's *A day has four-and-twenty hours in it* is gone with the rest of the arithmetic. The Book gives no numbers and names no mechanic. No text of any kind appears during a live Fight.
- **The battery and the gun.** A battery is a group of guns, not one gun (admiral doc §12). Twelve True Men matches the late cap of twelve guns, so no battery lacks a True Man even if every gun stood alone; that is a fit, not a rule, and the Book states no number of guns. In the first year there is one battery, *Kael's guns, that are yet all we have*.

### 5.12 Other surfaces, and the art hooks

- **Personas.** *Ask Seren* (not *Ask the Emissary*: the murdered Guest must not share a title with a helper). *Ask Halyna* answers in two voices, a line each, and says *we two*, never *Halyna*. Recommended: a persona answers in the register of the player's tab (§7.2 call 19).
- **The lore screen** is *Open the Book*.
- **Store art and screenshots** use the Title of Liberty, drawn in the Captain's coal hand with the English beneath. Never the last line of the game, never the sliver's sign, never *We remember* or *We remember our home too.*
- **Achievements.** *Halyna's Blessing* (not *Rhyna's*). *Pacifist* may add one line, never a leaf: *"Once the wall held and not one hull was sunk. Halyna said nothing for a long while."* The rungs that translate the native hands are in §5.7.
- **Campaign cards.** The 330 quotes and the soldier roster are recast in a separate pass, in the cadence of the stone leaves, shortened for a card.
- **Dispatches.** Between sorties, on the Deploy map's state-read beat (admiral doc §9c), never during timed Build or a live Fight, one line may tell what the fleet learned of the player *in that siege*, in a lookout's voice or Halyna's, from the fleet's own record: *"They marked that the north holds its fire."* · *"They know we move the guns they find."* · *"They know we kill the bearers first."* A dispatch says only what the fleet has learned, never what it will do, and it forgets with the siege. It is what is seen, as a runner carries it.
- **Art hooks.**
  - The grown hulls are grey-green; every keep flies a crimson pennant, the colour of the torn cloak (*Crimson is the pennant on this Keep, and on every keep we raise.*); the answering sail blanches pale, *grey-green to white, like a man's face*.
  - The root-men are bound bundles of grey-green living root that walk and knot together in fours (*Off the transports the root-men came like spilled kindling.*). The crown-hulls are broad hulls whose crown opens; the winged seed has membranous wings; the husks fly with it, paler and empty; the great seed rides alone under its own bladder.
  - **The Captain's half-cloak, by stage:** to the knees at first launch; shorter by a hand's breadth at each wall death (Cam 2, 9, 16, 25, 29, 32 complete, Rimewatch, the Epilogue; the Rimewatch strip is §7.2 call 3); at Count VI *it cannot reach his belt*; the last strip is *the last of his half*, whatever remains; after it, a plain grey cloak.
  - **The Captain's hood:** up in every hearth scene; back only at IV.3's interstitial and at the Epilogue (§5.4).
  - **Crimson strips on pikes:** over the east cairns (Jory, Corlen, Wick, Pellow), on the east face over Ebba, over the north stone (Enrella), over the mere at Rimewatch (Hesk, by the call), and over the inner gate (Halyna).
  - **The north lamp** on the north wall, lit every dusk: by Hesk, then by the fisher-girl of the east keep.
  - **Two figures at every Stonewright post**, working as one; *look again. Ye will see two.*
  - **Each battery's lookout** with one of Pellow's far-glasses.
  - **The runners' stone** in the ward, a fallen block by the inner gate.
  - **The tale-stone** in its niche of the hearth-wall, face down, with hands on it.
  - **The sending boat's keyed stem**, the two halves of the first heart, with Halyna's mark across the join; the green sliver on moss in the bow, with the coin and the two chisels.
  - **The staff** in the gatehouse corner, under its dust; at the end, a clean stroke in the dust where it stood.
  - **The three grey stones** over the Long Hearth after the homecomings, the lowest scarred by old pipes.
  - **The inner gate standing open at night** after the seventh spring.

### 5.13 The builder's markers

These are HTML comments and fence blocks in the Book's source, and do not render. Plain Words carries every one of them in the same places (§1.11).

| Marker | Where | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `{{Name}}` | every pair-name | draw the lintel: one thin stroke over the name, the width of the name; in plain text the stroke drops |
| the dateline | the first italic line under a `###` heading (and under `## OF THIS BOOK`) | *v3:* a short sentence-case line, eight words or fewer; the builder must read it as the dateline and the next italic paragraph as the headnote |
| `<!-- PROLOGUE EXCERPT BEGIN -->` … `END` | I.1, from *The True Men looked to their Captain.* | the first-launch prologue (§5.2) |
| `<!-- FACING LEAF: filled by the Epilogue -->` | after I.1 | the leaf facing I.1 shows the Stone's unmarked rings and Seren's line until the Epilogue is laid |
| `<!-- READING -->` | after the headnote of every wood leaf (II.2, IV.2, IV.4, IV.6, V.3, V.4, V.6, VI.2) | *new in v3:* Halyna's reading; italic em-dash lines with two-space line breaks, the two inks alternating line by line, unmarked; it runs to *This is held in the grain.* |
| `<!-- HALYNA -->` … `<!-- HALYNA END -->` | IV.3's reading of Brenn; II.3 whole; and every split pair of Halyna's in another leaf (19 blocks) | alternate the two inks, paragraph by paragraph or half by half. *v3:* no longer on wood leaves |
| `<!-- MARKED -->` | II.3 | each paragraph opens with its teller's name; the italic lines are Seren's ink; the opening halves and *Birth united them…* take the two inks or neither as §5.8 says |
| `<!-- RING -->` | every wood leaf, twice | a short grain-rule between rings |
| `<!-- W -->`, `<!-- W: … only -->` | V.2 (the moved first carving, and the italic carvings only); V.7 (three carvings, and the italic line of Part II); VI.3 (*We remember our home too.*; *The Two Homes*, line 3 only) | wood-role text inside a stone leaf |
| `[⟦legend_naelear_chip⟧]<!-- RIPENS: VI.3 -->` | II.2 | the untold name: the sign, with its `::: note` caption, until VI.3 is laid; then *Naelear* beside it in Seren's hand |
| `<!-- RIPENS: II.2's untold name -->` | VI.3 | the moment that ripens II.2 |
| `<!-- TOKEN {LAST_THRONE_AT_HAVEN} -->` | VI.3 | the Throne the player felled last, read aloud as its true name and its haven (below) |
| `<!-- MIXED: … -->` | the Epilogue | stone letters on wood that became stone: the stone face with the wood tint |
| `::: native id kind` … `:::` | wherever a drawing stands | caption line, then the fold; kinds are `line`, `chip`, `round`, `stone` (§5.6) |
| `::: note` … `:::` | after a line holding `⟦id⟧` | the caption and fold for a drawing set inside a line of text (II.2; IV.2) |
| a bold-only paragraph | V.5 (**I · The Hasty Tide** …), V.7 (**Part I · …**), VI.1 (**Eldhythe.** …), the Knowings (**The Last Note · …**) | a part head; in VI.1 the italic line under it is the who-line (*Voss, for Corlen.*) |
| `facing …` in the Contents | II.1, IV.1, IV.3, IV.5, V.2, V.4, V.5, VI.1, I.1, the Epilogue | the facing spreads; *v3:* the Contents carries the facing rule, and no headnote does |

**The builder's gaps** (from the v3 editor's log, for `wf10/book/build_story_html.py` and `story.css`):
- it stops on `<!-- READING -->` as an unknown marker;
- it reads the first italic paragraph after a title as the teller line, so the v3 dateline is taken for the headnote;
- `<!-- HALYNA -->` sets `data-told="halyna"` on the unit, which `story.css` does not style, so it only inks the halves a and b; the split pairs outside a cue need the markers, which v3 now gives them;
- it must align the tabs by the shared anchors (§5.8), not by paragraph;
- IV.2's `::: note` English should show collapsed until the leaf has been read (w01's request).

**The token** completes the sentence *All that day lay the tenth Throne where it fell, black of grain and split open, a hill of timber higher than a harbour-house. It was {LAST_THRONE_AT_HAVEN}.* The wood's things are written by their own names (the foreword), so the token gives the true name and the place, never v1.3's English name or a bare code:

| The last Throne felled | Token text |
|---|---|
| Eldhythe | *Thaesaen, in the harbour of Eldhythe* |
| Tidesmeet | *Leavaren, in the river at Tidesmeet* |
| Fenholm | *Vaelress, in the fen at Fenholm* |
| Holtward | *Ralensaen, among the trees of Holtward* |
| Carnhold | *Rhenvael, among the cairns of Carnhold* |
| Emberhythe | *Esthaer, on the shore of Emberhythe* |
| Sandreach | *Senneir, under Sandreach* |
| Rimewatch | *Eirlenth, on the mere at Rimewatch* |
| Highreach | *Naelthar, on the crags of Highreach* |
| Glasspire | *Neivaere, in the cellars of Glasspire* |

The tenth Throne lies *split open* and Halyna go in. VI.1 has several Thrones sink, crumble or crust over; the token assumes the last one fell where a hand could go in (§7.2 call 20).

### 5.14 Ripples into other documents

- **The admiral doc §12, and the Legends delivery references in it:** *by horn* becomes *by his own voice, at the battery's side*; the runners are his eyes. Its links to v1.3's III.2 (the Thrones) go to VI.2.
- **The delivery map's own references** (v1.3 §7 and the §9 batteries row) are superseded by §5.11 and §6.
- **The Original** (*The Legends, in Their Own Hands*) must be set down anew from v3, every leaf (K2). Its I.3 (*lo et gomm*, the horn) and the lexicon's *gommharm* ("a horn-call") go; the lexicon already has *movenn*, "a banner, a sign flown", and the Tongues team chooses the words for the Captain's call at the battery's side. The grain of every wood leaf is set ring by ring; the readings are Halyna's English pieces and take no grain of their own.
- **MAP §4.1:** *the Captain's Stonwryt under the new capstone* becomes *Halyna's mark and the Title*. The marks a player can match are added (§5.6).
- **The Tongues doc §2.8 call-out, revised:** item 1 (the Fore on the chest lid) is replaced by Aldwena's Stonwryt (if its drawing echoes the Fore's shape, that is a hint only, and never a holy sign); item 3 is dropped; item 4's lines must not say *first tongue* (J25). No pair's mark is drawn as a cross. The Tongues doc's links into the old delivery map move as §5.0 says.
- **The battle-message lord's line:** *"My fathers sold their black pillars by the cartload…"* (§5.10).
- **The Fiction Hooks** still carry the old Throne and root names.
- **The UI:** *Ask Halyna*; *Halyna's Blessing*; *Open the Book*.
- **Plain Words** is `wf11/plain_v3.md`, the second tab inside the story page. The separate *The Legends, in Plain Words* (v1.3) and `wf10/plain_v2.md` are superseded.
- **The builders:** the story builder must learn the v3 frame (§5.13); the Notes builder (`wf10/notes_build/build_notes_html.py`) must take this document's new section numbers.
- **The working guides:** `wf11/voice_v3.md` §4 quotes some lines in forms the Book later changed (Corlen's *has*, Hesk's *has to*, the ship-masters' *The shore laughs* and *comes for you when you die*), and `wf11/craft.md` quotes *"Hull hangs back."* and *"Wait. The sea has not finished."* The Book's forms win (§1.2); if the guides are used again, they should follow it.
- **The outline** (`wf9/outline.md`): the lines §1.7 lists are retired.
- **The art list:** the half-cloak by stage, the strips, the hood up, the north lamp and the fisher-girl, the stem, the lookouts, the tale-stone, the grey cloak (§5.12).

## 6 · THE NAMES

*How the Book holds each name. "The Book" here is v3.0.0. Names marked "NOTES only" are never said in a tale; v3 moved several more there (§2.4).*

**The peoples and the land**

| Name | What it is | How it is used |
|---|---|---|
| **the Shorelanders · the Rivenmen** | the people, before and after the Naming | *Our fathers we call Shorelanders, for so they were, and ourselves but* we. (I.5). The wood calls them *the shore-men*, and the late carvings *Rhenear*. |
| **the Shorelands · Rivenkeep** | the land; the hold on its ridge | *Every haven the grey sails hold is Rivenkeep, and the edge of the sea is the edge of the Keep. It is a vow.* (I.5). The eldest murmur *"the Shorelands"* into their shawls yet. |
| **the ten havens** | Eldhythe (the Eldest Haven), Tidesmeet (where the river meeteth the tide), Fenholm (the town that was always sinking), Holtward (the keep under the canopy), Carnhold (the stronghold of the cairns), Emberhythe (the forge on the burning shore), Sandreach (the citadel carved in the canyon wall), Rimewatch (the watch on the frozen mere), Highreach (the tower of the floating stones), Glasspire (the last and proudest) | Each epithet stands at its haven's head in III.1. A haven need not stand on a harbour (Ulden's line; the reason is NOTES only). v1.3's *the ten harbour-cities* is no longer said in the Book. |
| **the Mystaeri** | the people behind the grey | the Rivenmen's word: *So we called it the ghost fleet, and its senders the Mystaeri, after the mist.* (III.2). Their own name, **Naelear**, *those of the breath*, is first given in VI.3 (*Mystaeri is but the word of the shore.*) and then fills the untold word of II.2. Never *Mystborn*. |
| **the Captain · the Commander** | the player, never named | The Rite names him *Commander of Rivenkeep* (I.4) and the wall uses the title; *In his own heart he was still only the Captain.* v2.0's *the Captain of the Torn Cloak* is no longer said in the Book. |
| **the warden** | the master of the outpost | never *commander*; his name is always ▒▒▒▒ in stone; in wood he is *the master of that house*, *the host*. |
| **the Trade Council** | the havens' governing merchants | sent out the axes; shelved the warning; kept the letter; *the Council named it fortune*; the warden *held the post by his father's name on the Trade Council*. |

**The wood**

| Name | What it is | How it is used |
|---|---|---|
| **the wood · the Mystwood** | their living wood: twisted boughs for rites, pale saplings for song, silver elders for council, black pillars at the edge | The Book says *the wood*. *Mystwood* stands only in Jack's line, *But Mystwood remembers.* (IV.5). It is not read; it is **known**. One life in two kinds: *From the first the long-lived guided the wood, and the wood breathed out the sky they lived by.* |
| **the Mystholders · the Naelsaen** | the black pillars at the fog's edge, the anchors of the mist | *Mystholders* is the ship-masters' name (IV.1). The wood says *our pillars* and *the Naelsaen, the mist-hearts: the anchors of our sky* (II.2). They regrew after the felling until the cutting of the regrowth made them stop; that is why the mist thinned. |
| **the grey · the fog · the breath · the Mystlands** | the living mist | The Rivenmen say *the grey* and *the fog*; Elspet says *the Mystlands* (Jack's own word, kept; IV.5). The wood says *the breath*. |
| **the silver elders** | the council-trees | the wood's name: *none lie beneath a silver elder*, save the Aelvaren's keel among their roots. *Silverbark*, the wall's grain-name, is NOTES only now. |
| **Ael'thar** | the holiest rite of fellowship: gift, blood and brow | *In the thunder of his own tongue he spake the name of the rite, Ael'thar* (IV.4). The rite is told as it happens: the gift set down kneeling; the hand on the neck and the foreheads together; a shallow cut on the host's arm, and then on the guest's own; the arms laid together, *that harm to the one should be harm to both*. The soft carved form *Aelthar* is NOTES only now. |
| ***ael*** | *the joining* (NOTES only) | the one hint of the two tongues' kinship to reach the hearth (V.7 Part I), and v3 no longer glosses it. Nothing more is said (J25). |
| **the Aelvaren, the Bearer of the Binding** | the Guest's boat | *proud of the name* (IV.6); the ship-masters' burning boat (IV.5); the sending turns as it turned (VI.3). |
| **Aelrhen · the Guest** | the emissary | *Aelrhen he was named when he took his errand: bond-to-stone.* (IV.4). Brenn says *the stranger*. The Rivenmen say *the Guest* and *the grey man of the old tale*. v2.0's *the Kneeling Guest* is no longer said in the Book. |
| **the Mystarchs · the Thrones** | the ten eldest pillars, grown into thrones and seated in the ten fallen havens | *The wall named them Mystarchs, and the soldiers the Thrones.* (V.5, Count VI); Ebba says *the last Mystarch* (I.5). Each has a fear-name (NOTES and the game only), an English name the wall used at its homecoming, and a true name (below). |
| **the long-lived · the children of the long-lived** | the Mystaeri, in the wood's mouth | the wood's own words for them. |
| **the council · the grove · the growing** | the council under the silver elders; the vote; the fleets | *No roof it had but the boughs, nor floor but the moss.* (V.4). *The Sending* is these notes' name for VI.3's deed. |
| **the bond of roots** | how the heart's orders go to every hull | V.6: *Through the bond of roots we carved them on into every hull about us, and every hull obeyed.* · *The long-lived made that bond into a leash.* Its wood name, *Aelralen*, is NOTES only now. |
| **the Last Carver** | the last carver in the groves | Seren's name for him: *The Last Carver I call him, for I think that when he cut this there was no other.* He never sailed. His fate is untold. |
| **Rhenear** | *the stone-deaf*, the late carvings' name for the Rivenmen | V.7 Part II: *Small in that carving was cut their word for us.* It shares *-ear*, *those of*, with *Naelear*. |
| **Myststone** | stone that was wood | the Epilogue: *Myststone the Rivenmen call it, and say it remembereth all.* *Stone does not translate* still holds for every other stone. |

**The Thrones**

| Haven | Fear-name (the soldiers', NOTES and game only) | Grain (NOTES only) | The wall's English name (VI.1) | True name (VI.2) | Its judgement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Eldhythe | the Leviathan | Heartoak | the Tide-Heart | **Thaesaen**, the tide-heart | *Give them the middle; take the sides; close the hand.* (the Wave's carving, carried whole) |
| Tidesmeet | the Hydra | Ironbark | the Bearer of New Branches | **Leavaren**, bearer of new branches | *Strike one head; two will answer.* |
| Fenholm | the Plague Herald | Yew | the Grain That Rots | **Vaelress**, the grain that rots | *Rot the wall; wait for the mending to fail.* |
| Holtward | the Ancient Treant *(§7.2 call 4)* | Ironbark | the Root-Heart | **Ralensaen**, root-heart | *Go under what you cannot go through.* |
| Carnhold | the Colossus | Blackthorn | the Stone-Grain | **Rhenvael**, stone-grain | *Every gun that speaks, the mountain answers.* |
| Emberhythe | the Magma Titan | Twistbough | the Burning | **Esthaer**, the burning | *Burn as you go; let them chase the fire.* |
| Sandreach | the Sandworm | Willow | the Deep-Beneath | **Senneir**, the deep-beneath | *Show them the host; come up beneath the stone.* |
| Rimewatch | the Frost Wyrm *(§7.2 call 4)* | Yew | the Long Winter | **Eirlenth**, the long winter | *Wait in the white; strike the stone that cannot mend.* |
| Highreach | the Storm King | Ashwood | the Blood of the Sky | **Naelthar**, blood of the sky | *Come as the young wood came.* (the Lone Stroke's carving, carried whole) |
| Glasspire | the Crystal Lich | Silverbark | the Silvered | **Neivaere**, the silvered, the mirror | *Show them themselves.* |

*v3 makes Tidesmeet's English name the Bearer of New Branches in VI.1 and the Knowings, to meet VI.2's gloss.*

**The Tides and the grains**

| The wall's name | The wood's name | What the wood of it holds (V.6's Song) |
|---|---|---|
| the Hasty Tide (W1) | *Leathae* (NOTES only) | *The green wood holds one word.* |
| the Second Tide (W2) | *Vaelthae* (NOTES only) | *The wood of forty summers holds a word, and waits for its brother.* |
| the Tide of Remembering (W3) | *Ralenthae*, *the tide the roots taught* (V.3) | *The wood of a shore-man's life holds a sentence, and a wall remembered in it.* |
| the Joined Tide (W4) | *Aelthae* (V.6, V.7) | one sentence across many hulls |
| the Tide that Learned Deceit (W5) | *Rhenthae* (NOTES only) | *The wood of a long life holds the morrow, and carves it, and waits, and carves again.* |
| the Last Tide (W6) | *Eirthae* (NOTES only) | whole plans with turnings, and a home clause |
| the Thrones | *Naelsaen* | *one thing only now… and it is grief* |

- **The grains**, in the wall's names and in the order of their tempers (NOTES only; V.6 keeps the tempers as griefs, with no names): the green wood (*too young to have a way*), Ironbark (comes again heavier where it failed), Willow (turns from the first failure), Blackthorn (goes where it lost the most), Heartoak (never turns aside), Twistbough (plays no trick twice), Yew (waits, and burns, and waits again), Ashwood (throws all it has at once), Silverbark (knows you best, and bends least). The tempers as tells are mechanics, and live in the fleet design.

**The fleet, as the wall names it**

| Name | What it is | How it is used |
|---|---|---|
| **the grey sails · the ghost fleet · the drums** | the soldiers' words for what they saw | No one stands on the decks and no one is aboard (*No one saileth in us.*, V.6). The drums are the knocking out of the grey, *like thunder, on and on*. A sail blanches pale when its hull wakes to another's deed. |
| **the Standard-Bearer · the bearer · the heart** | the fleet's chain of command: the bearer hull, the heart in it that carves each morrow, the bond that carries it to every hull | *When we sank a bearer, another took up the standard, and the fleet took on the new bearer's temper.* (Count II). *Sethvaren* and *Saenvael* are NOTES only. The fleet design calls the carving heart the Heartwood and its orders carvings. **The heart is not a person: the ship is the commander.** |
| **the root-men · treelings** | the warriors of the landings, grown of root | *Root-men* is the Book's word; *treelings* is the wall's slang (*Treelings the wall called them, as if a name could make them small.*; battle messages). *What those roots touched, the wood knew. They were the wood's hands.* |
| **the crown-hulls · the winged seed · a seed-fall** | the carriers, their bombers, a bombing run | *Then came the crown-hulls, and their winged seed, to wherever our guns were quiet.* (Count II). The husks are NOTES and art only now. |
| **the tablets** | the soldiers' word for the planks in the vault | *The wall called them the tablets. They were but planks.* (V.2) |

**The guild and the bond**

| Name | What it is | How it is used |
|---|---|---|
| **the Stonewrights** | the guild of master-builders and keepers of the faith | *Build, do not destroy. Protect, do not attack.* No Stonewright ever bore a weapon. |
| **the Stonwryt · the Stonwryt coin** | each bonded pair's own sacred mark; the coin struck under master-pairs' marks, part gold and part black stone | *The Stonwryt the masters call it: a vow sealed in living rock, earned by toil and skill* · *trust, sealed in stone* (II.1). Never a holy sign. The coin goes into the sending boat (VI.3). |
| **the Aetherbond · the Bonded** | the joining of two Stonewrights, made by God before birth, read in the cradle, made whole at the sealing | *the Bonded*, capitalised, is the guild's name for every bonded pair; the adjective stays lower case (*a bonded pair*). *When the left hand dies, does not the right one die too?* (II.3). |
| **the sealing · the pair-name** | the wedding of a bonded pair, when the guild gives *a name for the mouth, and a mark for the stone* | Six pair-names: **Halyna**, **Aldwena**, **Idrenna**, **Orvenna**, **Enrella**, **Wendhessa**. A pair-name stands under a lintel on the page and always takes a plural verb (§2.2, J2). |
| **Halyna · Halvard Stone-Warden · Rhyna of the Inner Gate · the Bonded of the Keep · Elder and Elderess** | the elders of the guild at the Keep, husband and wife | the pair-name is the guild's and the hearth's; in a scene the cradle-names do the work. *Elder Halvard am I, and she is Elderess Rhyna, and neither is the elder of the other.* (II.3). |
| **the unbonded · the third** | a guild child whose cradle-mate died before the sealing, given to a bonded hearth as its third | *The unbonded keep one name, and among the guild one name is a mourning.* The unbonded have no mark. |
| **Seren Two-Inks · Tarnel · Tarnard** | the scribe of the Book, Halyna's third; her cradle-mate, a boatwright's son of Tidesmeet; his father, the eldest of the boatwrights | *Seren* means *a single sorrow that overcomes* (NOTES only). *Two-Inks* for the two inks she keeps for Halyna, and in the Epilogue she writes *in one ink*. *Tarnel* means *little net* (NOTES only). |
| **the Theoliths · the Covenant of Completion** | the old master-pairs who teach; the vow they name to children | Halyna's Theoliths are Idrenna. *God taketh not the one without leaving the other a work to do.* |
| **the Knowing · a knowing · the Book of Knowings** | Halyna's rite of touching the wreck-wood; what the wood gives; the appendix | a knowing is given whole, as memory; the telling is the hard part, and Halyna give it *in pieces*. *The Knowing* is also the debrief line's in-world name. Anything read with the eyes (the Title, the slates, the rolls, a root sign, the Stone) is still *read*. |
| **the reading** | Halyna's pieces at the head of a wood leaf | these notes' name for the `<!-- READING -->` block (§1.5); the Book never names it. |

**The wall, its frame and its things**

| Name | What it is | How it is used |
|---|---|---|
| **the True Men · the Twelve** | the Captain's company, each the leader of a troop and of a battery | *We kept our oaths, and built what we promised, and fought never but in a just cause. That is the whole of a True Man.* (I.1). The roll: Stannard of the ridge; Corlen of the harbour; Tamm of the three spans; Marl the fen-born; Aske of the canopy; Garvel of the smithies; Harl of the burning shore; Ulden of the galleries; Hesk of the mere; Lanner of the crags; Pellow of the spire; and Kael the Counter, their oath-keeper, of no haven. Raised later: Voss who asked (Corlen's battery) and Ruan of the spire (Pellow's). *fourteen names* |
| **the batteries · the three calls** | groups of guns, each under a True Man; *Shift your ground* · *Change your shot* · *Hold, or loose* | called by the Captain's own voice at the battery's side, one battery at a time; never in *thou*. In the first year, one battery, Kael's. |
| **the runners · the runners' oath · the runners' stone** | the men who carry what is seen; their oath; the fallen block in the ward | *What I saw, I carry. I carry nothing else.*, and from IV.3, *Not as Brenn carried.* *Every haven had its runners' stone.* Hale and Wick are runners, not True Men. |
| **the six companies** | the soldiers who walked the long retreat | *soldiers of six broken companies, that knew their officers no more*; *a troop to each of us* (I.1). Voss was a corporal of them. |
| **the Long Hearth · the tale-stone · *laid* · *We remember*** | the frame | the tale-stone is the old capstone of the inner gate in its niche. A tale is *laid*, not told: *A tale that is but told is gone by morning. A tale that is laid is part of the wall.* The answer comes after every stone leaf, and after a wood leaf first in VI.3. *Tumar*, the Orrowen for *We remember*, is also how Orrowen says yes (NOTES only). |
| **the banner · the torn cloak · the half-cloak** | the written half on its pike; the unwritten half on the Captain's back | the banner is the hinge of the clock. The strips are cut from the half-cloak (§3.8). |
| **the Title · the Title of Liberty** | *In memory of our home, our families, our God, our freedoms, our peace.* | Jack's fixed line. In the course-hand: *Ul dumol ol varn, ol theldeth, ol Mardh, ol lunnath, ol sollan.* |
| **the black chest · the archive chest** | the founders' chest of black stone at the foot of the shaft; the guild's chest out of Eldhythe | two names only, never confused (§4.2). |
| **the founders' leaf · the youngest apprentice's leaf · the slates** | Aldwena's leaf of the raising; the leaf of the hearth's laying of tales; the Rite and the slates of building for battle | all from the black chest; all in the Hal. v2.0's *the hearth leaf* is these notes' name only. |
| **the staff · the staffs** | the first staff, *capped with the finest polished ashlar*; the havens' staffs | the law of the staff (§2.2, J10). |
| **granite-bloom · bloom** | the pale crust on worked stone untended for lives of men | *Bloom the masons call the pale crust…, for it flowereth where none come.* |
| **the east cairns · the north stone · the north lamp** | the Keep's burial ground on the east face; Enrella's grave in the north breach; Hesk's lamp, then the fisher-girl's | the rites of mourning (§3.8). |
| **the lintel of the western stair** | where the quarry-wardens cut their names, one beneath another | Kael swears by it (I.1). Halvard's is the last name, cut by Halyna (the Epilogue). |
| **the vault** | *the oldest room under this hall, where the Rite was found* | where the planks lie, *and whoso will may go down and lay a hand on them* (V.2). |
| **the ages** | the First Days; the Days of the Havens; the Harvest; the Summer of the Guest; the Forgetting; the Fall; the Road; the banner; the Fourteen Nights; the Going-Down; the Sending; the Years After | §3.2; the Book says only a few of these names (*the First Days*, *the Harvest rolls*, *the Fall*, *the Road*, *the Fourteen Nights*). |

**The hands and the tongues** (NOTES only, unless said)

| Name | What it is |
|---|---|
| **Orrowen** | the Shoreland tongue. The Book never names it; it says *our tongue* |
| **the course-hand** (*Garl Dhrenn*) | the chisel alphabet that stands on a mortar line, each word a stone. The Book's captions say *the course-hand* |
| **the leaf-hand** (*Garl Flenn*) | the ink hand, in which Seren writes the whole tongue, every small word |
| **the Hal** | the first builders' old hand, read for its meaning and not sounded. The foreword's drawing names it |
| **the grain** (*Eilseth*) | the wood's hand, holding meaning and not sound. The Book says *the grain* |
| **the thunder-tongue** (*Seilrhass*) | the Mystaeri's speech: *Our speech is the storm in the high boughs, all cracks and breaks.* (II.2) |
| **the dual** | the Orrowen verb ending (*-ana*) a pair-name takes; *we two* translates it |
| **Halvard's gloss** | *Halvard*, "bedrock-warden"; *Stone-Warden*, *tolmvard* |
| **the first-tongue hints** | *Tumar* and *thein*; *Aelrhen* and *Rhyna*; *-vard* and *-varen*; *senn*; *Brenn*; and *ael*'s meaning. Never told in a tale (J25) |

**Names kept in NOTES only:** Aldun and Bena (Aldwena's cradle-names); Idlan and Denna (Idrenna's); Wendel and Tessa (Wendhessa's); *Sethvaren*, *Saenvael* and *Aelralen*; *Leathae*, *Vaelthae*, *Rhenthae* and *Eirthae*; the soldiers' fear-names for the Thrones and the wall's grain-names; *Tumar*; the soft form *Aelthar*; the meaning of *ael*; the hands' and tongues' own names above; the meanings of *Seren* and *Tarnel*.

**Originality.** Jack, 2026-09-30: *"No more name or word checks are necessary. Overlap is just overlap now."* No name or word audit was run for v3, and none is owed. Only an unmistakable trademark lift would be flagged. The flags v2.0 recorded are kept here for the record and need no action: *the Ancient Treant* and *the Frost Wyrm* (game fear-names only now, and still a call for Jack, §7.2 call 4); *Highreach* and *Sandreach*; *Kael* beside *Ael'thar*; and *Orvenna*, a soft flag only.

## 7 · SETTLED AND STILL-OPEN CALLS

### 7.1 Settled

**Jack's notes of 2026-10-02, as applied in v3.0.0.**
- K1 to K9, as §1.1 records them, and the memory theme (§1.9).
- The register: the v1.3 voice and sentence-craft, made hard by heavy old English, never by length (§1.2). The 2026-09-30 note on long joined sentences is superseded for sentence length, and so is writing for the voice.
- The custom of burning is gone from the Book and from these notes; the burning is the warden's order, to hide the deed (§1.7, §1.8).
- Brenn only watched (§1.7).
- Plain Words is a true retelling (§1.10).
- One switch for the tabs; the Original back in every section; the shared copy is the whole story (§1.11).

**The calibration's rulings** (§1.2; `voice_v3.md` §9), which the writers inherited:
- Register A, HEAVY, by the judges' votes A 3, B 1, C 0. B (cosmetic archaism) and C (native words in the prose) are declined (§7.3).
- One past form to a verb, Book-wide; *wist* for a fact; *mine* before a vowel or *h*; one *-eth* to a sentence at most; plain words at the hinge.
- The True Men's refrains are under the dial (*Wait. The sea hath not finished.* · *Someone must keep a light.*); fixed matter is outside it; the three calls are never put into *thou*; the warden's letter is the one modern register in the Book.
- The burning is the warden's order, *Leave naught to show*, and the rhyme keeps his words; Tobe says nothing.
- The turn keeps IV.5's lock: the boat comes with *no wind* and goes *Into the wind … and against the current*. *Which no boat can do* is the ship-masters' wonder in IV.5, not Brenn's.
- The Guest's own cut is seen in IV.3 and named only in IV.4. The wood's scream is literal.
- I.1's roll stays bare, and each man's tag lives whole in III.1 (all eleven are carried there).
- Ebba's first *We remember* and Idrenna's leave to break Wendhessa's seal moved to the foreword. I.1's loaf picture is left out.

**Settled from Notes v1.0.0's open calls:**
- **The Invocation's register** (v1.0.0 call 1). It follows the Book: heavy grammar, every picture and key phrase kept (*Whoso holdeth it now is the teller.* · *the building and the breaking* · *we have kept both* · *Hold the stone. Lay the tale. When it is laid, the hearth will answer.*), with *Many hands have held it, and many shall.* new. Plain Words tells it plainly from v3's own Invocation. v1.3's wording stays on record as the fallback: *Sit, if there is room, and stand if there is not. The guns are cooling, and the mortar will not set before dark. / Here is the hearth, and here is the stone that fell from the inner gate. Whoever holds it tells. / We tell these tales because we forgot once. Our fathers forgot a keep, and it crumbled. Their fathers forgot a wrong, and it came back to us under grey sails. So we tell all of it: the building and the breaking, and what we did as well as what was done to us. / Some of these tales are ours, and they are cut plain, like the stones of the wall. Some are the wood's, and those are hard to tell, and will not be hurried. Where the two do not agree, we have kept both. / Hold the stone. Lay the tale. When it is laid, the hearth will answer.*
- **Length** (call 2): K6 answers it; every leaf is under its ceiling (§1.3).
- **More impact moments than the outline's one to three** (call 3): one halt to a tale, with its strike lines (§1.4).
- **III.2's two added lines** (call 7): gone from the Book.
- **The prologue's length** (call 14): about 300 words; paced by beats; no fallback cut needed (§5.2).
- **Kael's opening oath** (call 12): it ends *What I told not, that will I not tell.*, on one syllable, as his other oaths do.
- **The [may] calls v2.0 took, kept in v3:** the cradle-rhyme; Voss's *Child have I none of mine own, and yet I said* our; Kael's *I have no haven*; Harl carrying the bow to the tide; the *Burn* plank laid on the black crust at Emberhythe; *Two at the Gate* sung that night with the first voice silent; the inner gate standing open at night; the north lamp lit by the child.
- **The [may] call v2.0 took and v3 dropped:** the soldier's brand quenched in the sea (K8; §1.8).
- **The two children** (R6b's open question 4): one child. The fisher-girl of the east keep, Ebba's great-granddaughter, lights the north lamp in VI.1, answers the wood in VI.3, lays the first hand on the boat that came back, and keeps the light at the Epilogue's close. The small girl of II.3 is another child, unnamed, and needs no payoff.
- **Tobe** (R6b's open question 3): kept, for the poles and his thirty years, with no saying.
- **The Epilogue's fire beat** (R6b's open question 2): dropped.
- **The wood leaves' form** (R6b's open question 1): Halyna's reading, then Seren's story in the wood's *we* (§1.5).
- **Brenn's line from Jack's own words** (R6b's open question 5): *I did not stop them. A boy was I among the men.*; *the youngest of the twelve* is not added beside *Nineteen I was*.
- ***Hold the stone, Seren.*** is bare Book-wide; its whole form is said once (I.4).
- **The split pairs:** every pair of Halyna's halves is inside a HALYNA block wherever it stands; other pairs' halves stay in the leaf's ink; the Epilogue's remembered halves stay unwrapped, in one ink.

**Jack's notes of 2026-09-30,** as v2.0 applied them and v3 keeps or supersedes them (§2).

**Jack's ruling of 2026-09-30 on originality:** *"No more name or word checks are necessary. Overlap is just overlap now."* No audit is run or owed (§6).

**Jack's answers of 2026-09-27, still standing.**
- **Three tabs** at every tale and at the foreword, the Invocation, the Epilogue and the Book of Knowings: *The Book*, *Plain Words*, *Original* (§5.8); and now one switch for all of them (K1).
- **Tier 3:** the whole Book set down in its own tongues (to be done anew from v3; K2).
- ***Seren*** means *a single sorrow that overcomes*.
- **The Latin cross is fine** as the Bar Before's drawn shape, and both tongues may come from one first tongue; J25 adds that the kinship is only hinted and that no cross-sign is ever a holy sign.
- ***Tumar***, *We remember*, is Orrowen's yes.
- **The chisel's economy and the grain's rings** belong to the Tongues doc.

**Jack's answers of 2026-09-26, still standing.**
- **The sending** is kept and seaworthy: Tarnard lays the keel and the boatwrights build it of the vault's planks, Orvenna key the stem, Halyna cut their mark across it, and *A day it rode off the point in a hard swell, and took no water.* (VI.3).
- **The Bonded's death together** is kept (II.3, V.7 Part I, the Epilogue).
- **"The stone-deaf"** is kept, as *Rhenear* (V.7 Part II).
- **The Last Carver** is kept, and he never sails.
- **The proposed names** are kept, with *Naelear* spelled Jack's way. *Who are the unbonded?* is answered in II.3.
- **The Whispers** were given free rein (§5.9).
- **The smaller calls:** the torn cloak and every pennant are crimson; the Mystaeri live two and a half times as long as the Shorelanders; the UI currency sounds like *Stonewright* when spoken.
- **The assumed rulings:** the Thrones were not in the Fall (*Late came we. It was not we that burned your havens.*); no Mystaeri sail; Myststone is stone that was wood; the battles are fought along the line of keeps; Rhyna's cry whole; the dead heart's hour late; *But Mystwood remembers* in Voss's mouth; there is no admiral and no one aboard.
- **The Mystlands** kept as Jack's own word; **faith is never the butt of a joke**.

### 7.2 Still open, for Jack (each reversible)

*Each is applied in the Book or in these notes as recommended, and each can be undone in a line or a table row.*

1. **Brenn's laughter.** v1.3 and v2.0 had *I laughed. I have not laughed at anything since.* K7 says he only watched, so v3 reads *Then laughed his men. I stood among them, and I hear that laughter yet.*, and later, of the axemen, *I laughed not, for I had seen it turn. Yet I said naught.* **Recommended:** as the Book has it. **Fallback** (voice §9.1): v1.3's line returns, but only as a boy's echo of the men's laughter, and the axemen line (*I laughed not…*) changes to match.
2. **Sharing before the Original is set down** (K2, K3). The Original is owed (§1.11). **Recommended:** share the whole story page now, with *The Book* and *Plain Words* whole and the *Original* tab carrying every native drawing the Book holds, at its place; republish the same page as each leaf's Orrowen and grain are set down (the Book only adds). **Alternative:** wait until the Tongues pass has set the whole Book down.
3. **Hesk's strip.** v3's VI.1 ends Rimewatch on *He came home.*, and the strip that v2.0 told after it was cut, so the Book tells seven strips. **Recommended:** the art still shows a strip over the mere, and §3.8 counts eight; nothing in the Book contradicts it, and *It was the last of his half.* gives no number. **Alternatives:** restore v2.0's sentence after *He came home.*, which fits the v3 grammar as it stands: *That night the Captain cut a hand's breadth from his half of the cloak, and bound it on a pike over the mere.* (VI.1 has 50 words of room under its ceiling); or show seven strips in the art.
4. **The fear-names** *the Ancient Treant* and *the Frost Wyrm* are now game words only, in no leaf. **Recommended:** keep them, under Jack's ruling that overlap is just overlap. **Alternatives** (from v2.0): *the Root-Giant* and *the Rime-Serpent*, one word each in the battle messages.
5. **VI.1 calls each Throne by the English of its own name** (the Tide-Heart, the Bearer of New Branches…) and keeps the soldiers' fear-names out of the Book. The alternative is the fear-names in VI.1.
6. **VI.1 is laid at each haven's own fire**, on the night its Throne fell, with Kael's frame at the Long Hearth when the tenth was home. Fallback: lay every entry at the Long Hearth, recast the headnote's first sentence to say so, and change Voss's *They are in my pack tonight* to *I have carried them up the mountain*.
7. **VI.2's Eirlenth:** *At the last a small fire stood over us, where the hall had been.* It is Hesk's lamp, felt from under the ice, and it names nothing. The death ledger lists no VI.2 line for Hesk; it can be struck alone.
8. **Halyna cut Halvard's name** in the lintel of the western stair, together, *ere their chisels went into the grey* (the Epilogue). v2.0 had Halvard cut it himself. Another alternative: Stannard cuts it after their death.
9. **The new verses, for Jack's nod:** the masons' work-chant (I.2); the True Men's dawn drill, with the three calls word for word (I.3); Rhyna's mother's cradle-rhyme (I.4, four lines).
10. ***thee* in *"Hold it, and it will hold thee."*** (I.2 and the Epilogue): *thou* to one near hearer, a frightened child. If Jack holds the line fixed as *you*, it reverts in one word in both leaves. The Plain keeps *you*.
11. **Kael's *sloweth* and the wall's *lasteth*** (I.3). Jack loved v1.3's *"It is not the world that slows, lad."* The voice rule (no *-s* beside *-eth* in one mouth) made it *sloweth*. Revert both to v1.3's words if he prefers them.
12. ***The Captain standeth still.*** (I.3) is Hale's slip into the present at the run, at a strike line, where the hinge rule would want shared words. If the hinge rule should govern, it becomes *The Captain stood still.*, and the run's present ends a sentence sooner.
13. **Idrenna's prayer for the dead** begins *"God, thou madest these two one ere either drew breath."* (no Christian mark: *Lord* is gone), and is told whole once, over Enrella (V.7 Part I); at Ebba's grave it is told without its words, and the Epilogue no longer quotes it.
14. **The ship-masters' fourth saying** is an imperative: *Let the shore laugh, for the shore will never have to meet it.* The voice guide had *The shore laughs…*; in the Book's grammar that would be *The shore laugheth…*.
15. **Small stagings the writers added** inside the elements' frame, each reversible: the gull at the warden's door; Tobe's thirty years, and Tobe washing the poles; the boy at the front who counts twelve on his fingers; Seren singing *to the comma, and then … the rest*; the Captain coming in off the wall at his own Rite; the masons laying on below at *Hold*; Tamm's stones and Aske's hand on the wreck-wood at the hearth; the tellers laying hands on the stone in turn (III.2); Wick swearing the oath loudest; Merrick by the door, *and he is not gone out*; the first pillar, *that had marked the most of your hulls*; the tide that came in under the Aelvaren, *and turned, and he came not*; the one boat that came farther than any (II.2); Stannard carrying Enno the mile; Kael counting Hesk's steps to two hundred and twelve; Harl bearing the first plank to Tarnard's feet; the fisher-girl to her waist in the sea; Seren reading the Stone out to the children; *no child laughed*. Strike any Jack does not want.
16. **The lines that left the Book** (§1.14). Any can return in one edit.
17. **The foreword at first launch** (§5.2). Recommended: keep it in the codex from the start, as v1.3 and v2.0 did. Its costs: a player can read the dead teller *that held up a wet finger to every wind* as Corlen from Cam 5, four campaigns before he dies; it shows Enrella alive; and it tells that the seal was broken once the wood had spoken. Alternative: hold it until Cam 20, when Seren writes it in the third summer, which spares all three but leaves the reader without the frame's key for twenty campaigns.
18. **I.3 opens from Cam 2** (§5.3), so the play order matches the hearth's. Fallback: v1.3's Cam 1 gate.
19. **The hearth-words, Whispers and personas follow the reader's tab** (*new*): the Book's words under *The Book*, the Plain's under *Plain Words*. Recommended, since K1 makes the tab one choice for the whole Book. The alternative is the Book's words everywhere.
20. **The token against VI.1's fates.** VI.3 has the tenth Throne lying *split open*, and Halyna go in; VI.1 has Thrones sink, crumble or crust over. It is v2.0's premise and a game-wide rule, left as it was. The fix, if wanted, is a fate-clause in the token or a VI.1 allowance that the last one fell where a hand could go in.
21. **Smaller points kept as they stand:**
    - Rhyna says *So were we two sealed, and named Halyna.* once in II.3, as a name received, against the rule that Halyna never name themselves.
    - *Two at the Gate* is the old gate-song of the havens, yet it names *grey sails*, which no haven had seen before the Fall. It is kept verbatim (J5); the hearth can hear it as a song the masons made new in the Fall.
    - *Corlen did what the Captain would have done* names Corlen, so the antecedent is clear.
    - Tarnel's death is told by what it left (*She woke beside him.* · *Tarnel did not wake.*). If Jack wants it said outright: *The cold took him.*
    - *Naelsaen, the mist-hearts* stands in the clear in II.2 before VI.3 tells *Naelear*: a seed, not a spoiler.
    - II.2 and IV.4 both open their headnotes on a child's question.
    - *Tidesmeet, where the river meeteth the tide*: the epithet takes the *-eth*. If Jack rules *-s* forms for epithets, it is one word.
    - Idlan and Denna are named nowhere in the Book. If Jack wants them, their place is one clause in the foreword.
22. **One word in a Whisper.** Jack's GDD line *"The fleet cannot be recalled."* is, in Count VI, *"The fleet cannot be called home,"* / *"for the wood obeyeth the wood."*, to play against V.6's *No voice among us can call us home*. The Whisper follows the Book (§5.9). Restore *recalled* in the Whisper if Jack prefers his words.
23. **v1.3's open calls that are still open:**
    - **Seren's epithet**, *Seren Two-Inks*; fallback *Seren who writes*, to match *Voss who asked*.
    - ***Root-men* or *treelings*** as the Book's word (now *root-men*, with *treelings* the wall's slang). A one-word swap.
    - ***Rhenear*** is respelled to follow *Naelear*; restore the old spelling if Jack meant only the one word.
    - **The theaters:** seven fit; Forest, Mountain, Desert and Frozen need only a skin fix; Sky and Crystal need a new premise under the same mechanic; Volcanic needs a light touch. One rule would settle the rest: every theater's waterway begins at the sea.
    - **The native hands:** the leaf facing I.1 uncut until the Epilogue (recommended); the Stone's telling of the Title's five things (*Home. Kin. The one they kneel to. The open door. The stillness.*); when Seren's line translates (at V.1, recommended); the old hand at Cam 16 as *The Bedrock Hand*; the achievement names, and whether they show as achievements or only as the Knowing bands.
24. **The half-cloak's arithmetic.** Seven or eight hand's breadths do not quite carry a half-cloak from the knees to nothing; the art can make the last strip whatever remains (*It was the last of his half.*). The Book no longer states the rule that defines the strips; §3.8 gives it (the wall's dead laid at this hearth since the banner), and it leaves out the troop dead under the cairns, for the cloak could not hold them.

### 7.3 Declined alternatives

Kept here so that no choice is lost. Each was weighed and not taken.

**In v3:**
- **Register B (MEDIUM)**, inversions and *ere* with no old verbs: cosmetic, and its Plain would be re-punctuation again (one judge's vote).
- **Register C (NATIVE)**, Orrowen words in the English prose: it muddied the scenes and added lore (no votes).
- **A custom of the coast**, in any form, as the burning's reason or as the ship-masters' fear (K8). And v2.0's earlier option, the burning as *a message sent back* into the grey, stays declined with it. The reason is the warden's.
- **Brenn striking the Guest** (K7).
- **The Epilogue's fire beat kept**, with a ship-master's fear-cry for fire and the brand quenched (R6b's option i): it taught the custom by another road.
- **V.5 laid after V.7**, so the counts could not spend Part I: it would break the Contents, the V.5–V.6 spread and the dateline order. The counts were rewritten instead.
- ***ye* in Jack's dust line** (*If ye doubt the tale…*): fixed matter stays outside the dial.
- ***Forgetting chose our fathers.*** for the foreword: in the Book's object-first order it reads backwards. *Forgetting came on our fathers, and none chose it.*
- **The gloss *the joining*** on *ael*: it did the reader's thinking.
- **A richer Book Four Argument**, *What was felled at the edge, and who came to the door: the wrong, told from both sides of the grey.*: the bare line spoils nothing and matches the Contents.
- ***We will not lay it all on the Council.*** restored; ***and it was no runner's fault*** at Enrella's grave; **Idlan and Denna named** in the foreword or at the terms; ***the last Throne*** for Ebba's *the last Mystarch*; **Myststone moved** after the reading; ***made into ornament*** for *reduced to ornament*; ***Look Close*** and ***The Longest Knowing*** as V.7's part titles; **seeding *lest*, *scarce*, *whoso* and *whither*** where they would not fall naturally; and **Seren's verdict line** in II.3 (*Yet she can write it truer than we two can tell it.*), for the tellers' verdict is shown, never said.

**From v2.0, still declined:**
- **The calling-pike and the battery cloths**, read by far-glass, in place of the horn. The Captain's own voice at the battery was simpler and made *one battery at a time* physical.
- **The ten strips of the cloak** for the ten havens. One object that counts the dead without a number was chosen.
- **Idrenna dying in the Joined Tide**, instead of Enrella. A young pair, planted over three years, lands harder.
- **The rolls in the masters' packs**, with two chests of records. One archive chest holds the rolls, the mute stones and the bundle.
- **A Council lord's carved box** as the myrrh's source. The road's dead tie the anointing to Tarnel and Wendhessa.
- **The Rite in the first spring**, waiting for the bloom. The fourteenth day keeps *Commander* from the first battle.
- **Glasspire's great glass as a second cause of the sending.** The last elder's death is cause enough; the glass stays an injury the wood remembers (VI.2).
- **Halyna reading Brenn in the first winter** and holding it back. The seal and Idrenna's leave lock the delay to the Stonwryt and give Idrenna an act.
- **The stone tellings set in measured verse lines.** Jack chose prose.
- **Kael's tells as a rule-rhyme**; **the Count of Winters** end page; **young Ebba** as the fisher-girl's name; **the warden's grandson** at the hearth; **the coal bleeding into the pike's stone**; **a lost staff found at Glasspire**.

## 8 · VERSION HISTORY

*Kept here as a self-contained backup; the project journal is the authoritative record.*

| Version | Date | Changes |
|---|---|---|
| **Book v3.0.0 · Notes v1.1.0** | 2026-10-02 | Jack's notes of 2026-10-02 (K1 to K9) applied, with the memory theme he approved the same day. Research first (R1 Tolkien, R2 Homer and Auerbach, R3 economy and the archaic register, R4 how a story builds, R5 the measured diagnosis, R6a and R6b the element inventory), then a judged calibration of three registers (votes A 3, B 1, C 0) and two binding guides, the voice and the craft. The whole Book rewritten in v1.3's voice and sentence-craft, made hard by heavy old English (Early Modern grammar in every line, a small stock of old words, plain words at the hinge, no sentence over 30 words, no contractions). Every tale built as a story: a question by the hundredth word, the stakes, one halt, a turn, a cost, a closing picture; every death planted and told whole once. The frame made light: datelines of eight words or fewer, headnotes of fifty, the *Hear how* openings and the records after the end gone, the hood back twice, the strip as the growing refrain, two lines *Added in Seren's hand*. The Book at 29,541 words leaf by leaf, against v2.0's 50,976, every element kept. The wood leaves rebuilt as Halyna's fragmentary reading (`<!-- READING -->`) and Seren's story in the wood's *we*. Seren's craft and the tellers' agreement shown, never stated. IV.3: Brenn only watched; the warden, a weak man, burned the boat to hide the deed; there is no custom of burning anywhere, and IV.5, V.1, V.2, VI.3 and the Epilogue are mended. Smaller changes: Halyna cut Halvard's name; the True Men raise the new capstone; the fisher-girl of the east keep is one child, from the north lamp to the boat that came back; Tarnel a boatwright's son; *the Bearer of New Branches*; *The wall named them Mystarchs, and the soldiers the Thrones*. Every song, Jack's lines, the liturgy, the oath, the letter, the Guest's sentence, the Title and every native key unchanged. Plain Words retold from the beats (84.8% of the Book's length; 13.6% of its four-word runs shared with the Book). One switch for the tabs; the Original owed, to be set down from v3; the shared copy is the whole story. Notes: a new §1, *What v3 changed*; the custom removed throughout; the cast, the deaths, the clock, the puzzle pieces, the delivery, the names and the calls brought up to v3; the writers' and editors' notes folded in; sections renumbered (the table at the head of this document) |
| Book v2.0.0 · Notes v1.0.0 | 2026-10-01 | Jack's notes of 2026-09-30 (J1 to J26), his choice of heightened prose made to be read aloud, and his correction of the register, all applied. The whole Book rewritten as epic prose for the voice, in a high register of long joined sentences, every song and fixed line verbatim. Book Three split into III.1 *Of Their Raising* and III.2 *Of Their Fall*; the Homecomings and the Thrones moved to Book Six, now *the Book of the Homecomings* (VI.1, VI.2), and the green wood's tale became VI.3. A dateline under every tale and an Argument under every Book; the siege set at six winters, a Tide each. The Halyna lintel and the plural rule; *we two*; six pair-names. Halyna made discoverers: I.4, II.1, IV.1, V.1 and VI.3 now Seren's; Idrenna, Orvenna, Enrella, Wendhessa, Stannard, Tarnard and the whole hearth given the deeds. Twelve deaths, each planted; the half-cloak and its strips. The horn removed; the runners made the Captain's eyes; Brenn made the outpost's runner. The Wound reshaped: the brutal killing, a reason given for the burning (v2.0's custom, withdrawn in v3.0.0), the ramming of two boats, three survivors, twenty in the ship-masters' mouths. The wood leaves cleared of shore knowledge. The Book of Knowings gains the Last Note. The design moved out of the Book into this NOTES document. *Plain Words* made again from v2; *Original* to be set down anew |

**NOTES v1.0.0 (2026-10-01).** The first NOTES document, made from the editor's notes and the writers' notes of the v2 rewrite, the outline for the poets and its three studies (the Reckoning, the Cast, the Puzzle Pieces), the voice guide, and the design sections of Legends v1.3.0 ("How the Legends Enter the Game", the names table, the settled and open calls, the version history).
